# SillyBunny Lessons

This file lists the bugs that recur in SillyBunny, where they happen, and what guards against them. Read the section for the area you change before you fix a bug or make a non-trivial change.

The source is the fork's own fix commits from April to October 2026, checked against `staging` on 2026-10-22. `#123` is a pull request; a short hash is a commit.

## Most-fixed files

These files received the most fix commits, most first. "(upstream)" marks a file that comes from SillyTavern.

1. `public/script.js` (upstream)
2. `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`
3. `public/index.html` (upstream)
4. `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`
5. `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`
6. `public/scripts/openai.js` (upstream)
7. `public/style.css` (upstream)
8. `public/css/sillybunny-mobile-shell.css`
9. `public/scripts/extensions/in-chat-agents/agent-runner.js`
10. `public/css/mobile-styles.css`
11. `public/scripts/group-chats.js`

## Recurring bugs

The sections go from the most fixes to the fewest. "No test" lists fixes that shipped without a test that guards them.

### Mobile, viewport and WebKit

This is the largest class by a wide margin.

- **Breaks:** viewport or virtual keyboard changes hide the composer or detach the shell.
- **Files:** `public/css/sillybunny-mobile-shell.css`, `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`, `public/style.css`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`.
- **Tests:** `tests/mobile-keyboard-focused-input-scroll.test.js`, `tests/mobile-shell-lifecycle-wiring.test.js`, `tests/expanded-editor-touch.e2e.js`, `tests/openrouter-picker-touch.e2e.js`, `tests/webkit-prefix-companions.test.js` (WebKit prefix parity, #562).
- **Limit:** `tests/mobile-ios26-keyboard-layout.e2e.js` simulates `visualViewport` in Chromium. It does not test real Safari.
- **Limit:** `tests/webkit-prefix-companions.test.js` checks `backdrop-filter` and `user-select` in fork `.css`, inline CSS in JavaScript and HTML, and bundled agent templates. It skips the upstream-origin files in `tests/fixtures/upstream-origin-public-files.json` and does not check camelCase style properties such as `style.backdropFilter`. It uses its own brace scanner because `@adobe/css-tools` 4.4.4 does not parse CSS nesting and drops rules in `public/style.css`.
- **Side-panel sheets:** adopted upstream panel sources can retain mobile `100dvw` sizing; constrain the source and its descendants to the panel. Size sheets from available space, not their intrinsic height: a flex child with percentage sizing can otherwise collapse the source and its nested scroller to zero. Keep same-side docked panels split across the measured chat-shell height. Tests: `tests/side-panel-adapters.e2e.js` (390×844, 360×740, 1440×900, split panels) and `tests/mobile-shell-smoke.e2e.js` (MovingUI containment).
- **Lazy images in a closed `<details>`:** Chromium still fetches an `<img loading="lazy">` inside a closed `<details>`, so a large image in a collapsed expander must get its `src` only when the expander first opens. Found with the former Home "More Assistants" expander (`342c557dd`), which the one-column Home removed; no current code depends on it.
- **Closed menus stayed in the DOM (no longer true for side panels):** a side panel used to keep its own `role="menu"` element while closed, so "is a menu open?" had to test for a visible box, not for a `[role=menu]` match. A plain match made the former Bundled Extras page inside Home (`342c557dd`) ignore Escape forever. The header menu of a side panel is now the shared action menu (`openActionMenu` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/side-panel.js`, a popover on a wide window and a sheet under the overlay breakpoint), which builds its `.sb-action-popover` or `.sb-action-sheet` on open and removes it on close. Tests: `tests/side-panel-engine.e2e.js` (menu assertions at every viewport), `tests/unit/sillybunny-side-panel-activation.test.js`.
- **Side-panel default side:** a panel that changes what the main view shows (Author's Note, CFG, Recent Chats) defaults to the start side; a panel that only inspects it (Token Probabilities, Companions) defaults to the end side. Recent Chats (`sb-chat-sidebar`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`) was the one panel that broke the rule. A stored `Move to Other Side` choice (`movingUIState`, keyed by panel id) still wins. Test: `tests/side-panel-adapters.e2e.js` (the panel table lists `sb-chat-sidebar` with `side: 'left'`).
- **F9 and Escape:** F9 (`handleShortcut` in `side-panel.js`) hides every open panel and remembers them, and the next F9 reopens the same ones in order; with nothing open and nothing remembered it does nothing. It is ignored with a modifier key, while composing text, and while a modal dialog or an `aria-modal` surface is open. Test: `tests/side-panel-engine.e2e.js` (`F9 hides the open panels and brings the same ones back, and leaves an open dialog alone`).
- **First render of a hidden view:** `renderAlternativeTokensView` skips a view that is not shown, so Token Probabilities rendered nothing when it was opened before any chat event fired, and the panel stayed blank until the next one. `registerSidePanel` has an `onOpen` option that runs after the panel is shown and before focus; Token Probabilities passes the render to it (`public/scripts/logprobs.js`, `adapters/side-panels.js`). Test: `tests/side-panel-adapters.e2e.js` (`Token Probabilities shows its notice when it opens, and the notice breaks between words`).
- **Prose in a character-run view:** `#logprobs_generation_output` sets `word-break: break-all` (`public/css/logprobs.css`, upstream-origin) so tokens can break anywhere, and its empty-state notice inherited it ("i / n the User Settings ... fea / ture", F-099). `public/css/sillybunny-side-panel.css` resets the notice to `word-break: normal` and gives it the muted text colour in place of `opacity: 0.5`, which axe flagged. Same test.
- **Side-panel sheets without a composer:** a phone side-panel sheet sits above `#form_sheld`, and Home hides the composer (`#sheld[data-sb-home='on'] > #form_sheld { display: none }`, `public/css/sillybunny-home.css`). A `display: none` composer has an all-zero rect, so a sheet anchored to its top got zero height. `getSheetAnchorTop()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/side-panel.js` uses the bottom of `#sheld` when the composer has no layout box. Tests: `tests/unit/side-panel.test.js`, `tests/home-state.e2e.js` (a sheet opened on Home is taller than 100px and stays inside `#sheld`).
- **Docked side-panel bounds:** side panels also carry `.sb-dialog--window`, so the Window's `max-height: 90dvh` can clip a panel below the chat shell. Remove that cap from docked panels; `tests/side-panel-adapters.e2e.js` checks the panel against `#sheld` at 1440×900. Introduced by `70801186d`.
- **Detached modal content:** moving mobile chat tools into a shared dialog without hiding the source fragment leaves its search field painted in the document. This was introduced by `aa19e952a` and affected the Characters mobile opening path as well as Toggle Panels. Keep detached dialog content hidden and inert until the frame owns it, then hide it again after disposal. Test: `tests/mobile-character-toggle-and-panels.e2e.js`.
- **Mounted list spacing:** legacy tag controls carry a top margin, and the mobile drawer can apply a more specific margin on top. Reset that margin only inside the mounted Characters/Groups filter row; keep its 40px desktop and 44px mobile target. Test: `tests/character-list.e2e.js` (`Groups filter toolbar avoids extra vertical padding`).
- **Mobile editor labels:** a select that should sit below its label needs the shared combo row's `wide: true` mode. Otherwise its intrinsic-width suffix can squeeze labels such as Group Folder into multiple lines. Test: `tests/group-editor-member-lists.e2e.js` (`group folder label stays on one line on mobile`).
- **Popup scroll:** upstream `popup.css` sets `min-height: fit-content` and hides overflow on tall desktop popups, which clips the final rows. The tier frame fixes this; `tests/popup-scroll.e2e.js` checks the last row and visible response. Characterized by `e78207e03`, framed by `5d0d2dd9b`.
- **No test:** #438 (iOS overscroll blanks the page, keyboard moves the composer).
- **Fixes:** #658 (composer stays above the iOS keyboard), #840 (touch scroll in expanded mobile editors), #914 (pickers open during scroll).
- **Labels that may not fit:** a width breakpoint cannot decide whether a text label fits, because the label's width depends on the user's font size (`--fontScale`, the Global Font Size slider) and on the translation. The Home assistant card footers first used fixed container-query widths; at a 20px font or with the French "Start new chat", the ⋮ button sat up to 135px outside the card. `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/footer-fit.js` measures each footer with both labels shown (one read pass, one write pass, in an animation frame so the write cannot loop the `ResizeObserver`) and sets `data-sb-footer-compact` so both footer buttons stack together with their labels (a button shows a label or an icon, never both, so the labels no longer hide); a CSS safety net (`min-width: 0`, ellipsis) keeps a miss inside the card. `scrollWidth` alone missed buttons that spilled into the footer's end padding, so the check compares the buttons' span with the content box. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-footer-fit.test.js`, `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` (20px font, long labels, every card width).
  - **Stacking is not enough at a large text size (the 200% regression of the stacked footer):** the stacked footer shares its card with the menu button, so at 150% and 200% text two cards per line on a phone (174px wide at 390px) cannot hold "Temporary Chat" (187px at 200%), and the labels ended in an ellipsis (`Start Ne…`). The accessibility probe `brokenWords` reports an ellipsized word as "breaks … across lines" because its text range has two client rects, so that message can mean a cut label, not a wrapped one. `footer-fit.js` therefore also measures the card width a stacked footer needs (the widest label's natural width plus the button and footer padding, the menu button and the card borders, all from computed style) and sets it as `--sb-home-card-need` on the grid; `.sbHomeCards` takes it as the minimum track width, in the two-column phone rule too, so the grid drops a column instead of cutting a label. The need comes from the labels alone and never from the card's width, which is what stops the grid and the fit from chasing each other. After a font size falls, the cards take their new widths a frame after the need changes, so a test reads again after a few frames. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-footer-fit.test.js` (card width a stacked footer needs), `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` (150% and 200% text), `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js` (Home page 200% text).
- **Home, one chat for a character with none (F-075 and the two-chats fix):** selecting a character that has no chat file already creates and saves its first chat (`getChat` with `allow_create`), so `openRoleplayChat` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/assistant-chat.js` reads the chat list before selecting and starts a new chat only when one existed. Read it before, because the selection is what makes the file. A recent-chats request that fails is told from an empty list (`onRequestFailed` in `welcome-screen.js`, the `failed` block of `sillybunnyHome.html`). The e2e `tests/home-fresh-character-chat.e2e.js` needs the real server, because `protectMaintainerServer` rewrites `allow_create` and stubbed chat routes never make the file. A card's click target is its overlay: a test clicks its corner, since the centre can land on `Connect a Model` once the footer makes the card taller.
- **Home header and the hero's pixels:** a version line under the title adds a fractional height unless its line is rounded up (`--sb-home-version-line`), and a fractional offset above the hero draws its pixel art in uneven blocks. `tests/art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js` at 390px and scale 3 catches it.
- **Measure the placement you are testing for:** `header-fit.js` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/`) keeps the Home title centred on the column and moves the version below it when the version beside the title would touch the main menu button. Each pass removes `data-sb-version-below` first and measures the beside placement, so the answer depends only on current widths and cannot oscillate; below the title the version has zero inline size, so a text change resizes nothing, and a `MutationObserver` on the version triggers a pass. A test helper that measured the version in its current placement never found the switch point at 390 and 360. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-header-fit.test.js`, `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` (title centred at every width and at 20px text, version below on narrow phones).
- **Popovers and the visual viewport:** a fixed popover is placed in layout-viewport coordinates, but the user sees only the visual viewport, which is smaller while zoomed in or while an on-screen keyboard is open. `getVisibleViewport()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-action-menu.js`) clips `bounds` to the visual viewport's own rectangle: WebKit reports `innerWidth` and `innerHeight` as the visual size while pinch-zoomed, so clipping by them as well collapses a panned viewport to nothing. An open popover places itself again on `visualViewport` `resize` and `scroll`, once per frame, because an iOS pan and Chrome's resizes-visual keyboard fire neither a window resize nor a document scroll. A menu button at the end of a card inside much wider `bounds` passes `alignment: 'end'`; the automatic rule (a trigger in the start half of `bounds` aligns to its start) opened Home's Assistant Menu over the next card. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-action-menu.test.js`, `tests/action-menu-viewport.e2e.js`.
- **Composer menus as sheets share Popper's node:** `presentMenuAsSheet()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/sheet-gestures.js`) presents a menu that upstream shows with `display` and Popper places (`#options`, `#extensionsMenu`, `#sd_dropdown`, the Data Bank Add menu) as a bottom sheet in the mobile layout (`MENU_SHEET_QUERY` in `sillybunny-editor/breakpoints.js`: 768px wide or narrower, or 360px tall or shorter; dialogs keep `OVERLAY_SHEET_QUERY`, 450px), on the same node. The action menu and `picker.js` take the same query. The condition was once 450px, with 360px tall only up to 768px wide because the sheet rules sat in `sillybunny-mobile-shell.css`, which loads at 768px and narrower, so the Options and wand menus stayed popovers at 451 to 768px while the rest of the shell was in its mobile layout; the rules now sit in `sillybunny-theme.css`, which every width loads, because the height rule has no width limit. Popper rewrites inline `position`, `inset`, `margin` and `transform` on every update, so the sheet rules (`@media screen and (max-width: 768px), screen and (max-height: 360px)` in `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`) win with `!important`, the presenter never writes those properties, and a swipe moves the node through `translate` (`bindSheetGestures({ property: 'translate' })`), which composes with Popper's `transform`. A drag from the handle leaves its click on the node, which upstream outside-click handlers (`$(document).on('click')` in `script.js`) take for a click outside the menu, so the presenter stops clicks that land on the node or the handle. The width crossing 768px closes the menu through its upstream path; Popper places the next opening. `#export_format_popup` is not adopted: the character editor already replaces it with an action menu (`_bindExportMenu()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-character-editor.js`). Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-menu-sheet.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-sheet-gestures.test.js`, `tests/composer-menu-sheets.e2e.js`; `tests/window-dialogs.e2e.js` dismisses the Data Bank Add sheet from its scrim.
  - **The sheet does not give the menu a dialog role:** `presentMenuAsSheet()` set `role="dialog"` on the menu node in `15559b5a8`, and `a11y.js` gives the rows of `.options-content` and `.list-group` the role `listitem` under a list role, so the dialog took the list away from 15 rows (`aria-required-parent` on `#extensionsMenu` and the Data Bank Add menu at 390px). The presenter now names the node with `aria-label` only and keeps its role; a node with no role (`#options`, `#sd_dropdown`) gets `role="group"`, which can carry a name. Nothing traps focus, so the dialog role added nothing (`tests/unit/sillybunny-menu-sheet.test.js`, `tests/composer-menu-sheets.e2e.js`). Upstream's `.list-group-item { opacity: 0.5 }` also dimmed the `#sd_dropdown` rows below 4.5:1 in the light tone once the sheet changed the surface behind them; the sheet rule gives its rows `opacity: 1`, as the theme does for the other popover rows.
  - **Menu rows keep upstream's hiding, and the wand menu's own rows are rows too:** the row rule of `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css` had `display: flex !important`, which beat jQuery's `hide()` and `.displayNone`, so "Toggle Panels", "Back to parent chat" (outside a checkpoint chat) and "TTS Playback" (with TTS off, no icon yet) showed in menus that hide them; `display` has no `!important` there now. The row rule also covers the rows that the shell and extensions append straight to `#extensionsMenu` (`:not(.extension_container, .sb-dialog__handle)`), which had upstream's `opacity: 0.5`, 36px height and 5px padding. Rows set `flex: none`: a wand menu that overflows its `max-height` shrank them to their text height at 844x390. The Options menu ends with `#option_delete_mes` after its own `<hr>`, in `--sb-color-danger`. Tests: `tests/composer-menu-sheets.e2e.js`.
  - **A drop-down that a menu row opens opens beside the menu:** `#sd_dropdown` (`stable-diffusion/index.js`) is placed `right-start` of the row that opens it, with `flip` and `preventOverflow` (`altAxis`) so it stays in the window; Popper's old `top` placement put it on the wand menu's rows and, at 844x390, above the window. Its surface is the opaque Popover layer (the extension's `style.css`); on a phone it is a sheet above the wand sheet.
  - **A Font Awesome glyph is the content of its control:** the composer's `div.interactable` icons (`#extensionsMenuButton`, `#send_but`, `#mes_impersonate`, `#mes_continue`, `#mes_stop`, `#sb_prose_polisher_but`, the STscript buttons) show a `::before` glyph that is read as their text, so their `title` never became the name and their name was empty. `a11y.js` gives them `role="button"` and the tooltip as `aria-label`.
- **`rem` does not follow the font slider:** the Global Font Size slider sets `--fontScale`, and `--mainFontSize` and every `--sb-type-*` token scale with it, but the root font size stays 16px, so a width in `rem` does not grow with the text. The art-and-text intro first stacked its line below the art at a `13rem` basis, which left about 18 characters per line beside 128px art at 20px text. `.sbArtIntro > p` uses `flex: 1 1 14em` in the line's own size (`public/css/sillybunny-windows.css`). Test: `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` (Shortcuts at 360×640 stacks at 20px text and sits beside its art at the default size).
- **Touch size follows the pointer, not the width:** the settings page's Back and Close (`.sb-settings-back-button`, `.sb-settings-close-button` in `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`) are 36px squares by default.
  - **Breaks:** with touch emulation, both measured 36×44px at 390×844 and 360×780, below the 44×44px that DESIGN.md (Touch targets) asks for on coarse pointers. At 820×1180 with touch, where the settings page has two columns, Close measured 36×36px.
  - **Cause:** `public/css/mobile-styles.css` loads only up to 768px wide (`media="(max-width: 768px)"` in `public/index.html`) and gives every `button` `min-height: var(--sb-mobile-touch-target, 44px)`. Phones got the height but not the width, and a touch screen wider than 768px got neither.
  - **Guard:** a `(pointer: coarse)` block after each base rule sets the width and height to `var(--sb-mobile-touch-target, 44px)`. It gives `.sb-settings-header-spacer` the same width, because the spacer balances Close and keeps the title centred in the two-column layout.
  - **Tests:** `tests/mobile-shell-smoke.e2e.js` (`settings Back and Close are at least 44px touch targets at 390 and 360 wide`). Before the fix it failed on the width of both buttons at both sizes.
  - **Limits:** the compact-mode rule (`:root[data-sb-compact-mode='true'] .sb-settings-close-button`, 28px) is more specific, so in compact mode Close stays 28px wide on a coarse pointer, and the 44px spacer puts the two-column title 8px right of centre. The test does not turn compact mode on.
- **The top bar's tracks need a content floor, and its verdicts need natural widths:** `#sb-topbar-inner` is `minmax(min-content, 1fr) minmax(0, auto) minmax(min-content, 1fr)` (`public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`), so a group never shrinks below its controls and the title takes only what they leave.
  - **Breaks:** the former tracks were `minmax(0, 1fr) auto minmax(0, 1fr)` with `overflow: visible` groups: the `auto` track took the title's whole width and the groups spilled over it (#975, labelled mode at 769 to 844px and with a long name at 820×360 with touch); icons-only mode scrolled its start group and clipped Customize and Search (F-001).
  - **Guard:** `fitTopbar()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`) decides what the groups hold, in this order as the width runs out: the title shortens by its ellipsis, the labels go (`data-sb-topbar-labels`) as soon as the labelled form leaves less than `min(60px, the title's width)` for the title, the title hides (`data-sb-topbar-title`), then Quick Access moves into the overflow menu (`data-sb-topbar-overflow`, title still hidden). Each step only goes further as the bar narrows, and a labelled bar never shows without its title on desktop. It reads each form's natural widths with that form applied and undone inside one task and never the state on screen, so a verdict cannot feed back into itself. One `ResizeObserver` on the bar and its controls queues it. Phones have no Quick Access, so they never reach the last step.
  - **Pitfall:** a bar control added later has to be a child of a group (it is observed from `buildTopBar`) and must hide with `display: none`, not with zero width, or the fit counts its gap.
  - **Active state:** Backend, Customize, Home and Characters are destinations and only the one on screen is active. `syncHomeButtonState()` reads `sbSettingsState.open`, and `syncSettingsPageActiveButton()` re-syncs Home on every open and close, so a page over Home takes the fill and Home gets it back on close. Search is a toggle with its own on state.
  - **Tests:** `tests/topbar-layout.e2e.js` (every width from 769 to 1100px in both modes with a long title and with every Quick Access slot filled; 820×360 with touch; phones from 320px; one destination at a time), `tests/topbar-label-tap-cycle.test.js` (the order of the steps, and that none goes back as the bar narrows).
- **The settings page on a phone crops itself when focus enters the pane that is off screen (F-303):** `#sb-settings-inner` is `overflow: hidden` and holds the sidebar and the page as two absolute panes, one translated out of it. The pane that is out of sight stays in the tab order, and focusing a control in it scrolls the clipped box sideways (`scrollLeft` 229, then 390), so the sidebar is cropped after Back. It is the same at `1e0c3995b` as at `62c2d60d1`, so Phase 4 did not cause it.
  - **Two sources:** a real Tab from the sidebar search reaches the off-screen Back button on the seventh press; and a test helper that did `if (await back.isVisible()) await back.click()` pressed it too, because `isVisible()` is true for a button that is laid out off screen. The second is what the original screenshot showed.
  - **Guard:** on a phone with the page open, `syncSettingsPaneInert()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`) makes the pane that is not showing `inert`, so Tab, the pointer and a screen reader's virtual cursor stay in the pane that is. It runs wherever `sb-settings-page--content` or the page's `hidden` changes, and when the phone breakpoint is crossed. It has to run before a focus call that moves into the pane that has just come into view: an inert node cannot take focus.
  - **Test helpers:** press Back only while the page is showing (`showSettingsSidebar()` in `tests/helpers/settings-phone-pane.js`, used by the header and status surfaces and the search spec); the class `sb-settings-page--content` is the test, not `isVisible()`.
  - **Editors in the pane:** the leave guards ask `isActive()` whether the editor is the open one. The persona, World Info and agent editors answered by looking for `[hidden]`, `[aria-hidden]` and `[inert]` on an ancestor, so once the page was Back on the sidebar they said no and a dirty editor stopped asking before a tab switch. They now look at `[hidden]` and `[aria-hidden]` only: a pane that a phone keeps inert still holds the open editor. A new "is it on screen" check for an editor must not read `inert`.
  - **`SillyBunnyShell.openTab(shell, tabId)` shows the page on a phone:** for every shell, as it already did for Characters. A named page that opened under the sidebar would be in the inert pane. Tests that open a page and then use the sidebar call `showSettingsSidebar()`.
  - **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-settings-pane-focus.e2e.js` (the Tab cases pass with the native tab order, and fail again if the pane is not inert), `tests/editor-close-guard.e2e.js` (Back to the sidebar over a dirty persona or World Info editor still asks; both fail with the old `isActive()`), `tests/unit/sillybunny-persona-editor.test.js`.

- **The composer field was 172px wide at 390px, and the chat 0px tall at 844x390 (F-090, found in 7.3):** the two action rails took 218px of a 390px row, so a short message wrapped every few words; at 450px and below the field now takes the first row and the rails share the row under it (the grid reads `--sb-composer-columns`, `--sb-composer-rows`, `--sb-composer-areas` and `--sb-composer-row-gap`, which a narrow block sets, so no `!important` is added). Another `#nonQRFormItems` block later in `sillybunny-mobile-shell.css` resets `gap` with the same specificity, so a row gap set in the earlier block is lost. A phone held sideways (844x390) matched no mobile rule, because `data-sb-mobile-ui-mode` followed `(max-width: 768px)` alone: the desktop stack of two bottom-bar rows, the speaker bar and the tray left the chat no height. The attribute now follows `BOTTOM_BAR_COMPACT_QUERY` (`(max-width: 768px), (pointer: coarse) and (max-height: 450px)`), and the registry's `isPaged()` and the steps that open the bottom-bar sheet read the same mode (`isBottomBarChipRow`, from the page).
- **Upstream paints a hovered Quick Reply button `#4d4d4d`, and a tap leaves `:hover` behind:** dark text on it fails contrast on Libadwaita Light. A resting fill on `#send_form #qr--bar .qr--button.menu_button` (two ids) beats upstream's one-id hover rule in every state, so no ungated `:hover` rule is needed (`hover-ungated` counts one).
- **Tests:** `tests/composer-bar-controls.e2e.js`, `tests/mobile-composer-spacing.e2e.js`.

### Scroll, streaming and cancellation

- **Breaks:** rendering or resizing moves the reader's position; cancelling leaves stream state behind.
- **Files:** `public/script.js`, `public/scripts/chat-render-lifecycle/`, `public/scripts/chats.js`, `src/request-cancellation.js`.
- **Guards:** `chat-render-lifecycle/scroll-intent.js` decides when to follow the newest message, `resize-observer.js` handles content that resizes after render, and `mobile-viewport.js` handles mobile viewport changes.
- **Guards:** a page hands its chat replies to the server and stops its stream on `pagehide`, not `beforeunload` (`public/scripts/resumable-generation.js`, `public/script.js`). A leave-site prompt can cancel `beforeunload`, and the page then stays with replies the server still writes into the chat (PR #877 review). Test: `tests/resumable-generation-frontend.test.js`.
- **Guards:** a freshly opened, greeting-only Reisen or Tewi chat stays at its top so the greeting picture is in view. The hold sits in the one bottom-scroll sink, `scrollChatElementToBottom`, through `shouldHoldChatAtTop()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/greeting-art-opening.js`), and is not skipped for `force`, because the pins of a chat load are themselves forced. The resize compensation in `RossAscends-mods.js` writes `scrollTop` outside that sink, so it is guarded too: without it, the Temporary Chat banner moves the chat 46 px and ends the hold. A first version shadowed `#chat.scrollTop` with an own accessor; review found it swallowed the Go to bottom button and could snap back a scrollbar drag, so the sink guard replaced it. The hold must outlast the load lock (`script.js` 2400 + 250 ms); a unit test reads both. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-greeting-art-opening.test.js`, `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js`.
- **Greeting art fits above a touch composer:** on a coarse pointer, a chat that holds only the greeting scales the picture down to the room that its text leaves (`layout()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/greeting-art.js`), so the whole greeting shows without a scroll or a hold. It draws the picture at its normal size, reads the overflow of `#chat` once, and gives that up, not below `GREETING_ART_MIN_FIT` of the normal height; a greeting that cannot fit even then (a 360x780 phone, a landscape phone) keeps the normal size. The fit steps down by whole device pixels per art pixel (`largestStepThatFits` in `pixel-art.js`) and shrinks smooth only when one device pixel per art pixel is still too wide, which is the case at 390x844 on a 3x screen because the art is 768 art pixels wide. The tray re-fits it, because the tray changes the composer height. The soft keyboard must not: `keyboardIsOpen()` (a composer has focus and the visible viewport is over 80px shorter than the last one seen without focus) keeps the size while it is up, because each platform shrinks `#chat` for the keyboard by a different means. Tests: `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js` ("is fitted again when the tray opens, and keeps its size while the keyboard is up" simulates the keyboard as a shorter Chromium viewport; it cannot reach real iOS `visualViewport` or an insets-only keyboard, and `tests/mobile-ios26-keyboard-layout.e2e.js` is a fixture of extracted shell functions that does not load the greeting art). `followsTheScale` accepts the fitted size on a touch screen only when nothing overflows.
- **Guards:** Stop cancels the run, so `Generate()` never reaches its finish path for the stopped reply. `stopGeneration()` calls `StreamingProcessor.finalizeStoppedMessage()`, which ends the reasoning with all reasoning that arrived, records `extra.reasoning_duration`, removes `extra.server_generation_placeholder` and saves the chat. Without it, a reply stopped during reasoning kept "Thinking..." and a null duration, and no save followed Stop, so a chat reload right after Stop dropped the reply (10f2b91e4 set `isStopped` in `onStopStreaming()`; 0167ddc2a made Stop a cancellation that clears the streaming processor). Tests: `tests/story-mode-request-controls.test.js`, `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js`.
- **Guards:** a Gemini reply that stops early can look finished or blank, because thinking counts against the output limit and a single request has a token cap. `CONTINUATION` and `MAX_TOKENS` from generateContent and `requires_continuation` and `incomplete` from Interactions reach the client as a length finish, and the direct Gemini sources then show a warning (`warnsOnGenerationLengthFinish()` in `public/scripts/generation-request-controls.js`, `src/endpoints/backends/chat-completions.js`). `StreamingProcessor.providerLengthFinish` is separate from `finishReason`, which records the provider's finish only with a prose cap and also covers the local cap. A chunk with a `continuationToken` and no `finishReason` is a checkpoint, not a finish. Tests: `tests/story-mode-request-controls.test.js`, `tests/google-model-request.test.js`, `tests/gemini-interactions.test.js`.
- **Guards:** `src/gemini-long-decode.js` continues a Gemini reply across requests while Google answers `CONTINUATION` or `requires_continuation`, so those finishes reach the client only when long decode could not go on. Every request repeats the same body with the newest token. Google counts `maxOutputTokens` across requests and answers 400 once a request's limit is spent, so later requests send the full limit. The stream removes the `CONTINUATION` finish and every token before forwarding, because `public/scripts/openai.js` keeps a length finish once it sees one. A `CONTINUATION` finish continues from the newest token whatever went out after that token, as Google's js-genai SDK does. A stream that stops with no finish resumes from its token only when nothing visible went out after that token, because the next request repeats that content and the client cannot take it back; otherwise the stream ends with a cut-off finish. A token the request already has marks nothing new, so content beside it counts as content after the token. A new token covers the text on its own event: live streams carried one on every event from about 32K tokens on, and resuming from such a token on 2026-11-05 repeated nothing. A request that stops with no new token and no content goes out again up to 3 times. An HTTP error status, a blocked prompt, an error event with no new token and a status that asks for the next request with the same token all end the reply instead. A non-streaming request's text joins parts with a separator and joins requests with none, because a request boundary can fall inside a word. The first non-streaming Gemini 4 request above 32,768 tokens goes out capped, and only that request continues past `MAX_TOKENS`. Vertex uses `v1beta1` for the same Gemini 4 limits and `v1` for everything else. Each later request calls `extendLifetime()` on the resumable generation, because one reply can outlast the sweep's lifetime limit while no single request can. `readRawEvents()` keeps an event in pieces and scans only the new text, because a regex flattens a concatenated string on every read and that cost grows with the square of an inline image's size. Tests: `tests/gemini-long-decode.test.js`, `tests/google-model-request.test.js`, `tests/gemini-interactions.test.js`, `tests/resumable-generations.test.js`.
- **Guards:** `getGemini3ThinkingLevel()` in `src/prompt-converters.js` sends `high` for `xhigh` and `max` on Gemini 4, because its documentation names no higher level. Gemini 3 models still receive the picked rung. Test: `tests/prompt-converters.test.js`.
- **Tests:** `tests/chat-render-lifecycle-script-wiring.test.js`, `tests/chat-scroll-regressions.e2e.js`, `tests/request-cancellation.test.js`, `tests/in-chat-agents-companion-panel.test.js`, `tests/sse-stream.test.js`, `tests/frontend/SseStream.e2e.js`.
- **Fixes:** #271 (stabilise chat scrolling), #314 (cancel when a stream disconnects), #771 (resize and load lock work against each other), #783 (streaming moves the screen).

### In-chat agents and companions

- **Breaks:** cancelled or stale agent turns run again or change the active response.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/extensions/in-chat-agents/agent-runner.js`, `agent-store.js`, `companion/companion-runner.js`, `companion/companion-panel.js`.
- **Tests:** `tests/in-chat-agents-runner.test.js`, `tests/guided-generations-profiles.test.js`, `tests/guided-generations-steering.test.js`.
- **No test:** `59aeea3f1` (#385, abort agent generation on cancel), `20353df60` (hotfix, swipes rerun agents).
- **Fixes:** #839 (Guided Generations cancel, swipe cleanup and profile restore).
- **Companion panel is a side panel:** `companion/companion-panel.js` registers `#ica--tracker-panel` through `registerSidePanel` (id `ica-companion-panel`, title "Companions"), so the engine owns its dock, its sheet form, its header (title, menu, Close) and its Escape handling. The former fixed slide-out lay over the chat and the top bar at 1440×900 and over the composer at 390×844, with no handle (F-196). Lock and Regenerate All stay in a toolbar inside the body (`.ica--tpanel-header`). The panel follows `Move to Other Side` (stored under `ica-companion-panel`), not the handle edge; the handle hides while the panel is open. Focus returns to the opener only on Close or Escape (`closeCompanionPanel({ restoreFocus })`), not on an outside click. Tests: `tests/in-chat-agents-companion-panel.e2e.js` (`docks beside the chat on a wide window and is a sheet above the composer on a phone`), `tests/in-chat-agents-companion-panel.test.js`, `tests/ui-motion-css-compliance.test.js` (no `clip-path` in the companion stylesheet).
- **Order matters:** Guided Generations calls `integrateQrBar()` before it returns for a hidden bar (`public/scripts/extensions/guided-generations/index.js`). If the order changes, Quick Replies show in the composer after a reload (`8ce1a10a5`). Test: `tests/guided-generations-settings.test.js`.
- **Dry runs:** the `GENERATION_STARTED` listener in `public/scripts/extensions/in-chat-agents/index.js` returns for a dry run, as the listeners in `agent-runner.js` and `public/scripts/sillybunny-conversation/init.js` do. A dry run does not change the generation state. Without the check, `refreshGenerationUi()` → `activateSendButtons()` → `showSwipeButtons()` → `refreshSwipeButtons()` added `.fade` to every rendered message. Prompt Manager starts dry runs from its own debounced timers after `CHAT_LOADED` (`saveServiceSettings()`, then `renderDebounced()`, in `public/scripts/PromptManager.js`), so one can land right after `redisplayChat({ fade: false })`. Holding `/api/settings/save` until after the redisplay reproduces the nightly failure in issue #920: 12 `.fade` classes in `redisplay keeps render follow-up hooks applied after batched render`. Tests: `tests/chat-scroll-regressions.e2e.js` (`a dry-run generation keeps redisplay follow-up hooks applied`), `tests/in-chat-agents-generation-ui-wiring.test.js`.

### Bundled defaults, templates and user state

- **Breaks:** a refresh, reset or update overwrites user themes or templates, or brings back deleted defaults.
- **Files:** `default/`, `public/scripts/preset-manager.js`, `public/scripts/extensions/in-chat-agents/agent-store.js`, theme endpoints and theme CSS.
- **Guards:**
  - `src/endpoints/content-manager.js` does not overwrite a seeded file that already exists.
  - "Restore default presets" (`restoreDefaultPresetFiles`, `src/endpoints/content-manager.js`) used `cpSync(..., { force: true })` over every bundled preset, so it reset bundled presets the user had edited while its confirm text promised only that deleted ones come back. It now copies with `COPYFILE_EXCL` and skips a file that exists, edited or not, including one that appears during the restore (`tests/default-preset-deletions.test.js`). Bundled Extras restores one preset through `/api/presets/restore` and `/api/presets/save` instead (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/bundled-extras-presets.js`).
  - `reconcileManagedBundledQuickReplies()` removes a bundled Quick Reply only when its hash matches a known stale version. It does not touch user-edited files.
  - Bundled agent template updates match by template `id` (`scripts/bump-agent-template-versions.js`). An update keeps the agent's enabled state, quick-toggle pin, order and profile overrides.
  - `✅ Run Unit Tests on PR` fails when an existing bundled agent template or `index.json` entry changes without a version bump. It runs `scripts/bump-agent-template-versions.js --check` against the PR's merge ref. After a commit, bump with `npm run agents:bump-template-versions -- --base origin/staging`; without `--base` the script compares only uncommitted changes against `HEAD`.
- **Tests:** `tests/preset-save-deleted-default.test.js`, `tests/create-only-installs.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-theme-css.test.js`, `tests/theme-custom-css-import.test.js`, `tests/in-chat-agents-templates.test.js`, `tests/bump-agent-template-versions.test.js`, `tests/user-data-migrations.test.js`, `tests/startup-data-migrations.test.js`, `tests/config-key-migration.test.js`, `tests/bundled-default-validators.test.js`.
- **No test:** `a354f263d` (#753, redundant bundled-agent updates).
- **Fixes:** #687 (saving a preset over a deleted bundled default's name), #834 (an external theme pack wipes saved themes), #919 (theme data lost on update, export or reset; Custom CSS overwritten).
- **A bundled theme revision reaches users three ways:** new installs seed `default/content/themes/*.json` through `default/content/index.json` and take their applied palette from `default/content/settings.json`, which must match the default theme's file (`tests/libadwaita-theme-migration.test.js`). Existing installs keep their own copy, which `reconcileManagedBundledThemes()` (`src/endpoints/content-manager.js`) replaces only when its SHA-256 equals a previous bundled file (`MANAGED_BUNDLED_THEMES`, `CONTRAST_REVISED_THEME_HASHES`), so edited and deleted copies stay as they are. The colours a user has applied live in their own `settings.json`, which only a startup repair changes: `planThemeTextRepair()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-theme-revisions.js`), run once from `public/scripts/power-user.js` behind `power_user.sb_theme_text_revision`, acts only when the saved theme name and all ten colour keys equal a previous bundled version, and replaces the CSS only when it is the previous bundled CSS. Without that repair a user keeps the old colours, and switching themes later shows the prompt for CSS no theme stores. Tests: `tests/bundled-theme-reconcile.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-theme-revisions.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-theme-text-repair.e2e.js`, `tests/bundled-theme-text-contrast.test.js` (every bundled theme's text roles reach 4.5:1).
- **Bundled extensions open their own first-run dialogs:** Quick Image Gen opens its setup wizard whenever its saved settings are missing or empty (`public/scripts/extensions/quick-image-gen/index.js`), which happens on a new install, after a settings reset and after an import without its settings. On a first run the wizard sat under the modal onboarding dialog, where its Skip button could not be pressed, and `waitForAppReady()` (`tests/frontend/frontent-test-utils.js`) needed a locator handler to close it. `seedQuickImageGenFirstRun()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-first-run-defaults.js`), called once before `loadExtensionSettings()` in `public/script.js`, seeds `{ setupWizardSeen: true, starterPresetsSeeded: false }`: QIG treats any non-empty saved object as an existing install, so `starterPresetsSeeded` must be an explicit `false` or the starter presets are skipped too. The vendored files are unchanged. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-first-run-defaults.test.js`, `tests/quick-image-gen-first-run.e2e.js` (with the seam disabled, the no-wizard and reset tests fail; without the explicit `false`, the starter-presets test fails).
- **What the app rewrites when a card is saved:** the character editor posts `FormData`, and the browser sends its multi-line text with CRLF line breaks; `charaFormatData` (`src/endpoints/characters.js`) writes an `extensions.depth_prompt` with an empty prompt into every card it saves, and the editor embeds the linked world as `data.character_book` under the world's name. A fingerprint of a card's text must normalise all three, or saving a bundled card without a change makes it look edited. `src/sillybunny-managed-content.js` normalises line breaks, treats an empty depth prompt and a book named after a manifest world as none, and pins literal hashes so the algorithm cannot drift without a failing test (`tests/managed-content-app-roundtrip.test.js` posts real CRLF multipart through `/edit`).
- **Managed bundled content never overwrites what it does not recognise:** reconcile replaces a card, sprite file or world only when its fingerprint is a known earlier release; anything else (a user edit, a malformed or unsafe file) is `modified` and left alone, and only an explicit apply or restore writes it, after a backup that aborts the write when it fails. A world's root `extensions` count, because the app copies them into embedded books. Renaming a managed card moves its file, so it shows as `deleted` and Restore adds a second card (`tests/managed-content-app-roundtrip.test.js`).
- **A bundled card or world that changes before release still needs its old fingerprints in `previousFingerprints`:** testers' servers hold the version they were seeded with, and reconcile replaces a file only when its fingerprint is a listed earlier one, so a manifest rebuilt at version 1 with no history left a tester's older Reisen, Tewi or SillyBunny Lexicon `modified`, and Home showed Reisen framed to the waist beside the current Tewi. Rebuilding the manifest at version 1 with no history was the earlier practice for content that had not shipped. The two assistant cards list their two pre-release versions and the Lexicon its seven pre-release ones (2 to 7 November), newest first, and `tests/unit/sillybunny-shipped-managed-content.test.js` pins them as literals at the end of the list ("stay at the end of the earlier fingerprints, newest first, so an unedited copy of a pre-release card is updated at start", and the Lexicon's twin), because a later rebuild adds fingerprints in front of them. `buildManifest` keeps hand-added history while the file is unchanged, so adding it needs no rebuild and no version bump; status ignores the version. A card that was only used (favourite, tags, a new chat, an editor save without a change) keeps its image bytes and its fingerprint, and a card whose text was edited stays `modified` (`tests/managed-content-app-roundtrip.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-managed-content.test.js` "brings an untouched card to the bundled version and keeps what the user set on it").
- **Every writer of an image must drop its cached thumbnails, because the thumbnail route never re-checks a cached file:** `GET /thumbnail` (`src/endpoints/thumbnails.js`) serves a cached thumbnail whenever one exists; the "original is newer than the thumbnail" test lives only in `generateThumbnail`, which the route reaches when no cached file exists. Upstream's model is that whoever replaces an image calls `invalidateThumbnail(directories, type, file)`, which removes the desktop and the mobile copy, and `characters.js` does. Managed content wrote cards without it, so after the start-time update, **Update** or **Restore** a tester's Home still showed the old Reisen from the 28,750 B mobile thumbnail (the browser's 304 came from the server's own ETag, so clearing the browser cache changed nothing). `createManagedContent` now takes an `invalidateThumbnail(filename)` hook, calls it once after each card file it writes (never for a world or sprite file, never when nothing was written), and logs a hook that throws without failing the update; the router (`src/endpoints/sillybunny-managed-content.js`) and `seedContentForUser` (`src/endpoints/content-manager.js`) pass it, because that module cannot import `thumbnails.js` (it reads the configuration when it loads). The start also drops the thumbnails of a managed card whose cached copy is older than the card file (`mtimeMs` of the card over `ctimeMs` of the thumbnail, the comparison `generateThumbnail` makes), which repairs the cards that earlier builds replaced. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-managed-content.test.js` ("the cached thumbnails of a managed card"), `tests/unit/sillybunny-managed-content-router.test.js` ("the cached thumbnails of a card"), `tests/managed-content-seeding.test.js`, and `tests/managed-content-app-roundtrip.test.js` ("the thumbnail of a card that is replaced", which requests the real mobile thumbnail before and after Update, the start and the self-heal). A test file that mocks `src/util.js` and imports `content-manager.js` now also mocks `src/endpoints/thumbnails.js`.
- **The default assistant is Reisen, with or without her card:** `getDefaultAssistantConfig()` in `public/scripts/welcome-screen.js` returns the registry's Reisen entry. The default used to be Reisen once her card existed and the Bunny Guide until then, which needed a resolver that remembered the answer per `characters` array and a cache that five character events cleared. The Guide is no longer shipped, so the resolver, the cache and the fingerprint check are gone. `ensureBundledAssistantCharacter` became the lookup `findBundledAssistantCharacter`: the server seeds Reisen and Tewi, and the browser never creates a card. A user who still has the Guide card keeps it as an ordinary character, and a pointer at it stays valid until Reisen's card exists, when the pointer migration moves it. `BUNDLED_CARD_AVATARS` keeps the Guide's file name as a literal, so that kept card does not count as a card of the user's own in Getting Started. Do not add Pura's preset files or the Guide card to a deletion list: existing users keep them. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistant-registry.test.js`, `tests/bundled-assistants.e2e.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-getting-started.test.js`; review `ses_e6bdd0f01ffeY4maAD47mMHxoC`.
- **A finished Getting Started stays on Home until the user hides it:** the state used to set `hidden: true, hiddenBy: 'auto'` itself when the last step was resolved, so the tile vanished at the moment of finishing, and `planCompletionToast` returned null because no step was left open: no feedback at all. Now resolving the last step does not touch `hidden`. `isFinished()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/getting-started.js`) drives the finished tile (Success Green check, the finished line and `4 of 4`; `tiles-view.js`), the What's Next row (`sillybunny-welcome.js`), the final toast and the Hide Getting Started button in the window, which calls `hideFinished()` (`hidden: true, hiddenBy: 'auto'`, one write; it does nothing while a step is open or when the tile is hidden already). The stored format and `HIDDEN_REASONS` are unchanged, and `'auto'` is still the only reason that a step open again ends: `followResolvedCount()` runs on each change and once when the state is built, so a step that a later version adds brings the tile back at the next start. A stored `hidden: true, hiddenBy: 'auto'` from the earlier auto-hide stays hidden and shows the finished tile after Show Getting Started; a finished value that is shown starts with the finished tile. The final toast (`{ kind: 'finished' }` from `planCompletionToast`) follows the other toasts: only a signal announces it (Skip in the window does not, because the window shows the state), never while hidden, and Show Me opens the window. The tile that opened the window is gone after Hide, so the button's handler focuses the first tile of the row (`focusFirstHomeTile`, `windows.js`); `focusCurrentOpener` used to move focus to the main menu button after every window whose opener had gone, so it now leaves focus that is already inside Home alone. Changing `sillybunny-home.css` and `sillybunny-windows.css` moved their `?v=` keys (`style.css` at both `index.html` sites, `sillybunny-window-styles.js`). Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-getting-started.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-getting-started.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-tiles.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-windows.test.js`, `tests/getting-started.e2e.js` ("once every step is resolved"), `tests/home-layout.e2e.js`, `tests/home-windows.e2e.js`.
- **Image paths that chats store are part of the data format:** `system_avatar` is saved as `force_avatar` of `/sys` narrator messages (`public/scripts/slash-commands.js`), tool-call messages (`public/scripts/tool-calling.js`) and system messages (`public/scripts/system-messages.js`), and `updateMessageElement` in `public/script.js` uses a saved `force_avatar` as the avatar's `src` as it is. Releases saved `img/sillybunny-pixel-logo.png` (v1.0.0 to v1.5.2), and from v1.5.3 `img/sillybunny-pixel-logo-og.png` or `img/sillybunny-badge.png`, whichever the Frontend Icon choice picked. Those three files stay in `public/img/` and are not on any deletion list, and `style.css` still gives the pixel logo `object-fit: contain`. `img/sillybunny-badge.svg` was a value only from `1179a9f37` to `a3493ff0e`, which no release tag contains, so it was removed with the rest of the Bunny Guide art. Before deleting an image, search the history (`git log -S`) for it next to `system_avatar`, `force_avatar`, `char_thumbnail` and `avatar`. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-saved-chat-images.test.js`.
- **Retired keys are removed at every start, not once:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-retired-keys.js` deletes `WelcomePage_DeckView`, `WelcomePage_TutorialStatus`, `WelcomePage_TutorialIndex`, `bundledAssistantGuideAvatar` and `bundledAssistantGuideDeleted` from the account and the browser, and `sb-frontend-icon` from the browser, on `SETTINGS_LOADED` and `APP_READY`. `accountStorage.removeItem()` saves settings only for a key that exists, so a repeated run costs nothing, and there is no "first run" flag to lose in an import. The listener must run after `accountStorage.init()`; before it, `removeItem` only warns. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-retired-keys.test.js`, `tests/bundled-assistants.e2e.js`.
- **An unnamed account's persona is `'[Unnamed Persona]'`, not `'User'`:** `addMissingPersonas` (`public/scripts/personas.js`, called from `getUserAvatars`) binds every avatar without a persona to the name `'[Unnamed Persona]'` with empty fields at boot, while `name1` stays `'User'`. A check on the name `'User'` alone never matched a real account, so Welcome's reopen form missed the untouched default; `isUntouchedDefaultPersona` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-welcome-persona.js`) accepts `'User'`, `'[Unnamed Persona]'` or no bound name, with an empty description, title and lorebook, no connections or appendices, and not the account's default persona. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-welcome.test.js`, `tests/welcome-startup.e2e.js`.
- **Lexicon keys are whole phrases, and the budget holds about six entries:** World Info matches a key as a whole phrase (`world_info_match_whole_words` in `default/content/settings.json`), so `A71`'s keys "replies cut off" and "reply cut off" never matched "My replies keep getting cut off halfway"; without the entry both assistants named the wrong Backend tab and, in Conversation, a Continue button that mode lacks. Give an entry the short phrase a user types ("cut off") as a key of its own. On a new install's Chat Completion defaults (context 4095, 300 for the reply) the 25% budget holds about six entries, and an entry pulled in by another entry's text takes a slot from one the question needs: `A2`'s "upstream SillyTavern" brought `A76` into every Roleplay prompt, so `A76` has `excludeRecursion`. Test: `tests/world-info-scan-target.test.js` "the SillyBunny Lexicon under the shipped World Info settings" runs questions a new user asks through `checkWorldInfo` with the shipped settings and checks that each is keyed on its entry and keeps it within the budget.
- **The entries about the assistants are keyed on a bare name, so they need a character filter and both recursion flags:** `A78` (Reisen) and `A79` (Tewi) teach each assistant about the other, so each is keyed on her name, which the common-word guard otherwise bans because her name is in every prompt of her own chats. Each entry leaves her own card out (`characterFilter` `{ isExclude: true, names: [<card file without .png>] }`, which `world-info.js` applies to a Conversation scan target too), and `tests/unit/sillybunny-lexicon-coverage.test.js` allows the bare name only on an entry with that filter. Both entries also set `excludeRecursion` and `preventRecursion`: on a new install recursion runs from the constant `A0` through `A40` and `A77`, which name both assistants, so without `excludeRecursion` `A79` joined every Reisen chat, and without `preventRecursion` their own text ("character list", "Welcome Page Assistant") put `A37` and `A77` into the budget ahead of entries a question needs. Tests: `tests/world-info-scan-target.test.js` "gives each assistant the entry about the other one when she is named…" and "the entries about the assistants" (both failed with the flags off). Rebuild the managed manifest once per change, from the committed manifest; a second rebuild records the fingerprint of a version that never shipped.

### Group chats

- **Breaks:** turns are routed before the user message or its metadata is saved; stale IDs remain.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/group-chats.js`, `public/scripts/group-turn-routing.js`, `src/endpoints/groups.js`.
- **Tests:** `tests/group-turn-routing.test.js`, `tests/group-member-rename.test.js`, `tests/generation-lifecycle-wiring.test.js`.
- **No test:** `1527a1d29` (v1.2.8 hotfix: stale group IDs cause a missing `.jsonl` file).
- **Fixes:** #821 (the "typing" indicator stays on), `2e2d0fc94` (member renames propagate), `3e8c42b6c` (route turns only after the user message is saved).

- **The speaker bar hid its own chips while a member typed (F-088):** `mobile-styles.css` set `visibility: hidden` on `.group_speaker_list` under `.is-typing` and drew the typing line over the same grid cell, so a held generation left the member chips unreachable on a phone. The typing line now has its own grid row (`grid-template-areas: "typing typing typing typing" "avatars greeting speak hide"`), so the chips stay in view. On a desktop window the chips were cut off in a fixed 328px list (F-087): the list wraps, and the bar is a size container whose queries drop the button labels at 900px and the chip names at 520px, by its own width and not the window's. The chips are `<button>`s, so the chosen one also carries `aria-pressed`, set in the three places that set `.selected` (`tests/util/group-chats-sandbox.js` stubs it).
- **Tests:** `tests/composer-bar-controls.e2e.js` (chips unclipped at 1440, label steps, typing row above the chips at 390), `tests/group-chat-greetings-qol.test.js`, `tests/group-speaker-selection-api.test.js`.

### Chat writes, reloads and backups

- **Breaks:** an old queued write replaces a newer chat; backups push each other out of the backup ring.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/chat-save-guard.js`, `public/scripts/chat-reload-guard.js`, `src/endpoints/chats.js`, `src/generation-commit.js`, `public/scripts/server-generations.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - The server writes a reply over an existing message only when that message carries `extra.server_generation_placeholder` with the generation's start time. The browser removes the mark when the reply is finished or stopped, so a reply the page saved itself, and later edits to it, are not overwritten (`src/generation-commit.js`).
  - `Generate()` awaits `settleGenerationCommit()` before it saves a finished reply. If the server answers `committed`, the page reloads the chat and does not save its own copy, which would fail the integrity check. Stream readers return at `[DONE]`, which can come before the body ends and claims on its own (PR #877 review).
  - A tab keeps its server event stream (`public/scripts/server-generations.js`) only while it is visible or reads a reply of its own. Over HTTP/1.1 a browser opens about 6 connections per server for all its tabs; with a stream in every tab, a seventh tab never loads (PR #877 review). Tests: `tests/server-generations-frontend.test.js`, `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js`.
  - A tab that reloaded once for a server-written reply and still finds no mark for it acknowledges it. Otherwise each reload reconnects, hears of the reply again and reloads again until the server forgets it.
  - `reloadCurrentChat()` removes an open message editor but leaves `this_edit_mes_id` set. A reload for a server-written reply reopens the edit with its unsaved text through `messageEdit()`, unless the reply changed that message, and clears the id with `setEditedMessageId()` (`restoreOpenEdit()` in `public/scripts/server-generations.js`). Waiting with the reload does not help: once the server has written the chat, saving the edit fails the integrity check. Tests: `tests/server-generations-frontend.test.js`, `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js`.
  - A temporary chat writes nothing because one flag (`public/scripts/sillybunny-temporary-chat-state.js`) is checked at each seam that reaches the server or the card: `saveChatImmediately()`, `saveChatDebounced()`, `updateRemoteChatName()`, `statMesProcess()` (`public/scripts/stats.js`), `getOpenChatTarget()` (`public/scripts/server-generations.js`), `clearChat()`'s itemized-prompt save, the settings payload's `active_character`, and the chat collection in `public/scripts/extensions/vectors/index.js`. The flag check must not compare the selected card: when a route changed the card without leaving, the next save would write the temporary messages into the other card's real chat. `reloadCurrentChatUnsafe()` ends the temporary chat instead when the card no longer matches. Tests: `tests/temporary-chat.e2e.js` (no write outside an allowlist), `tests/temporary-chat-seams.test.js`, `tests/temporary-chat-navigation.test.js`.
  - Every route out of a chat passes `flushPendingChatSavesForNavigation()` before `setCharacterId()`. `/go` and `/char` (`openChat()` in `public/scripts/slash-commands.js`) and `/random` (`doRandomChat()` in `public/scripts/power-user.js`) set the new card first, so a review probe saw a pending save of the old chat sent with the new card's file name and avatar, and a temporary chat stayed on under another card. Both now flush first and stop on false (`tests/temporary-chat-navigation.test.js`).
  - `doNewChat()` with "delete current chat" deletes the chat named in the card, which after a discarded temporary chat is the character's real chat. It skips the delete when the open chat was temporary (`tests/temporary-chat-seams.test.js`, red before the fix).
  - "Nothing changed since the user chose Discard" must compare the messages, not `chat.length`: an edit, a swipe or a continue keeps the length, so the next leave dropped an edited temporary chat without asking. `leaveTemporaryChat()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-temporary-chat.js`) compares each message's `mes` and `swipe_id`.
- **Tests:** `tests/chat-cloning-lifecycle.test.js`, `tests/chat-save-guard.test.js`, `tests/chat-save-queue-runtime.test.js`, `tests/chat-integrity-rotation.test.js`, `tests/reload-chat-safeguard.test.js`, `tests/prove-368-differential.test.js`, `tests/server-generation-commit.test.js`, `tests/resumable-generation-frontend.test.js`, `tests/story-mode-request-controls.test.js`.
- **Fixes:** #819 (queued saves across swipes clone chats), #849 (staged reloads wipe chats), #373 (extra pre-write backups push older backups out), #856 (issue #706: a chat saves when opened).

### Chat, group, file, vector and asset route input

- **Breaks:** a request field, a stored group chat ID, a line in a chat file or a failed download makes a route use a path outside its folder, or makes the route throw outside its error handling.
- **Cause:** a rejection from an async route reaches `handleRouteError()` in `src/route-errors.js`, which answers 500 without saying what was wrong, and a throw in a stream or readline event crashes the server, which the supervisor then restarts. So that code must answer its own errors. A JSON body or an extended query string can send an object whose `toString` is not a function, and loose equality, `String()`, template literals and `toString()` throw for it. `pipe()` leaves an error from its source stream unhandled. Paths were checked with a string prefix or not at all, and `sanitize()` (sanitize-filename 1.6.3) turns `.`, `..` and Windows device names into an empty name, which makes the joined path name the folder itself.
- **Files:** `src/endpoints/files.js`, `src/endpoints/chats.js`, `src/endpoints/groups.js`, `src/endpoints/vectors.js`, `src/endpoints/assets.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - `/api/files/delete` and `/verify` accept a path only inside the files folder (`isPathUnderParent()`). A string prefix also accepts sibling folders whose names start with the files folder name.
  - `getGroupChatPath()` in `src/endpoints/chats.js` joins a stored group chat ID only when it is a string or a number and its path stays inside the group chats folder. Chat search and recent chats skip any other ID, and group create and edit answer 400 for a chat list that holds one. A slash inside the folder, as in a checkpoint name, and numeric IDs from older groups still pass.
  - Chat export runs inside one `try` block and answers 400 for a `file` that is not a string or that sanitizes to an empty name. It waits for its read stream through a promise with `error` listeners on the stream and on readline, because readline re-emits input stream errors and an `error` event without a listener throws. It skips a line that is not a chat message, such as an empty line in an older chat file.
  - `getChatInfo()`, which chat search, recent chats and group chat info read through, counts and matches `mes` only when it is a string, because nothing catches a throw in its readline event.
  - Group delete answers 400 for an `id` that is not a string or a number.
  - The vector routes return `regenerateCorruptedIndexErrorHandler()` from their catch blocks, so it catches errors from its own regeneration and answers 500.
  - `getIndex()` refuses a vector source or collection ID that sanitizes to an empty name, and the routes answer 400. Vector purge answers 400 for such a collection ID, so each purge path is one collection folder inside its source folder.
  - `validateAssetFileName()` in `src/endpoints/assets.js` refuses a file name that is not a string, so asset delete, asset download and Data Bank upload answer 400 for one. The asset routes compare the category with `===`, and the character asset list answers 400 for a `name` that is not a string.
  - Asset download writes the body through `pipeline()`, so an error in the download body answers 500. With `pipe()`, the request never got an answer under Node.js, and Bun raised the error as uncaught. Its catch block answers only when nothing was sent, because the character branch answers before it removes the temp file.
- **Tests:** `tests/route-guards-scenarios.test.js`, `tests/route-guards-rows.test.js` (rows on the hostile file name harness, `tests/util/hostile-file-names-*.js`). `tests/hostile-file-names-storage.test.js` requires 400 from the vector routes for every source and collection ID that sanitizes to nothing, except an empty source, which the routes replace with `transformers`.
- **Fixes:** `8bd570ff8` (files folder), `7c1cb041b` (group chat IDs), `5f40fd1cb` (chat export and chat info), `cab2e7be3` (group delete id), `3819943a4` (vector index regeneration), `5c194ad04` (vector folder names), `42e7698e4` (asset routes).

### Dialog adapters and legacy DOM

- **Breaks:** moving Chat History controls into a shared Window can lose the original cleanup tree, leave dynamically created backup nodes behind, or return focus to a detached mobile sheet action.
- **Guard:** record source parents, sibling positions, child order and hidden state before adoption; restore the source before removing the page and use a connected mobile chat-chip fallback when the sheet opener is disposed. Keep backup visibility driven by the list-page parent state.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-chat-dialogs.test.js`; `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js`; `tests/mobile-shell-smoke.e2e.js`; `tests/mobile-character-toggle-and-panels.e2e.js`.
- **Hash links under `<base href="/">`:** `public/index.html:6` sets `<base href="/">`, so a hash-only link such as `href="#bg_global_tab"` resolves to `/#bg_global_tab`, not to the current URL. jQuery UI 1.13.2 tabs (`_isLocal`) compare that with `location.href`, so with any query string in the page URL (the login redirect keeps the query, logout adds `?noauto=true`) the Backgrounds tabs counted as remote and `load()` fetched `/` into a new panel: a second copy of the app whose `#sheld` and `#chat` came first in document order, which broke Home. It shows only after first run, because `initBackgrounds()` runs after onboarding; upstream SillyTavern has the same markup (`1da6e4012`). Guard in `public/scripts/backgrounds.js` (`initBackgrounds`): `aria-controls` on each tab `<li>` from its anchor's hash, `tabs({ beforeLoad: event => event.preventDefault() })`, and the `aria-live` jQuery UI puts on "remote" panels removed. An unhandled `href="#"` click under `<base>` navigates to `/#`, a full reload when the page has a query; the existing handlers call `preventDefault`. Test: `tests/backgrounds-tabs-query.e2e.js` (query at init, query lost, query gained after init, no query; one `#sheld`, local panel ids, no request for `/`).
- **Closing popups keep their IDs:** a Popup stays in the document until its exit animation ends (`runAfterAnimation`, 200ms for the theme's `.popup[closing]` rule), and its `show()` promise resolves only after `dlg.remove()`. A dialog that carries fixed IDs (the message screenshot form: `#message_screenshot_start_id`, `#message_screenshot_end_id`) therefore existed twice when it was reopened inside that window, and `locator.fill` failed with a strict mode violation in "recovers after a failed capture" (`tests/message-screenshot.e2e.js`): a failed capture toasts after about 110ms. The test had passed before `e14b96c66` only by accident: `.popup[closing]` used `animation: pop-out var(--popup-animation-speed) ...`, and `--sb-transition-fast` is a duration plus an easing, so the shorthand was invalid at computed-value time and the dialog was removed in about 30ms.
  - **Guard:** `promptForMessageScreenshotRange` (`public/script.js`) awaits the previous screenshot dialog's `show()` before it builds the next one. A dialog with fixed IDs does the same, or waits on `.popup[closing]` leaving the document. Tests that reopen such a dialog wait for the old one to leave first.
  - **Tests:** `tests/message-screenshot.e2e.js` ("never holds two screenshot forms when reopened while the first dialog is closing", "recovers after a failed capture ...").
- **Adopted search inputs:** restore a caller's input to its original parent and sibling on page teardown, remove only frame-added classes and attributes, and unbind frame listeners. Reuse must retain one input callback and the shared Ctrl/Cmd+F and type-to-search behavior; Escape clears before closing only when `escapeClearsFirst` is opted in. Destroying active header-slot selection must restore the real title and controls without stealing focus from a closing dialog. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-dialog.test.js`; `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js`.
- **Opening layout:** a dialog showed its upstream layout for the length of the opening animation and then changed: a framed Popup showed labelled inputs and a leftover heading before the adapted rows, a destructive confirmation showed a blue OK that turned red, and a Window opened at one size and jumped to another. `Popup.show()` (`public/scripts/popup.js`) runs `onOpen` through `runAfterAnimation()` (`public/scripts/utils.js`), which waits for `animationend` or 500 ms, and the adapters changed the DOM there. With reduced motion `onOpen` runs in the same task as `show()`, so the jump does not show.
  - **Guard:** DOM, class, focus and layout changes of a Popup go in `onOpening(popup)`. `opened()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/popup-frame.js`) ends with it, in the same task as `showModal()`, after the frame is built and the default focus is set, through `runOpening()`. That function logs a throw, so the dialog still opens; calls `contentChanged()`, which measures the frame again; and finishes the CSS transitions the handler started: a class added after the style flush in `opened()` otherwise transitions, and OK faded from blue to red. Leave `onOpen` where it is, because `docs/dialogs.md` guarantees its timing to extensions. Load the list of a content-sized Window before it opens.
  - **Opening scale:** during the animation `getBoundingClientRect()` returns the size at scale 0.8. Measure with `offsetWidth`. A Popup Alert that measured rectangles kept long labels in a row with motion on and stacked them with reduced motion on. Code that has to read rectangles either stays in `onOpen`, as the extension row actions do, or divides the distance it takes from them by the scale, as the swipe picker does when it scrolls to the current swipe in `onOpening`; scrolling in `onOpen` showed the top of the list and then jumped.
  - **Measuring in `onOpening`:** the footer height reservation (`--sb-popup-response-height`) updates from a `ResizeObserver`, after `onOpening` ends. Code that measures the body after it changes the footer calls `popup.sbFrame.contentChanged()` first; otherwise the swipe picker's scroll position is off by the change in footer height.
  - **Height lock:** `bindWindowHeightLock()` records its first height in `opened()`, before `onOpening` runs, and only grows. `contentChanged()` calls `remeasure()`, or a content-sized sheet keeps the empty space that the adapter removed (60px on the phone Admin and Snapshots Windows). A page binds no height lock (see Short Windows and their pages), so there `contentChanged()` only lays the frame out again.
  - **Theme transitions:** Moonlit Echoes sets `transition: all 0.5s` on `.menu_button`, so `flex-grow` and `width` animate between the layouts of an Alert, and the buttons start narrow. A transition starts from the style before the change, so `layoutWithoutTransitions()` keeps transitions off until a forced layout has applied the final value. Both Popup Alerts and `openAlert()` use it.
  - **Tests:** `tests/dialog-opening-layout.e2e.js` compares the first frame with the settled size at 1440×900 and 390×844; `tests/swipe-picker.e2e.js` (`with motion on`) also checks the scroll position. `tests/utility-dialog-first-paint.e2e.js` and `tests/destructive-first-paint.e2e.js` compare every frame from the first with the settled dialog, also in the WebKit config; `tests/unit/popup-contract.test.js`; `tests/unit/sillybunny-popup-frame.test.js`. The dialog test helpers default to reduced motion, so pass `MOTION` from `tests/helpers/dialog-opening.js` at boot; `page.emulateMedia()` after boot does not clear `body.reduced-motion`.
  - **Pitfall:** a spec with `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` runs `onOpen` at once, so it cannot see this class of bug. The first frame can come after the animation when the main thread stalls, as WebKit does on the first layout of the Extensions list, so the helper does not require a frame inside the animation. In WebKit the first Popup of a page can open with no animation, so the first-paint specs open a throwaway Popup first.
  - **Open:** The character lorebook dialog in `public/script.js` sets up Select2 in `onOpen`; the shared select row hides the Select2 container, so nothing visible changes, but its change handlers bind after the animation. The new chat confirmation in `public/script.js` (`newChatConfirm.html`) also binds its checkbox listener in `onOpen`, so a toggle during the animation is missed.
- **Preset Menu:** the Presets page hides each backend's original preset select row and action buttons, the Chat Completions title card and Master Import/Export, and the row's Preset Menu clicks those hidden originals, because upstream and PresetManager bind every action to them (`mountPresetRows()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`).
  - The menu is built on every open, so PresetManager's late restore-defaults button and the read-only Update of a bundled preset are current.
  - **Pitfall:** the menu's clicks are untrusted, and preset autosave saves a pending edit only on a trusted press of a control in `FLUSH_CONTROL_SELECTOR` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-preset-autosave.js`). That list names `[data-sb-preset-row]`, so the press on the row's ⋮ or picker saves first. Without it, a menu action such as Delete… or Rename… starts while the edit is still pending.
  - **Pitfall:** Edit Prompts is an activatable `createRow()` row, but the activatable-row and chevron rules live in the deferred `public/css/sillybunny-editor-shell.css`, which the Presets page does not load. On desktop the Characters list usually loads it first, which hides the gap. The page keeps its own copy of those rules in `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`.
  - **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-backend-settings.e2e.js` (menu sections, Import… opens a file chooser, Edit Prompts styled with the Editor stylesheet off), `tests/sillybunny-preset-autosave.e2e.js` (read-only Update, the press saves at once, Edit Prompts and autosave placement).
- **Preset API/Sampler Linking:** `mountPresetLinkingGroup()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js` moves the three Chat Completions linking blocks out of their drawer into a group after Presets. It removes the emptied drawer, because the flatten pass would turn it into an empty headed section inside Presets.
  - **Pitfall:** `preparePromptingPanel()` runs while the Prompting content is still detached from the document, so `document.getElementById()` returns `null` there. Look nodes up from the container instead.
  - **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-backend-settings.e2e.js` (the Preset API/Sampler Linking group test).
- **Character editor action menu:** the More menu (`_buildActionSections()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-character-editor.js`) offers Global Styles, External Media and Character Stats by clicking hidden transports. Their handlers act on the selected group or on `this_chid`, which is not always the card in the editor. Upstream hides `#character_open_media_overrides` with an inline `display: none` where its dialog would act on another entity: in the create form (`select_rm_create()` in `public/script.js`) and on a group member's card (`select_selected_character()` toggles it with `!selected_group`).
  - **Breaks:** the menu listed External Media in both states anyway, so in the create form it opened the dialog for the character that was selected when the form opened, and in group peek for the group.
  - **Guard:** the menu leaves out a recovered action whose transport has an inline `display: none`. `.sb-editor-transport` hides every transport with `display: none !important`, so the resolved style is `none` for all of them and only the inline style carries upstream's signal. Global Styles and Character Stats never get an inline display, so the menu offers them in the create form, where they act on `this_chid`; upstream shows both buttons in the create form too.
  - **Tests:** `tests/character-editor-rendering.e2e.js` (`the action menu offers External Media while editing and leaves it out of the create form`, 1440 and 390 px). Group peek has no test.
- **Adopted selects and their descriptions:** `createSelectRow()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/select-row.js`) hides the adopted `<select>` from assistive technology (`aria-hidden="true"`, `tabindex="-1"`), and its trigger button is the control that takes focus, so a description on the select reaches no one.
  - **Breaks:** the trigger was described only by the row subtitle. The NanoGPT Allowed and Ignored providers rows lost the PAYG billing warning (`aria-describedby="nanogpt_billing_warning"` in `public/index.html`), and seven other Connections select rows, `#model_openai_select` among them, lost the help that `applyConnectionsFormPass()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-connections-form.js`) attaches to the select after the row is built.
