Use plain `test` for a test that reads a rect or a computed layout, a sheet or floating presentation, or an art scale, because those answers change with the window. When unsure, use `test`: it only adds passes to a quick run, while `sizeFreeTest` in the wrong place hides a failure from every run that sets the variable. `tests/.eslintrc.cjs` lists `sizeFreeTest` as an alias of `test`, so the Playwright rules still apply to its body. At a size a quick run skips, `sizeFreeTest` and its `.skip`, `.only`, `.fixme`, `.fail` and `.describe` forms register nothing (`tests/unit/e2e-matrix-helper.test.js`).
  - **Trimmed to the desktop whole:** `tests/home-mode-memory.e2e.js`, `tests/managed-updates.e2e.js` and `tests/temporary-chat.e2e.js` run at 1440×900 only. `tests/request-errors.e2e.js` has no sizes; a quick run keeps the first row of each API path.
  - **Always run in full:** the "Home shell width" describe (1440 and 1920) and the describes with a fixed size in `tests/home-layout.e2e.js`, `tests/home-windows.e2e.js`, `tests/splash.e2e.js` and `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js`. The `Splash at <size>` loop in `tests/splash.e2e.js` is not trimmed either.
- **Frame timings in WebKit start too early:** WebKit keeps the main thread busy for several seconds after `APP_READY` (5 to 9 s in the Playwright 1.64.1 WebKit container, at `f6c1b217b` as well), so its first frames come seconds apart. A test that counts the frames or frame gaps of a motion it starts then measures that boot work as a stall. In the WebKit container the first one to three frames of a page push can still stall, depending on the host's state, so `tests/welcome-transitions.e2e.js` fails its painted-frames floor in about one run in ten and stays out of `DIALOG_SPECS`; run it by hand with `--retries=2`.
  - **Guard:** `waitForQuietFrames(page)` (`tests/helpers/quiet-frames.js`) waits for 10 frames in a row under 50 ms, and throws with the latest gaps when they do not come. `tests/welcome-transitions.e2e.js` calls it before it installs its transition probe.
  - **CDP is Chromium only:** `page.context().newCDPSession()` works only in Chromium-based browsers (Playwright documents it so), so a test that throttles the CPU through it skips there (`tests/welcome-transitions.e2e.js`, "Troubleshooting still ends at its rest height when the page is slow to render"). Getting Started ("that the app applies without a gesture of the user…") and the two pixel-ratio cases of `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js` skip in WebKit for the same reason; the reload step of Getting Started's save test stays, and only its last step needs a script without a gesture.
- **Home specs in WebKit:** the 17 Home, Welcome and splash specs that are not in `DIALOG_SPECS` showed these differences from Chromium. Each is a test assumption, not a product defect, except the last.
  - **Focus scrolls later:** `focus()` on a button inside a clipped, sideways-scrolling row (the Recent Chats filters) scrolls the row in the next rendering update in WebKit, and inside the call in Chromium. Tab reaches the buttons in Playwright WebKit (real Safari skips buttons unless its Tab setting is on, which this test does not cover). Poll `scrollLeft` (`tests/home-layout.e2e.js`).
  - **Observers attach later:** the hero art starts watching its band when its layout runs, a frame after the panel appears, so a count of observed elements read at once is one short in WebKit. Poll it (`waitUntilFollowed` in `tests/home-observers.e2e.js`).
  - **Programmatic focus shows a ring:** a dialog's first button, focused by script after a mouse click, matches `:focus-visible` in WebKit and not in Chromium, so its inset ring changes the pixels next to it. A contrast test that samples pixels beside a chip blurs the active element first (`tests/getting-started.e2e.js`).
  - **Clipboard:** Playwright WebKit has no `clipboard-write` permission, so use `trackClipboard` and `copiedText` (`tests/helpers/browser-neutral.js`).
  - **Splash screenshots need one line:** the splash picks its line at random, and the line's width moves the picture by up to a device pixel, so two pages that share a screenshot clip pass `sameLine` to `openSplash` (`tests/splash-loop-frames.e2e.js`).
  - **Splash loop edge, a product difference:** at a fractional device scale WebKit dropped the first device row or column of the first loop frame (Reisen's top row and Tewi's left column at 1.5, Tewi at 1.25 and 1.75), because `.sb-boot-loop { overflow: hidden }` clips at an edge that is rounded to a device pixel, and the still has no clip. `.sb-boot-loop` is now clipped with `clip-path: inset(-1px)` and no overflow clip (`public/css/loader.css`). Clip a box that must show every edge pixel with an outset `clip-path`, never `overflow: hidden`. The outset is one CSS pixel because rounding moves an edge by less than one device pixel, which is at most one CSS pixel at a scale of 1 and above: `-0.5px` fixed 1.5 and 1.75 but still lost Tewi's column at 1.25. Nothing can show in the outset: all 21 frame pictures are the scene's size at 0,0, and each effect is an `svg` of the scene's size that clips itself. Below a scale of about 0.75 the first-frame check still fails with the outset (also at `-2px`), so the check covers 1 to 3. Tests: `tests/splash-loop-frames.e2e.js` ("looks like its still at scale …" for 1, 1.1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2 and 3 in Chromium and WebKit).
- **Fitted text boxes settle late in WebKit:** About ▸ Troubleshooting's info box is fitted to its text by a ResizeObserver (`fitToText`). Chromium has the fitted height at the first sample; WebKit runs the callback about 100 ms after the page shows, so a one-shot read sees the `rows` height and reports an overflow that is not there. Poll the measurement (`tests/splash.e2e.js`, the boot marks in Troubleshooting).
- **Forced colours in tests:** forced colours keep a background colour's alpha and drop every `box-shadow`, so a computed colour can mislead and an edge or state drawn only with a shadow disappears. Measure contrast from the rendered pixels (`readTextContrast` in `tests/helpers/page-contrast.js`), and give a shadow-only edge or state an outline or border under `forced-colors: active`. Found that way and fixed: the greeting picture's edge and focus ring (borders of the same box in `CanvasText` and `Highlight`, `public/css/sillybunny-home.css`; `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js`), toasts (an outline, because a toast rule sets `border: none !important`; `tests/request-errors.e2e.js`) and an `aria-invalid` field (a double border, since forced colours have no error colour, with `!important` because entry rows set their border with an id selector; `tests/connection-notices.e2e.js`). Open: the persona entry and tag-manager name fields have their own `aria-invalid` rules (`sillybunny-persona-detail.css`, `sillybunny-tag-manager.css`) and likely the same problem. In Playwright WebKit, `forcedColors: 'active'` makes the media query match but applies no palette (authored colours stay, `Canvas` stays white), and real Safari never matches it (WebKit bug 225281): a test of the palette replacing authored colours skips in WebKit, while a test of the app's own `@media (forced-colors: active)` rules still runs there (`tests/splash.e2e.js`, `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js`).
- **Measuring toasts:** `protectMaintainerServer()` hides toasts until `showToasts(page)`, and a toast that times out can vanish mid-measure, so a test that reads a toast's geometry sets `toastr.options.timeOut = 0` first (`tests/request-errors.e2e.js`).
- **Firefox runs:** `npm run test:e2e:firefox` (from `tests/`, against a running server, in the Playwright container) runs `tests/playwright.firefox.config.js`: the WebKit config's `DIALOG_SPECS` plus `welcome-transitions`.
  - **Breaks:** after every server start, the first load of each browser context reloads twice to clear old caches (`sillybunny-restart-cache-clear.js`). Playwright Firefox's `goto({ waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded' })` resolves on the first document, so the next call into the page fails with "Execution context was destroyed" in `waitForAppReady`. Chromium resolves after the last reload.
  - **Guard:** the global setup `tests/helpers/restart-cache-clear-seed.js` seeds `sillybunny_cache_boot` and `sillybunny_cache_cleared` in the storage state, reading the names from the page script the server serves; `tests/restart-cache-clear-seed.test.js` ties them to the middleware.
  - **Not runnable in Playwright Firefox,** listed with reasons in the config: drags past the bottom edge of the viewport (Firefox delivers no mouse events there), `page.setViewportSize()` before the first navigation in an `isMobile` context (it never settles), a computed font size compared as a string (Gecko serialises 1/64 px), and `:focus-visible` after a lone Shift press. Tab at the last control of a Popup modal does not wrap without browser controls to take the focus; that one is an open question for a headed Firefox.
  - **Limits:** headless Firefox and Xvfb draw in software, so a problem in the compositor does not show, and Firefox's Playwright video capture tears even on a plain test page.
- **The Playwright clock keeps running until it is paused:** after `page.clock.install()` time flows naturally as the timers fire, so a timed picture checked at an exact time drifts by the page's start-up time. Pause it (`page.clock.pauseAt()`) for exact-time checks, as `tests/art-motion.e2e.js` does. `page.clock` replaces `setTimeout`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `performance` and the other time functions, and `install` must come before any other clock-related call, so a test's own patch of those functions goes after the clock call or is lost.
- **Breaks:** with many workers against one server, tests time out. A server handles its requests on one main thread, and `workers: '50%'` started 64 workers on a 128-thread machine. **Guard:** `getDefaultWorkers()` in `tests/e2e-servers.js` allows at most 16 workers for one server (test: `tests/e2e-servers.test.js`).
- **Breaks:** the settings conflict prompt ("Settings changed on another device") opened in a page about one second after APP_READY when another page on the same server had saved since this page loaded its settings. It covered the page and blocked every later settings save. Tests on one server met it often (37 of 57 first `openQuietChatForSmoke()` calls with 4 servers; 7 of 8 pages that booted at once), and so did a user with a second tab or device, without any settings change. **Cause:** every boot saves the settings without a change, through `initCustomSelectedSamplers()` and `validateDisabledSamplers()` in `public/scripts/samplerSelect.js` (SillyTavern #4782 moved what they saved to localforage), and the version check refused a save from a page that had loaded before another page saved (`prepareSettingsSave()` in `src/settings-version.js`). **Guards:** a page sends no save when its settings equal the text that it last loaded or saved (`createSettingsSync()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-settings-sync.js`, which keeps a key-sorted copy of that text, because the page keeps changing the objects that it parsed). This also covers a page whose settings another tab changed in the second after its boot. `POST /api/settings/save` answers a save whose content equals the stored settings with `unchanged: true` and the stored version (`isUnchangedSettingsSave()` in `src/settings-version.js`), so it raises no version, and `saveSettingsInner()` in `public/script.js` accepts that answer. A real change from an older page still gets 409 and the prompt. **Tests:** `tests/settings-version-guard.test.js`, `tests/settings-save-unchanged.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-settings-sync.test.js`, `tests/openai-custom-endpoint-credentials.test.js`. **Test guards:** tests that save real changes at the same time on one server still meet the prompt, as two users would. `protectMaintainerServer()` (`tests/helpers/maintainer-server.js`) answers `/api/settings/save` for the specs that call it, and `keepSavesInPage()` (`tests/helpers/saves-in-page.js`) also serves the saved settings back to `/api/settings/get` (`tests/nanogpt-controls.e2e.js`, for its reload test). `tests/frontend/shared-page.js`, `tests/chat-scroll-regressions.e2e.js` and `tests/frontend/CoreBrowserParity.e2e.js` (its preset test awaits a save inside `selectPreset()`) answer it themselves. `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js` also opens a new chat on each page, because pages that write one chat file at the same time fail each other's chat integrity check.
- **Breaks:** a test helper that removes a popup's `<dialog>` leaves the popup's promise waiting forever. `saveSettings()` chains every save on `settingsSaveQueue` in `public/script.js`, so with the settings conflict prompt removed, every later save of that page waits too: `nanogpt-controls.e2e.js` "provider controls preserve restrictions" hung until its 90 s timeout. A popup settles only through `complete()` (`public/scripts/popup.js`), whose `runAfterAnimation()` callback has a 500 ms fallback. **Guard:** `dismissOpenDialogIfPresent()` in `tests/chat-scroll-regression-helpers.js` completes each popup in `Popup.util.popups` with `completeCancelled()`, and removes only the other dialogs.
- **Breaks:** a test on a page that other spec files share fails on state that one of them left behind. **Guards:** `tests/frontend/shared-page.js` restores the chat metadata, which holds the local variables, and the global variables of its boot before each test, and it fails a test that runs on a page that has navigated since the boot. Only files that leave no other state behind import it: MessageFormatter adds permanent formatter hooks, MacroEnvBuilder registers an env provider that throws, and StscriptParserFixes replaces the parser settings getter, so they boot for each test. **Tests:** the seven files that import it.
- **Breaks:** a byte budget fails when the run asks for uncompressed responses. `tests/frontend-performance.e2e.js` sums `transferSize`, which is about 20.7 MB of JavaScript without compression against its 12 MiB budget. **Guard:** that spec asks for compressed responses with `test.use({ extraHTTPHeaders })`, whatever the config or runner sends.
- **Breaks:** with `SILLYBUNNY_TEST_SERVERS`, a run started no server and used the default base URL when its shell passed `TEST_PARALLEL_INDEX` with an empty value. `getServerConfig()` in `tests/e2e-servers.js` took every defined value for the index of a worker. **Guard:** an empty value counts as unset (test: `tests/e2e-servers.test.js`).
- **Breaks:** a server that the config started still showed the first-run prompt to the tests, so a spec file that does not answer it failed all its tests. **Cause:** `tests/e2e-servers-prime.js` closed its page at APP_READY, and the app saves the end of the first run only after its save delay (`saveSettingsDebounced()` in `public/script.js`). The save came first only while the rest of the boot took longer than that delay. **Guard:** the prime step waits until the stored settings say that the first run is over (`waitUntilFirstRunIsOver()` in `tests/e2e-servers.js`). **Tests:** `tests/e2e-servers.test.js`; `tests/e2e-servers.e2e.js` checks the server of each worker that runs it.
- **Breaks:** a server that the config started used the `config.yaml` of the repository root, so it ran with the settings of the developer's own server, and it wrote that file with the default values (`port: 4444`) when the file was absent. **Guard:** `getServerCommand()` in `tests/e2e-servers.js` gives each server a `--configPath` inside its data root. **Tests:** `tests/e2e-servers.test.js`; `tests/e2e-servers.e2e.js` checks the server of each worker that runs it.
- **Breaks:** with `SILLYBUNNY_TEST_SERVERS`, every worker would use one server if Playwright stopped importing the config in each worker after it sets `TEST_PARALLEL_INDEX` (it does in 1.64.1, `lib/worker/workerProcessEntry.js`). **Guards:** the main process config has no `baseURL`, so such a worker fails at its first navigation; `tests/e2e-servers.e2e.js` checks the server of each worker that runs it.
- **Breaks:** a fake clock moved past a debounce does not prove that no write was sent. `page.clock.runFor()` fires the page's timers at once, but a request that a timer starts reaches a Node route handler in real time, after `runFor()` returns. **Guard:** after the move, a negative check waits a short real tail (`WRITE_TAIL_MS` in `tests/sillybunny-preset-autosave.e2e.js` and `tests/connection-profile-change-notice.e2e.js`); a positive check polls for the write. A window that ends only when the page's own requests settle (the profile settle wait in `profile-change-model.js`) stays in real time, because those requests land late on the moved timeline.
- **Breaks:** a locator on a shared component class matches every surface that builds that component. `.ds-profile-row` comes from `createProfileRow()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/select-row.js`), which the Connections panel, the preset rows and the in-chat agents use, so `.ds-profile-row .ds-select-row__menu` matched five menus and four tests of `tests/connection-profile-change-notice.e2e.js` failed in strict mode. **Guard:** scope such a locator to its section (`.sb-connections-profile`).
- **Breaks:** a button row inside a settings page showed no focus ring. `#sb-settings-page .sb-settings-mounted-shell :is(.ds-pref-group, .sb-settings-flat-section) > .ds-row { box-shadow: none }` (`public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`) outranks the shared `.ds-row.ds-button-row:focus-visible` of `public/css/sillybunny-editor-shell.css`, so the ring came only from page rules; the Connections rules that gave it were deleted with the page's old row styling, and `tests/sillybunny-connections-layout.e2e.js` ("CC and TC Authorize match the General button row") failed. **Guard:** `#sb-settings-page .sb-connections-panel .ds-row.ds-button-row:focus-visible` restores it for Connections. Other settings pages with button rows rely on their own rules.
- **Breaks:** `presentButtonRow()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-connections-form.js`) removes `menu_button` from an upstream `div` button, so the settings inventory no longer saw `#customize_additional_parameters` as a button (it matches `.menu_button` and `[role="button"]`). **Guard:** the helper sets `role="button"`, `tabindex="0"` and an Enter/Space handler itself, and its teardown goes through the panel's controllers.
- **Breaks:** `test.describe.configure({ mode: 'serial' })` skips the rest of its group after a failure ("did not run"), so removing it can expose a test that never ran. In `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js` the desktop "Stop during streamed reasoning" test failed on a close button that the Settings page had replaced, and the mobile twin first ran, and failed the same way, once the mode was gone. **Guard:** before calling a failure after such a change a regression, run the test alone on the old tree with only that test selected.
- **Breaks:** a test that writes to a fixed folder outside the repo fails on every machine without it: a recursive `mkdir` under a home folder that does not exist throws `EACCES`, because `/home` belongs to root. About 25 tests wrote review screenshots to `/home/geechan/.opencode/plan/...` or `/tmp/opencode/...`. **Guard:** write artefacts to `testInfo.outputPath()` (one folder per test, under the run's output), as `tests/helpers/q56-review.js` does.
- **Breaks:** a fixed wait for a dialog after each boot costs a second per test even where no dialog can open. **Guard:** `protectMaintainerServer()` records its pages (`isMaintainerServerProtected()` in `tests/helpers/maintainer-server.js`); `openQuietChatForSmoke()` and `openReadyChat()` skip the first dialog wait for those pages, because neither the settings conflict prompt nor Welcome opens there, and keep it for other pages.
- **Breaks:** in WebKit, a control inside a Settings section can keep a computed `visibility: hidden` after the section becomes active. Inactive hosted roots have `visibility: hidden !important; content-visibility: hidden` (`public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`, `.sb-settings-mounted-shell:not(.sb-settings-active-root)`), and WebKit did not recompute the inherited value of `#tts_provider`'s group after activation: `getComputedStyle()` and `checkVisibility()` said hidden, while `elementFromPoint()` returned the select and screenshots showed it; any style write on the group fixed it. Playwright's actionability check then waited until the timeout (`tests/native-dialog-replacements.e2e.js`, SpeechT5, about half the WebKit runs). **Guard:** that spec selects with `force: true`. The app keeps the `content-visibility` rule; app code that reads computed `visibility` there can see the same stale value.
- **A wait that an endless animation holds:** `element.getAnimations({ subtree: true }).length === 0` is never true while a spinner or an endless hover animation runs, and `animation.finished` never resolves for one, so a step that waits on either holds until the test timeout. The swipe picker did this: the click that opens it leaves the pointer on a row's delete icon, which spins while it is hovered. Wait with `settle()` (`tests/helpers/surfaces/steps.js`), which counts only animations that have an end and fails after a bounded time with the list of those still running (`513f155a2`, `d2f7dceda`).
- **A wait for every image:** an `<img loading="lazy">` that has no box, is hidden or sits off screen never starts to load, so `[...images].every(image => image.complete)` never becomes true. Home's chat rows behind "show more" did this once more chats were seeded, and every Home surface timed out. Count a lazy image only when it is shown inside the viewport, as `homeSettled()` and `windowSettled()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/home.js` do (`fd841aeb5`).
- **The app scrolls a loaded chat back to the bottom:** after a chat loads, `scrollLoadedChatToBottom()` (`public/script.js`) scrolls to the bottom again on timers (`CHAT_LOAD_SCROLL_SETTLE_DELAYS_MS`, the last at 2400 ms, plus `CHAT_LOAD_BOTTOM_LOCK_EXTRA_MS`) until the user scrolls. A scripted `scrollIntoView()` in that window is undone by a later timer. A `wheel` or touch event on `#chat` clears the lock (`clearChatLoadBottomLock()`), so a test turns the wheel over the chat first, as `scrollToOlderMessagesControl()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/chat-steps.js` does (`3d5f3e028`).
- **The delayed chat autofocus dismisses an open menu:** `getChat()` (`public/script.js`) triggers a click and a focus on `#send_textarea` a moment after `CHAT_LOADED` (`debounce_timeout.short`), unless a visible text input has the focus or Settings is open. A menu that closes on any click outside it closes when it is opened in that window. The persona picker (`public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`) did, for a user as well as for a test; its outside-click handler now ignores a click that is not the user's (`event.isTrusted`, `644fde062`; `tests/persona-picker-dismiss.e2e.js`). A test that opens another such menu waits until the focus is in a visible input or textarea first, but only when it loaded the chat itself: `openPersonaMenu()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/status.js` goes through `openAuditChat()` of `dialog-steps.js` (`66247bfe9`). A chat that the boot restored has had its autofocus already and the persona bubble can hold the focus, and a wait for an input then never ends (about one boot in fifty, a 120 s hang).
- **The delayed chat autofocus takes the focus back from the greeting picture button:** the same callback (`getChat()` and `getTemporaryChat()` in `public/script.js`) ran after the greeting viewer had closed and returned the focus to `.sbGreetingArtOpen`, because a chat that loads more than once ends its autofocus 200 ms after the last load, and a button is not a text input. The focus went to `#send_textarea`, and `dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js` (greeting picture viewer, Escape) failed 2 of 24 runs at load 44. Both callbacks now return while the focus is inside `.sbGreetingArt`; `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js` (`keeps the focus on the picture button when the delayed autofocus of a chat load comes after it`) loads the chat again and puts the focus on the button inside the 200 ms. 80 of 80 runs passed at load 61 after the change.
- **The logit bias list is not stable right after the Sampling page shows:** the app fills the preset select and builds the rows after the page is shown, and applying a Chat Completion preset that holds bias presets sets `bias_preset_selected` from the preset (`public/scripts/openai.js`, where the preset's bias presets are normalised). In audit runs the list was empty in some runs and filled in others, and the page differed by a few pixels in height. `selectLogitBiasPreset()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/steps.js` selects the fixture's preset, waits for its rows, checks again once the page has settled, and selects again when the selection was undone (`c7894912c`). The order of the two events was not traced.
- **Seeded data changes what a page shows:** the persona list shows a batch of personas (`Personas_PerPage`, 50 when unset) behind a Show More row, and Home hides older chat rows behind "show more", so a step that expects a row on the first page fails once other fixtures add data. Steps page to the row (`openPersonaEditorEntry()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/panel-steps.js`), and the audit global setup seeds every fixture for every run, so that a run narrowed to one family sees the same data as a full one (`tests/audit/seed-fixtures.global-setup.js`, `de1fe8f5d`).
- **`firstRun: false` in any test file:** `tests/unit/welcome-helper-coverage.test.js` flags a file under `tests/` that contains `firstRun: false` or `firstRun = false` without an import of a Welcome helper, whatever the text means there. An option of that name in a registry file failed it (`070931790`); name such an option differently.
- **State read during a transition:** `tests/character-shell-performance.e2e.js` compared two fills while the toggle's `background-color` transition ran, and now waits for the transition (`bc29198c0`). The foundation sheet-motion test (`tests/foundation/shell-navigation.e2e.js`) polled for the sheet's Web Animation after the click and missed it when tracing slowed the action; it observes from before the click (`638cb6157`).
- **A hidden icon that keeps spinning:** `.hidden` only sets `visibility`, so the hidden connection profile spinner ran an endless `fa-spin` animation for the whole session, and a test that waits for running animations saw it. `:is(.fa-spin, .fa-spin-pulse, .fa-pulse).hidden` no longer animates (`public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`, `362bfdb4d`); `tests/header-icon-button-parity.e2e.js` covers both states of the spinner.
- **Tests that need a connection or saved data:** a throwaway data root has no API connection and no saved tracker. `tests/frontend/ConversationLorebooks.e2e.js` sets a connected status before it sends, and the long-tracker test in `tests/in-chat-agents-editor.e2e.js` creates and removes its own saved tracker through a pass-through that is open only for those two calls (`638cb6157`). `tests/card-script-sandbox.e2e.js` holds Shift over the message to reveal its actions, because the run button is hidden until the row is expanded (`bc29198c0`).
- **A journey that runs close to its timeout fails when the app gets slightly slower:** the mobile "Manager create…" test of `tests/character-folders.e2e.js` is about 100 steps of 0.3 to 1 s against the default 30 s, and took 29.5 s at `7b4ba0a66` (1 run of 3 already failed) and 30.3 s at the head, where the folder rows animate (`createFolderMotion`) and the manager's close takes 0.5 s longer. It is a data-preservation journey and the motion has its own test, so the test runs with `page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: 'reduce' })` (17 s on a phone, 13 s on desktop). When a test fails only at the limit, time its steps against an older tree before raising the limit.
- **A `waitForFunction` predicate that throws fails the wait:** `window.SillyTavern.getContext()` throws `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'chat' before initialization` (`public/scripts/st-context.js`) while the app's modules start, so a bare `getContext()?.eventSource…has('app_ready')` fails the test on a page that is slow to start (2 of 60 runs of the "connection profile change notice" test at 16 workers on one server). `waitForAppReady()` (`tests/helpers/app-ready.js`) and `finishBoot()` (`tests/helpers/surfaces/boot.js`) treat a throw as not ready. About 45 other specs and helpers still carry the bare form.
- **A settings page builds its groups after its tab opens:** a read of `.sb-prompting-group:visible` titles right after `openPanel()` missed "Character Names Behavior" on Presets (`tests/sillybunny-backend-settings.e2e.js`). Poll the titles (`expect.poll`) while that page is open; only the open page has visible groups.
- **The startup request count is an exact ratchet:** `STARTUP_RESOURCE_LIMIT` in `tests/frontend-performance.e2e.js` equals the present count, so a new module that loads at startup fails the test until its commit raises the limit on purpose (`bc29198c0`, `55244b488`). The budget for compressed JavaScript there is 13 MiB (`646f13755`).

- **A page already has its own `#qr--bar`, and on a phone it sits in the collapsed extension tray:** a test that injects a second `#qr--bar` reads the real, empty one through `querySelector`. `tests/composer-bar-controls.e2e.js` fills the page's own bar and opens the tray with `.sb-bottom-chat-tray-toggle`; the tray opens with a scale animation, so a height is polled (`expect.poll`) and not read after a few frames (42.1px against 44px).

- **The Home card's open action loads the chat twice, and the first greeting is on screen before the second chat replaces it:** `openRoleplayChat` (`sillybunny-home/assistant-chat.js`) runs `selectCharacterById` and then `doNewChat`. A test that waits for `#chat .mes[mesid="0"]` and sends at once can have its generation ended by the second load (`generation_ended`, a new chat, no request to the provider), which is how `tests/request-errors.e2e.js` "chat answered with 401json" failed with no toast at all. `openChatWithToastRecorder` counts `chatLoaded` events from before the click and waits for two. Other specs that open a chat through this card and send straight away may have the same race.

### Unit tests that run git

- **Breaks:** the unit suite, started from a process that git spawned, writes to the real repository: on 2026-11-12, `git rebase -x 'cd tests && npm run test:unit'` left `core.bare = true` and a `[user]` section in the shared `.git/config`, two tags (`v0.0.1`, `v9.9.9`) and test commits on the rebase head.
- **Files:** `tests/server-plugin-manager.test.js`, `tests/bootstrap-installer.test.js`, `tests/list-sb-class-changes.test.js`, `tests/bump-agent-template-versions.test.js` and other suites that build temporary repositories with plain `git init`, `git config`, `git commit` and `git tag`.
- **Guards:**
  - git exports `GIT_DIR` and related variables to `git rebase --exec` commands and to hooks, and a child `git` follows `GIT_DIR` instead of its working directory, so `git init --bare` in a temporary folder re-initialises the real repository.
  - Run the suite from a normal shell. To test each commit of a series, `git checkout <sha>` in turn and run it there, or unset `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE` and `GIT_INDEX_FILE` first.
- **Repair:** `git config core.bare false`, remove the `[user]` section, delete the tags, `git rebase --quit`, then `git checkout -f <branch>` and `git clean -fd` in the worktree.

- **Confirm verbs:** an upstream confirm that sets no `okButton` shows "Yes". `confirmVerb(label, { destructive, negative })` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-delete-confirm.js` returns the Popup options for one call site (a spread under a `SillyBunny:` marker), so the destructive rule (`defaultResult: NEGATIVE`, `is-destructive` on OK in `onOpening`) lives once. The helper sits in a file that `script.js` already imports, so it adds no startup module. A `confirmVerb` spread that sets `negative` is a `cancelButton` key, which stops `replaceDefaultNegativeLabel` from renaming the negative to "Cancel"; use it where the negative means "do not do this part" and the flow goes on (seven sites). Third-party confirms keep the frame's default. Tests: `tests/unit/confirm-verbs.test.js` (the approved list per file, a marker within three lines of each use, and a parse of every upstream-origin CONFIRM call that fails on one without a verb), `tests/unit/sillybunny-destructive-confirm.test.js`, `tests/unit/popup-contract.test.js`.
- **Two Cancels and the Cancel slot:** a custom response that resolves to `null` is ordered `-2` in an Alert (`buildAlert` in `popup-frame.js`), ahead of the default Cancel and the negative, so the real cancel is first in a row and last in a stack. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-popup-frame.test.js`.
- **A mock of `popup.js` must export what the helper reads:** `sillybunny-delete-confirm.js` imports `POPUP_RESULT`, `POPUP_TYPE` and `Popup` from `popup.js`, and the call sites import `t` from `i18n.js`; a unit test that mocks `popup.js` for an upstream file needs all of them (`tests/unit/bulk-edit-overlay.test.js`, `tests/quick-reply-settings-ui.test.js`).
- **A confirm without `okButton` can read "OK" in zh-cn and zh-tw:** `popup.js` sets `data-i18n="OK"` on the OK button before the CONFIRM branch writes "Yes", and `i18n.js` translates every added `[data-i18n]` node, so a third-party confirm shows "确定" (checked in a zh-cn context). A label passed as `okButton` sets its own `data-i18n` and is not affected.
- **Legacy `#dialogue_popup_ok`:** it is the suggested action, and `callPopup` marks it `is-destructive` only for the types that delete. The sheet rule needs two ids (`#dialogue_popup_controls > #dialogue_popup_ok`) because `style.css` paints `#dialogue_popup_ok` crimson at one id.

### Dialog surfaces and layering

- **Breaks:** a broad `.sb-dialog` descendant textarea rule changes the in-chat Agents Regex editor height (159.75px instead of 160px).
- **Guard:** scope textarea sizing to the adapter rows that need it, not generic dialog descendants. `89cd805db` keeps the `8lh` minimum on the window-content adapter's scenario and YAML rows; regression: `tests/in-chat-agents-editor.e2e.js` (regex fields at least 160px).
- **The expanded editor's text area is named from the Window title (p6 follow-ups):** the `.editor_maximize` handler in `public/scripts/chats.js` (upstream-origin, `SillyBunny:` marker) builds the Window title from `label[for]`, then the `h4` or `.ds-field-header` around the button, and now a third source, `closest('.ds-pref-group-header')` and its `.ds-pref-group-heading`; the same string goes on the text area as `aria-label`. The persona, agent and World Info entry fields have no `label[for]`, `h4` or `.ds-field-header`, so their expanded Window had no title and the text area no name (six axe `label` records, now removed). Their expanded Window gets a title because of the third source. Test: `tests/window-content-dialogs.e2e.js` (the text area's accessible name equals `.sb-dialog__title`), the `field-expanded` audit states.
- **Floating rows:** an empty entry row can paint its placeholder underneath the floated title. Keep the hint in `aria-description` and suppress the painted placeholder while the title floats. Fixed in `357be55f7`; regression: `tests/sillybunny-editor-rows-layout.e2e.js`.
- **Action-menu sheets live for one opening:** the phone bottom sheet of `openActionMenu` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-action-menu.js`) used to be one module-level sheet that closing only hid. It kept `#sb-action-menu` and `#sb-action-sheet-title`, so the next popover got duplicate ids and its `aria-controls` resolved to the stale menu, and it kept `data-sb-prompt-menu` (written by `sillybunny-prompt-editor.js` and read by `sillybunny-preset-autosave.js`) on every later menu. `createSheet()` now builds a sheet per opening, `releaseSheetIds()` strips the ids from a leaving sheet at once, and the sheet is removed in `createSheetController`'s `onHidden` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-sheet.js`), which runs once both exits finish, immediately when nothing animates. Popovers keep their block size and top inside `bounds`, and keyboard focus scrolls the focused row into view (`getRevealScrollTop()`), because `focus({ preventScroll: true })` had left End and ArrowDown below a capped popover at 844×390. Tests: `tests/action-menu-viewport.e2e.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-action-menu.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-sheet.test.js`, `tests/sheet-swipe.e2e.js` (reads the drag state through a handle taken before the sheet is removed).
- **Help window levels and selection:** Help (`public/scripts/sillybunny-help/`) has a front page, section pages and topic pages, with a section sidebar. Wide layouts replace the content pane in place, and the trail, More Information and the sidebar go up; narrow layouts push section and topic pages, the trail pops to the page it names, and See Also swaps in place; one `view` state survives resizes across the collapse breakpoint. `validateHelpSections()` rejects a model with repeated ids, an empty section or an unknown `seeAlso` id before the window opens. The selected sidebar row is the neutral `--sb-flat-selected-bg` fill with an accent icon and an unchanged label weight, never a side stripe (DESIGN.md Active State); its hover keeps `--sb-flat-selected-hover-bg`, because the generic row hover (0,3,0) otherwise beats the selected fill. Page roots are named regions, so focus moving to a page is announced. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-help-topics.test.js`, `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` (Help).
- **Layer order:** side panels and zoomed avatars must stay below Settings and shell drawers. Keep sprite, avatar, panel, shell, settings, popover, and dialog layers in one ordered ladder. Fixed in `357be55f7`; tests: `tests/libadwaita-surface-layers-css.test.js`, `tests/side-panel-adapters.e2e.js`.
- **Shared entries:** upstream `.text_pole` rules and surface-specific selectors can silently fork dialog search fields. Keep the shared `.sb-dialog__entry` rule more specific than those inputs, remove surface copies, and compare rest/focus metrics across the manager overlays in their e2e suites.
- **Adopted rows:** moving an upstream list into shared dialog chrome must preserve the original action nodes as the handler boundary. Add adapter unit coverage for every forwarded action and keyboard route, plus an e2e that reaches the overflow actions through the production menu.
- **Touch close controls:** nested Popup close nodes can bypass direct-child coarse-pointer rules. Keep Close in the shared header layout and assert its 44×44 box on coarse-pointer Data Bank and Tag Manager surfaces.
- **Pushed-page completion:** closing an `openDialog()` frame must resolve every active page's `closed` promise with the root response, not only destroy its view. Drain the page stack during disposal so popped pages cannot settle again. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-dialog.test.js` (nested Close and root `forceClose()` with a page open).
- **Selected shell controls:** persistent current-section buttons share the same neutral selected fill, including `.sb-settings-active` Characters; test the computed fill against Home at desktop and mobile sizes. Test: `tests/character-shell-performance.e2e.js` (`Characters uses the Home selected-pill fill`).
- **Header icons and custom popovers:** `.sb-header-icon-button` uses the shared control radius, matching Chat Archive rather than becoming a pill; its local `--sb-icon-control-radius` also keeps the high-specificity icon-only rule in line. Persona and World Info base button rules exclude shared header icons and status-page actions so shared hover fills and suggested-action accents remain visible. A custom `openPopover()` closes when its own trigger is activated again; native manager triggers stop Enter from reaching the document keyboard bridge, which would synthesize a second click alongside the button's native activation. Tests: `tests/header-icon-button-parity.e2e.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-action-menu.test.js`, `tests/tag-manager.e2e.js`, `tests/character-folders.e2e.js`.
- **Status-page states and loading:** `createStatusPage` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/status-page.js`) loads `css/sillybunny-editor-shell.css` itself (`SillyBunnyAssets.loadStylesheetAsync`) and keeps the page `hidden`/`visibility: hidden` until that sheet resolves, so a surface that mounts it before the deferred sheet arrives does not flash unstyled. The key in the file must match the `LIST_EDITOR_STYLESHEET` call sites. A `state: 'loading'` page is hidden for `STATUS_PAGE_LOADING_DELAY` (200ms) before it shows, so a fast load never flashes a spinner; pass `delay: 0` where a test needs no blank frame (the characters panel, `tests/character-shell-performance.e2e.js`). `data-state` is a data attribute and `className` stays `sb-status-page`. The spinner carries `aria-busy`, so assertions on busy buttons must select `.menu_button[aria-busy]`. The theme's boxed `h3` rule (`sillybunny-theme.css`, `h3:not(.popup h3):not(.sb-dialog h3):not(#chat h3)`) has specificity (1,2,3) and boxed the title in every non-dialog host until the reset became `:root .sb-status-page h3.sb-status-page__title:not(#chat h3)`. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-status-page.test.js`, `tests/foundation/shared-ui.e2e.js` (`expectStatusPages`).
- **A loading page that waits keeps its place:** `createStatusPage({ state: 'loading' })` shows after `STATUS_PAGE_LOADING_DELAY` (200 ms), so a quick load shows no spinner. A page that replaces a panel (`revealDeferredCharacterPanel()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`) cannot start hidden with `hidden`, because for the delay the panel area would hold nothing; it passes `reserveSpace`, which keeps the page in the layout and only withholds its paint (`visibility: hidden`, shared with the held-until-styled state in `status-page.js`). The panel makes a new page for each request, because the delay starts when the page is made. Tests: `tests/character-shell-performance.e2e.js` (every frame before the panel shows has the page, with a height; it is painted only from the delay on), `tests/unit/sillybunny-character-shell-performance.test.js`.
- **Inline hint, not a status page, for an empty part of a section:** the persona menu's Scenario Notes, the companion panel's no-state row and the companion note card's empty and cancelled rows are one centred Caption line in the dimmed foreground (`.sb-inline-hint` in `sillybunny-tabs.css`, DESIGN.md Inline hint). The in-chat-agents sheet (`style.css`) has no `?v=` key at its call site, so its edit rides the extension's own load. Test: `tests/sillybunny-inline-hint-css.test.js`; the audit surfaces `status.persona-picker.no-notes`, `status.companion-panel.no-state`, `status.companion-card.empty`.
- **Status-page actions:** generic `.menu_button` fills and mobile corner rules can override the shared neutral/suggested hierarchy, including through the paper theme's `!important` radius. Exclude `.sb-status-page__action` from legacy defaults and keep the shared component selector more specific than surface button rules. Write such an exclusion inside `:where()` (`.menu_button:where(:not(.sb-status-page__action))`): each `:not()` counts as a class, and `554574c70` raised the World Info and Persona editor base button rules from (1,2,0) to (1,4,0), so they beat the later `display: none` summary rule and the `(pointer: coarse)` 44px rules (fixed in `public/css/sillybunny-world-info-editor.css` and `sillybunny-persona-editor.css`). The same commit raised the mobile (≤768px) and compact-mode `.sb-shell-panel-scroller` button rules in `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`, so shell icon buttons and button rows dropped from 44px to 38px, accent swatches grew from 24px to 38px, and compact full-width buttons fell to 32px on touch; fixed the same way (test: `tests/shell-panel-button-sizes.e2e.js`). Keep the extra specificity only where it is wanted, as on the focus-visible rules that keep the ring on `.sb-header-icon-button`. Tests: `tests/header-icon-button-parity.e2e.js`, `tests/in-chat-agents-list.e2e.js`, `tests/mobile-character-toggle-and-panels.e2e.js`, `tests/world-info-editor.e2e.js` (`:157`, `:631`), `tests/persona-editor.e2e.js` (text action buttons keep 44px).
- **Window filtering:** content-sized Windows can shrink when a live search removes rows. Keep Window surfaces fill-sized by default, opt only deliberately short Windows into content sizing, and lock a content-sized frame at its largest laid-out height. Tests: `tests/tag-manager.e2e.js`; `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js`; the Window and media dialog suites.
- **Short Windows and their pages:** a content-sized Popup Window with one row filled `90dvh` on desktop (`70801186d`). A floating Popup is `position: absolute` with `inset: 0`, so `height: auto` stretches it until `max-height` stops it. A sheet has `top: auto`, so phones did not show this.
  - **Guard:** use `height: fit-content`, as the shared `.sb-dialog--window` does.
  - **Pages:** a page used to copy the height of the surface it covers, so the New User form over the short phone Admin sheet showed 90px of its 444px. That height is now the page's minimum: the page grows with its content up to `--sb-page-max-height`, and a fill page is that tall. Pages have no `--sb-page-top`, because floating frames are centred and a page that fits still lands on the parent's rectangle. Pages bind no height lock, because its inline `min-height` would outrank that minimum and leave the page short after the viewport grows.
  - **Placeholder rows:** `.ds-entity-row` has `content-visibility: auto`, so its first layout uses `contain-intrinsic-size`. That made an Admin row 8px too tall for one frame, and the height lock kept the extra height. Rows in a content-sized Window turn the placeholder off.
  - **Row menus:** `positionPopover()` keeps a popover inside its `bounds`, with a 120px minimum. A row menu bounded by the short Admin Window had to scroll 118px of its items, so that menu passes no `bounds`.
  - **Tests:** `tests/window-content-dialogs.e2e.js` (`Short Windows and the pages pushed over them follow their content`, `A short Window leaves room for the action menu of its rows`); `tests/sillybunny-dialog-frame.e2e.js` (`over a short Popup Window`, `a content-sized Popup page keeps the rectangle of a fill Window when the viewport grows`).
- **Observed extension rows:** hiding crowded extension actions changes their measured width to zero. Repeatedly writing the same hidden styles from the row observer can then loop on its own mutations and block the main thread. Cache measured widths and write row classes and inline display only when the value changes. Tests: `tests/extensions-row-actions.e2e.js`; `tests/window-tools-dialogs.e2e.js`.
- **Selection mode in a header bar:** `createSelectionMode()` reuses a real dialog header through `headerSlots` and needs no `header` element; it hides only a standalone `header`, and it exposes `cancelButton`, `selectAllButton` and `selectionTitle` on its handle instead of writing them into the slots object. The generic `.menu_button:not(...)` fill outranks the raised header-bar rule in `sillybunny-theme.css`, and the flat `[data-selection-mode='true'] button.menu_button` rule used to match the header's Cancel and Select All whenever `root` contained the header (the Chat Archive passes its dialog surface, because the full-width action bar and Escape handling need that ancestor). The flat rules now name only `.sb-selection-action`, and the raised fill, hover, active, focus ring and 44px coarse height of the header buttons sit in `public/css/sillybunny-editor-shell.css` behind a selector chain as long as the generic one. `bindHeaderLayout()` adds the inline padding of `.sb-dialog__titles` to the title width, or a short centred title such as `3 selected` ellipsizes at 390px. Tests: `tests/sillybunny-chats-archive-layout.e2e.js` (computed raised fill, border and height of both header buttons; the selection title on a phone), `tests/unit/sillybunny-dialog.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-selection-mode.test.js`.
- **Tier surfaces:** a tier rule that sets only the surface background leaves `.sb-dialog__body` on the Window layer. `openAlert` surfaces painted the Window layer, and after the surface fix their body was still darker than their response bar. Set the tier layer on the surface and on `.sb-dialog__body` (`14199fd70`). Test: `tests/sillybunny-dialog-frame.e2e.js` (`an openAlert surface paints the same Alert layer as a Popup alert, not the Window layer`).
- **Overlapping pages:** during a push or pop two pages overlap, and a `z-index` inside the covered page paints over the page that slides. The list editor divider (`z-index: 4`) drew a bar across the Chat Archive's chat page until the slide ended. `.sb-dialog__page` sets `isolation: isolate` (`4584dec17`). Test: `tests/sillybunny-dialog-frame.e2e.js` (`while pages slide`).
- **Page chains:** Close on a page in a chain of Popup and `openDialog` frames closes one level at a time, through each level's own close path, and stops at the first level that refuses.
  - Await each level fully before the next.
  - When the walk starts, take a snapshot of the closes already in flight. Otherwise a veto handler that resolves the same promise runs `onClosing` twice.
  - A page dims only when nothing beneath it dims already, so each chain shows one dim.
  - `4ef8c438b`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/page-chain.js`. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-page-chain.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-dialog-frame.e2e.js` (`page chains`, `one dim is painted over the chain …`).
- **Generated hover mirror:** `public/scripts/dynamic-styles.js` copies each `:hover` rule onto `:focus-visible`, unless a focus rule with the same base selector already exists.
  - **Breaks:** the `.menu_button` hover rule sets `box-shadow: none`, so its copy erased the inset focus ring on every enabled `.menu_button`. Keyboard focus showed no ring on dialog responses, extension actions and header icon buttons.
  - **Guard:** give such a hover rule a same-base `:focus-visible` counterpart inside the same `@media (hover: hover)` block (`public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`, the `.menu_button` hover block). A `.menu_button` rule that draws its own outline then sets `box-shadow: none`, or it shows two rings.
  - **Tests:** `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js` (every Tab stop shows one focus indicator; the Editor's Presets button shows one ring), `tests/sillybunny-chats-archive.e2e.js` (the drawer button shows one ring).
- **Focus under forced colours:** forced colours remove `box-shadow`, so a ring drawn only as a shadow disappears.
  - **Guard:** the theme's forced-colours block draws a `Highlight` outline on every `:focus-visible` element.
  - **Pitfall:** a rule with `outline: none !important` defeats that outline. Use `outline: 2px solid transparent !important` instead: forced colours repaint it, and it draws nothing in normal mode.
  - **Pitfall:** a selected control filled with `Highlight` needs a `HighlightText` focus outline, or the ring is invisible.
  - **Tests:** `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js` (forced colours).
- **Focus return:** after a dialog or side panel closes, the opener can be disabled, hidden or removed, and focus then fell to the document.
  - **Guard:** `returnFocusTo()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/page-chain.js`. It retries a disabled opener for a few frames, falls back to a recorded container or a fallback control, and never focuses behind another open modal.
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-return-focus.test.js`, `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js` (Escape returns focus).
  - **WebKit:** WebKit can refuse `focus()` on a visible opener for a frame after a dialog is removed. `returnFocusTo()` retries any opener that is connected, focusable, visible and not behind a modal, and falls back at once for an opener that can never take focus.
- **Header title clipping:** `bindHeaderLayout()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/dialog.js` compares integer widths with fractional capacities.
  - **Breaks:** the old one-pixel slack kept a title that overflowed by one pixel centred, so the browser clipped it with an ellipsis (seen in WebKit on "0 selected").
  - **Guard:** a clipped title switches to the loose layout, and a loose header stays loose until the title is a full pixel inside the centred capacity, so the layout cannot flip on every resize pass.
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-header-layout.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-chats-archive-layout.e2e.js`.
- **Motion end state on a direct cancel:** `play()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-motion.js` ran `onEnd` only on finish or through `motion.cancel()`.
  - **Breaks:** code that cancelled the animation directly, for example through `getAnimations()`, skipped `onEnd`. A pushed dialog page then never hid the page it covered, which stayed visible with its own Close.
  - **Guard:** `animation.oncancel` runs the cleanup and `onEnd` while the motion is still the active one, so no path runs them twice.
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-motion.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-dialog-frame.e2e.js` (the covered page is hidden and inert once the push settles, even when the slide is cancelled).
- **E2E during page slides:** a push or pop keeps both pages in the DOM for the slide, and each page has its own Close. A strict locator counts every match at once and fails rather than waiting.
  - **Guard:** for an `openDialog` host, get the Close through `visiblePageClose(host)` in `tests/helpers/dialog-pages.js`, which first waits for exactly one unhidden page. A Popup page is its own `dialog.popup` with no `.sb-dialog__page` wrapper, and its Back button keeps the close class, so match `.sb-dialog__close:not(.sb-dialog__back)` there.
  - **Pitfall:** looping animations outside the surface under test, such as the hidden `#connection_profile_spinner`, run for the whole session. Count only the surface's own animations, and read geometry only after its own animations finish.
  - **Tests:** `tests/tag-manager.e2e.js`, `tests/character-folders.e2e.js`, `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js`, `tests/side-panel-engine.e2e.js`, `tests/side-panel-adapters.e2e.js`.
  - **Pitfall:** a closing menu keeps its items in the DOM during its exit fade, so a strict `menuitem` locator can match two. A saved `reduced_motion: true` in the test data root hides these races, so clear it before a run.
- **Service workers bypass route stubs:** once the app's `/sw.js` controls a page, `page.route()` does not see the requests the service worker handles, so stubs are skipped and writes reach the real server.
  - **Breaks:** under WebKit, the Account settings test in `tests/misc-form-dialogs.e2e.js` posted `/api/users/reset-settings` to the test server, and every later boot showed the first-run welcome popup.
  - **Guard:** `serviceWorkers: 'block'` in `tests/playwright.webkit.config.js`. In `tests/playwright.config.js` (Chromium) service workers are not blocked by default; specs that stub writes opt in with `test.use({ serviceWorkers: 'block' })`.
  - **Tests:** `tests/playwright.webkit.config.js` runs the dialog specs with service workers blocked.
- **Wide glyphs in action-menu icon boxes:** the shared action-menu icon box is 14px wide (`public/css/sillybunny-message-actions.css`, `.sb-action-menu-item > :is(i, svg, img):first-child`), and `fa-fw` cannot widen it, so a 1.25em glyph such as `fa-comments` or `fa-masks-theater` spills about 2px on each side and nearly touches its label. Home's Assistant Menu scales those glyphs with `sbHomeMenuIconFit` (0.8em, `public/css/sillybunny-home.css`) instead of widening the shared box, which would move every menu label in the app. Test: `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` (the glyph-to-label gap equals the main menu's, popover and sheet).
- **Read-only text blocks size to their text:** a fixed `rows` count clipped the last line of About → Troubleshooting on phones, where the user agent wraps. `fitToText()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-about.js`) sizes the block from a collapsed measurement and re-fits when its width changes, capped at `min(32rem, 60dvh)` with scrolling beyond (`.sbDebugInfo`, `public/css/sillybunny-windows.css`). Test: `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` (no hidden overflow at 390 and 1440).
- **Home has a surface in every theme:** `#chat` paints the theme's chat tint at its own alpha, and 47 of the 56 bundled themes keep that alpha at 0.05 or less. So Home's tiles, cards and headings sat on the page tone or the wallpaper (the title measured 1.1:1 on a bright wallpaper), a line taken from the theme border (Everforest Dark: `rgba(167, 192, 128, 0.3)`) all but vanished, and a column painted as the page tone blended into the page beside it (user report for Everforest Dark and Black Orange: column edge 1.02:1 and 1.05:1). While Home is shown, `#sheld[data-sb-home='on'] > #chat` paints the Window layer source (the blur tint, forced opaque times `--sb-page-overlay-opacity`) with the column's own tint on top as a gradient layer: the page tone is the blur tint mixed 12% toward the text colour, so the column always steps away from the page, and the blur tint is a surface every theme designs. `--sb-home-line` (set on `#sheld[data-sb-home='on']`) mixes 25% of the text colour into the theme border (50% under `prefers-contrast: more`), as libadwaita derives `--border-color` from the foreground; at 769px and wider the column carries it as a 1px inset line, with a `CanvasText` outline under `forced-colors: active`, which drops box shadows (`public/css/sillybunny-home.css`). Never force a near-transparent tint opaque: its colour was never seen as a surface, and a light theme with a `rgba(23, 23, 23, 0)` tint (Azure's value) turned Home near-black. Test: `tests/home-contrast.e2e.js` (Everforest Dark over a bright wallpaper, the column edge in Everforest Dark and Black Orange, Surface Transparency, forced colours, a light theme with a dark transparent tint).
- **Pressed fills follow hover fills:** an `:active` rule declared before an equal-specificity `@media (hover: hover) { … :hover }` rule loses to it while the pointer is down, so a mouse press showed the hover fill on Home's tiles, footer buttons, menu buttons, Recent Chats rows and their buttons, and Show More. Declare `:active` after its hover rule. `:active` also matches the ancestors of a pressed element, so a container with buttons inside fills only when its own target is pressed (`.sbHomeCard:has(.sbHomeCardOpen:active)`, `.welcomeRecent .recentChat:has(.recentChatOpen:active)`; `public/css/sillybunny-home.css`). Test: `tests/home-press.e2e.js` (real `mouse.down`, and a row stays at its hover fill while its pin or menu button is pressed).
- **Pushed pages keep their heights during the slide:** in the shared page chain the entering page stays in flow and the previous page is positioned over the pages node (`public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`, the `.is-underlay` and `.is-leaving` pages), so a content-sized dialog took the new page's height in the first frame and re-centred (Welcome 495.7 → 363px), and the leaving page was cropped or stretched to that height. `swapPages()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/dialog.js` animates the pages node from its old height to its new one with the slide's spring, measures the new height in a microtask so content added after `push()` returns counts, and pins every page that is still showing at its own height until the last height motion ends (several swaps in one task share one motion; closing the dialog releases the pins). The pins are held Web Animations (`fill: 'both'`, `persist()`), not inline styles, so pinning and releasing write no inline style. Pages slide from the inline end, so a right-to-left document mirrors the slide. Test: `tests/welcome-transitions.e2e.js` (frames stepped by pausing `document.getAnimations()`: first and last frame heights, a second Next mid-slide, late content, cancel and close mid-slide, right-to-left).
- **A pushed page can settle after the slide has measured it:** About → Troubleshooting sizes its debug text through a `ResizeObserver` (`fitToText()`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-about.js`), which runs after the page chain's one-time measurement, so the slide ended 18px off and snapped. `watchIncomingPage()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/dialog.js` follows the incoming page's header and body until the slide ends and retargets the height on the same timeline, ignoring nodes that have left the DOM and capped at four retargets so a page that never settles cannot force layout every frame. Stepped, paused animations hid this; sample painted frames in real time (`window.__live` in `tests/welcome-transitions.e2e.js`). Two same-tone pages sliding over each other also read as a glitch until the Page Transition Shade (DESIGN.md § Elevation) dimmed the covered page and shaded the sliding edge. The shade is a static `box-shadow` on `.is-sliding` plus a `::before` dim layer whose opacity animates, in `--sb-dialog-backdrop`; `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/slide-shade.js` installs those rules once per document (an adopted stylesheet, or a `<style>` fallback) because the blocking stylesheet has no room.
- **`:has()` rules make DOM and inline-style writes restyle the whole document:** the stylesheets hold about 426 `:has()` rules, so a DOM insertion, an inline `style` write or a `hidden` change can restyle about 500 elements (10–12 ms at 1×, 45–50 ms at 4× CPU; class and data-attribute toggles cost nothing). That holds at phone widths, where a body-rooted `:has()` applies; at 1440 the same writes counted about 0 with the art fix reverted. Welcome's page slide stuttered because it read layout between such writes, 6–7 whole-document restyles per slide; `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/dialog.js` now reads before it writes, queues the focus microtask before the mount, and `bindHeaderLayout` writes `--sb-dialog-header-side` only when it changes and skips hidden pages (3 structural restyles per slide, 4 on a forward slide once the art is counted, see Rule). Guard: `tests/welcome-slide-restyles.e2e.js` has a MutationObserver test as the primary guard and a trace count as a smoke test (style recalculations covering at least 300 elements per slide, median at most 4; the old code gave 5). In test code, `finish()` on a cancelled animation revives it, so filter `getAnimations()` by `playState !== 'idle'` before finishing. Review `ses_e6a94aca7ffeSt1UxI0FIJt7sM`. The art panels keep to the same order: `<sb-art-panel>` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-layout.js`) chooses between a panel and a banner as it enters the document, because a panel that turned into a banner a frame later gave the slide the wrong height to move to; the window frame is measured from the page that holds the panel, because both pages are in the window during a slide; a page with no width, as a covered page has, keeps its mode; and every panel is measured before any is written. Tests: `tests/art-panels.e2e.js` ("a page that a slide brings in has its mode as soon as it is in the document, before anything is painted"), `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-layout.test.js`.
  - **Breaks:** the guard failed at 390×844 with 7, 6, 4, 4 per slide on `78138c6f6` and `d84385344` alike, from `ba562396f` (Welcome art motion on; found by `git bisect`). The art player inserted its layers (`install`) and wrote inline `visibility` and `display` (`show`, `showStill`) while the slide ran, and `applyMode` in `art-layout.js` wrote `data-sb-art-mode` and inline styles on every later layout pass.
  - **Rule:** art makes no DOM write while the pages of its dialog move. `dialog.js` puts `is-paging` on the surface for each page slide and height motion, and `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/page-motion.js` (`isPageMotionRunning`, `afterPageMotion`) reads it. The player loads and decodes during the slide and inserts and plays after it; a playing figure keeps its picture until the slide ends. The layout skips its passes during a slide and runs one after it, except the pass at mount, which is part of the mount batch. A figure in a `hidden` page leaves the still alone (`pause` there made a restyle of its own a frame after `hidePrevious`). No style sheet reads `data-sb-art-motion`, so that attribute can be written during a slide at no cost. The Home hero is in no dialog and plays as before. A forward slide with art costs 4 restyles in a trace, which is the limit: the insertion of the page, the mount writes after the art layout's first reads, the header variable, and `hidePrevious` together with the art's insertion (16 of 48 forward slides in 12 instrumented runs counted 5 or more; backward slides counted 2 to 3). A playing figure holds its current frame during a slide, because switching to frame 0 would be a write of its own. A figure that is no longer in the document when the slide ends gets no layers, and the wait for the end of the motion retries on a timer if it is woken while the class is still set, so it cannot spin in microtasks.
  - **Guard at 1440:** the only body-rooted `:has()` that costs a Welcome write sits in the `max-width: 768px` block of `public/css/sillybunny-mobile-shell.css` (`tests/css-has-scope.test.js` allows two more, upstream's `body:has(.drawer-content…) #top-settings-holder` in `public/style.css`; the rest were scoped by `83b5fd16b`), so at 1440 no write restyled the document and the guard counted 0 to 1 per slide even with the fix reverted. The test adds a copy of that rule (`BODY_HAS_PROBE`) and keeps the Home hero still (`keepHeroStill`, because its ticks write behind the dialog); with the fix reverted it counts 4 to 10 on a forward slide (`9,6,4,6,7,6,4,6` and `8,7,5,4,9,6,4,5` at 1440) and the test fails at 1440 and 390. It skips outside Chromium, which has no CDP trace.
  - **Direct test:** `tests/welcome-slide-restyles.e2e.js` ("a Welcome page slide makes no restyling write…") records every node added or removed, inline `style`, `hidden` and `data-sb-art-*` write that the dialog receives, with a `MutationObserver` from before each of the four slides, and allows only the header `--sb-dialog-header-side` write and the toasts; it expects `data-sb-art-motion="playing"` after each slide. Unit: `tests/unit/sillybunny-page-motion.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-player.test.js` ("while the page of the figure slides", "a figure in a hidden page"), `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-player-early-wake.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-layout-motion.test.js`.
  - **Open:** the first slide after Welcome opens adds one restyle (DisplayLock of `#user-settings-block`), and a pointer over the dialog can add a 1403-element one; the median absorbs both. `keepHeroStill` masks the ticks of the Home hero behind a Welcome slide; the hero is unchanged by decision.
- **A `:has()` rooted at `body`, `html` or `:root` restyles the document on every change inside it:** `body:has(dialog[open])` (in-chat agents) and `body:has(.sb-prompt-owned)` (prompt editor) put style recalculation in a 400-message chat at about 4–4.5 s per streaming round; scoped, it was 75–90 ms. In a 400-message repro at 4× CPU, appending a node, an inline style write or a `hidden` toggle took 245–320 ms before and 3–22 ms after. Replace such a rule with a sibling `~` selector from a `body`-level node or with a marker attribute the script sets. Guards: `tests/css-has-scope.test.js` (an allowlist of document-level `:has()` rules, three left on purpose) and `tests/has-scope.e2e.js`. For list builds the scope matters less (see the boxed-list entry); for streaming it is the main lever. Fix: #870 (`:has()` scoping).
- **A hidden element is still a description:** the accessible name and description computation includes a hidden element when `aria-labelledby` or `aria-describedby` points straight at it, so hiding a tile's voiced line in Compact left it in the tile's description. Home tiles are named by their title and described by the line and progress (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/tiles-view.js`), and `describeTiles()` rebuilds each `aria-describedby` after a display mode change (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/mount.js`). Test: `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` (accessible name and description in Full and Compact).
- **A page that loads its content shows it in place:** Help topic pages take their body from a template (`public/scripts/templates/help/<id>.html`). Built first and filled later, a topic page painted the `TODO(copy)` placeholder and then swapped in the template, which would flash once real text lands, and a slow load could land over a page the user had picked since. `loadViewBody()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-help/help-views.js`) loads the template before the page is built, and `createMoveRunner()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-help/move-runner.js`) drops a move that a later move or a closed window has overtaken. Tests: `tests/help-shortcuts.e2e.js` (the page enters the document once with its body; two quick clicks keep the second body; a failed template falls back to the marked placeholder), `tests/unit/sillybunny-help-move-runner.test.js`.
- **App-wide keys and other modals:** F1 (Help) and Ctrl+? or Cmd+? (Keyboard Shortcuts) open nothing while another modal is open, but still hold back the browser's default, so the key behaves the same everywhere (`hasOtherModalOpen()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-help/help-keys.js`). The modifier is Cmd on Apple platforms and Ctrl elsewhere, never Meta on Windows or Linux (Super) and never with Alt, because Windows reports AltGr as Ctrl+Alt and a layout that types `?` with AltGr would lose the key in every text box (DESIGN.md § Keyboard). Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-help-keys.test.js`, `tests/help-shortcuts.e2e.js`. A headless browser cannot show whether F1's own browser help or the macOS Help-menu search is suppressed; check those by hand.
- **One Escape, one question:** Chromium closes a modal `<dialog>` again after a `cancel` that was prevented, from the third Escape on, so one key press can deliver both `cancel` and `close`. The frame's `close` handler then asked `canClose` a second time, and Welcome went back two pages on one Escape. `openDialog()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/dialog.js`) records that the `cancel` handler answered this key press and skips the extra ask in `close`. Test: `tests/sillybunny-dialog-frame.e2e.js` ("each Escape asks the dialog once, also the third").
- **The deferred window stylesheet wins over Home's rules of equal weight:** `public/css/sillybunny-windows.css` is loaded by script with the first window (`public/scripts/sillybunny-window-styles.js`), after `sillybunny-home.css`, so an unscoped 64px size for a Bundled Extras row's art (`.sbHomeArt[data-sb-art-placeholder='row-nahida']`, since removed with that row) shrank a Home card's art from 128px to 64px once any window had opened, while a sizing test that ran before any window passed. A rule in that sheet for art that Home also draws must be scoped to its window (for example `.sbBundledExtrasDialog …`). Test: `tests/home-assistants.e2e.js` ("keep the box of their art once a window has opened") opens and closes Bundled Extras, then checks that every Home assistant card's art box is unchanged.
- **Floating entry rows never show a placeholder at rest:** `createEntryRow({ floating: true, placeholder })` moves the hint into `data-sb-floating-placeholder` and `aria-description` (`prepareFloatingPlaceholder`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/rows.js`) and sets the real placeholder to a space, and `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css` makes `::placeholder` transparent, raising the title only on focus or with a value. A row that must show a hint (Welcome's reopen name field, hint "User") sets `control.placeholder` after the row is built and takes the class `sbWelcomeNameHint`, which `public/css/sillybunny-windows.css` uses to raise the title and show the hint.
- **Forced colours: rows, flat buttons and trail links need an edge of their own or their list's:** in forced-colours mode a `.ds-row` is bounded by its boxed list's outline and the separators between rows, as libadwaita draws high contrast, and the accessibility probe accepts that (`isBoundedByList`, `tests/helpers/dialog-accessibility.js`); Home's flat buttons (Show Me, Skip, Learn More, Install) had no edge at all and now take a 1px `ButtonBorder` outline, and Help's breadcrumb links take an underline (`public/css/sillybunny-windows.css`).
- **Forced colours: a focus ring must not match what is behind it:** a focused, checked switch drew its `Highlight` ring on its own `Highlight` fill, so the ring vanished. Forced colours now draw a `CanvasText` ring 2px outside the track (`public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`), and the probe's `outlineOnBackdrop` fails a ring that matches its backdrop. Room in the blocking sheet came from merging seven `.checkbox_label > input[type="checkbox"]:X, input[type="checkbox"].sb-switch:X` pairs into `input[type="checkbox"]:is(.sb-switch, .checkbox_label > *):X` (same specificity).
- **Window widths that hold labels scale with the text size:** Help's sidebar was a fixed 220–360px, so at 200% text its section names broke inside a word; `help-window.js` multiplies both limits by `--fontScale` (`readTextScale`). The 600px collapse point stays unscaled, because scaling it moved the Help intro line off the art (`tests/home-windows.e2e.js` Intros).
- **Window rows wrap instead of breaking words:** the shared `.ds-row-text { overflow-wrap: anywhere }` (`public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`, a blocking sheet with no room) broke labels letter by letter on a phone at 200% text when keycaps, chips or buttons shared the line. Keyboard Shortcuts, Getting Started, Bundled Extras and Help opt out in `public/css/sillybunny-windows.css` with `flex-wrap` on the row and `min-inline-size: min-content; overflow-wrap: break-word` on the text, so the suffix drops below the label; Getting Started's nowrap note line needs `contain: inline-size` so it is not measured as a word. Tests: `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js` (200% text, no clipped labels).
- **Long boxed lists are built in growing chunks:** each row appended to a window's boxed list restyles and lays out the rows already in it, so the cost of an append grows with the list. Building all 3,000 rows of a large library in one insertion held the Choose Assistant window for about 0.35 s before its first paint, and fixed 60-row chunks took several seconds to finish. `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/assistant-picker.js` builds the first 40 rows, and the rows down to just below the selected one, before the window opens, then appends one chunk per frame that grows with the list (60 to 500 rows), filters late rows against the current search, and stops when the window closes. Rows are only appended, never inserted above the focused one, so the first insertion still grows with the selected row's position (about 0.9 s at row 1,500 of 3,000). Deleting most `:has()` rules cut the total build time by only about 29% in review, so scoping selectors is not the main lever. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistant-picker.test.js` (`83ac928b8`).
- **A control that hides when the list fits one page is a one-way door:** `createListPagination` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/list-pagination.js`) hid its whole bar, page-size control included, while the list fitted one page, so choosing a size larger than the World Info book or the persona list hid the only control that could undo it. The bar now hides only while the list is no longer than the smallest page size; `keepSizeVisible` (Characters, Groups) still keeps it for any nonempty list. The bar hides through `.sb-list-pagination[hidden]` rules (`sillybunny-editor-shell.css`, `sillybunny-entity-list.css`), because the class sets `display: flex`. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-list-pagination.test.js`, `tests/world-info-editor.e2e.js`. Fix: #870 (page-size control). The persona list has since left the pager for Show More (see "Persona list, folders and header"); the World Info list still uses it.
- **Focus can scroll a clipped container:** `focus()` without `{ preventScroll: true }` scrolls an `overflow: hidden` ancestor to reveal the element, in Gecko and Blink, even when the element sits in a translated page; `preventScroll` is honoured, and an `overflow: clip` box cannot be scrolled at all. `dialog.js` focuses a pushed page's first field with `preventScroll`; its Tab wrap (`wrapTab`) does not.
- **Text halo in dialogs:** the upstream universal rule `* { text-shadow: 0 0 calc(var(--shadowWidth) * 1px) var(--SmartThemeShadowColor) }` (`public/style.css:198-204`) sets a shadow on every element, so `.sb-dialog { text-shadow: none }` reached only the dialog surface, never its text. In Playwright WebKit that halo made page slides slower on themes with a shadow width above zero (paired medians of 12 slides: Tag Manager 177 to 111 ms lost, a synthetic push chain 191 to 85, Welcome and About 6–11% better). `.sb-dialog * { text-shadow: none }` in `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css` fixes it; a deliberate shadow inside a dialog needs a selector above (0,1,0), as the tag-chip rules have. The Libadwaita themes ship `shadow_width: 0`, so they never showed the halo or its cost. Test: `tests/sillybunny-dialog-frame.e2e.js` (no text shadow in a Dialog, Window or Alert while the theme shadow width is above zero). Open: the same universal rule still gives the Home page, top bar, menus and toasts a halo.
- **Dialog shadow during page slides:** the blurred Dialog Shadow (`--sb-shadow-dialog`) on a content-sized dialog was repainted on every frame while the height motion resized the surface, and WebKit stalled at the start of each slide. A fill-sized Window does not resize, so it gains about nothing.
  - **Guard:** `.sb-dialog.is-paging { box-shadow: none }` lives in the adopted sheet of `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/slide-shade.js`, so the blocking CSS budget is untouched. `dialog.js` sets the class through holds (`holdPaging`), one per page slide and one per height motion, each released when its motion ends. A late height change that restarts the height motion after the slide therefore keeps the shadow off, a close in the middle of a motion holds it until `dispose`, and `dispose` clears every hold. Reduced motion never reaches it.
  - **Pitfall:** take the new hold before a step that can release the last one. `stopMotion()` followed by a layout read otherwise computes, and can paint, the surface with its shadow back (`retargetHeightMotion`, `popPage`).
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-slide-shade.test.js`; `tests/sillybunny-dialog-frame.e2e.js` (shadow off mid-slide; a late growth triggered from the height animation's progress, not a timer, because a delayed first frame shifts any wall-clock offset).
  - **Measured:** paired WebKit medians were noisy at host load 13 to 28; the gain showed on content-sized dialogs (Welcome and About at 1440), not on fill-sized ones.
- **Picture overlays (p9.3a, F-065/066/067/190/198):** the zoomed avatar, gallery, popouts, crop and image-zoom frames and the Visual Novel sprites.
  - **Breaks:** the zoomed avatar was capped at `90vh` and centred on the window by `mobile-styles.css`, so at 360×294 its Close circle sat under the fixed top bar (`elementFromPoint` returned `#sb-character-toggle`), and at 844×390 and 820×360 half of it did. The bar is not in the cap, so no window height is safe.
  - **Guard:** `--sb-zoomed-avatar-height` (window less `--sb-topbar-layout-offset`) caps the avatar, its container and its image in `style.css`; up to 1024px the avatar is centred in the area under the bar (`sillybunny-theme.css`).
