The width test is 1024, not 1000, because `check:design-rules` allows only the ladder's widths. Close is `--sb-density-floor` square, and `.zoomed_avatar:focus-within` shows it as hover does.
  - **Keyboard:** the two `dragClose` templates in `index.html` (`zoomed_avatar_template`, `generic_draggable_template`) are `role="button" tabindex="0"` with the existing "Close" string; a delegated keydown in `script.js` clicks them on Enter and Space (Escape was already handled by `RossAscends-mods.js`). The summary and group-members popouts and `.qr--close` got the same role, tabindex and name in p9.3c (below).
  - **Opaque windows:** `.draggable` is the Window layer (`--sb-layer-window`, no blur); the gallery's inline background was removed. An opaque ground made axe measure `#memory_restore small` (upstream `small { opacity: 0.7 }`) at under 4.5:1 in Libadwaita Light, which the translucent ground had hidden as incomplete; `memory/style.css` sets that label to full opacity. Expect the same when another label sits on a newly opaque ground.
  - **Own frames:** crop, transparent and image-zoom popups keep their frames above `OVERLAY_SHEET_QUERY`; under it `frameOpened()` in `popup-frame.js` gives them the Dialog (crop) or Window (the others) sheet through `resolveOwnFrameSheetTier()`. Such a popup is then `.sb-dialog`, so the registry's `OWN_POPUP` selector (`tests/helpers/surfaces/dialog-steps.js`) no longer has `:not(.sb-dialog)`, and a swipe that springs back must not reach the caller's click-to-close (`ignoreClickAfterSwipe()`).
  - **Sprites:** upstream's `visualNovelUpdateLayers` puts sprites at z-index 0 to 2, under `#sheld` (30); SillyBunny's `calc(var(--sb-z-sprites, 100) + layerIndex)` in `expressions/index.js` puts them over the chat and composer. `body.waifuMode #visual-novel-wrapper` (`expressions/style.css`) keeps them in the area above the chat (top at the bar, height `60dvh` less the bar), and `--sb-vn-area` does the same for the docked wrapper in `sillybunny-side-panel.css`, as a variable because the docked rule is `!important`.
  - **Tests:** `tests/picture-overlays.e2e.js` (avatar fit and Close at five window sizes, Close name and keys, crop and image-zoom sheets, wide windows keep their frames), `tests/audit/picture-overlay-fixtures.e2e.js` (opaque popouts in four themes, sprites clear of chat and composer), `tests/unit/sillybunny-popup-frame.test.js` (popups with their own frame).
- **Upstream windows and dialogs (p9.3c, F-180/182/183/185/189/191/192/193a/194/195):**
  - **A Dialog page over a Window took the Window's height:** `coverSurface` in `page-chain.js` sets `min-height` to the parent's height on every page, so the Data Bank scrapers and Add Secret were 792 by 810 pixels around one text box. `dialog.popup.sb-dialog--page.sb-dialog--dialog:not([data-covers-sheet])` in `sillybunny-theme.css` sets `min-height: 0`. A Window page and a page over a sheet keep the rectangle they cover. The width still follows the frame column, so a scraper page is 792 wide. Tests: `tests/window-dialogs.e2e.js` (a scraper Dialog over Data Bank, Add Secret), `tests/misc-form-dialogs.e2e.js` (`expectFrame`: a floating Dialog page may be shorter or taller than its parent), `tests/libadwaita-surface-layers-css.test.js`.
  - **Add Secret is a Dialog page of the key manager:** `secrets.js` no longer passes `tier: 'alert'`; Rename Secret stays an Alert. The heading moves to the header, so a test looks for `.sb-dialog__title`, not `.sb-dialog__heading`.
  - **New User is a confirm page:** `user.js` opens it as `POPUP_TYPE.CONFIRM` with Create and Cancel in the header. `onClosing` on an affirmative result calls `form.requestSubmit()` and returns false, so a refused or failed creation keeps the page open and the form's own submit handler still validates and posts. The form's submit container is hidden but stays as its default button, so Enter in a field still submits (`adaptNewUserForm`); the success callback clicks `.sb-dialog__back, .popup-button-cancel`. Tests: `tests/window-content-dialogs.e2e.js` (Create, Enter, a blocked write, an accepted write).
  - **Debug rows are the control:** `adaptDebugMenu` makes each action an activatable row with `chevron: false` and keeps the source `[data-debug-function]` node, hidden, in the same cell. The row calls its `click()`, so the delegated handler in `power-user.js` and the test interceptor are unchanged. Tests that clicked the action click `#debug_table .ds-row-debug` in the same `td`.
  - **Legacy `callPopup` Cancel sat at the top of its button:** `callPopup` set `display: inline-block` inline on Cancel, which made it a block box while OK is a flex box (`.menu_button`), so only OK centred its label. It now clears the inline display (a `SillyBunny:` marker in `script.js`). Under `OVERLAY_SHEET_QUERY` the popup is a bottom sheet in `sillybunny-theme.css` (responses `column-reverse`, so the affirmative action is on top and Cancel last), with a grab handle and swipe-down from `adoptLegacyPopup()` (`sheet-gestures.js`, called next to `adoptComposerMenus()`). The swipe clicks Cancel, or OK when Cancel is hidden, as the Escape handler does, and the offset is cleared when `#shadow_popup` hides. The red Yes button is unchanged. Tests: `tests/mobile-shell-smoke.e2e.js` (sheet, order, centred labels, swipe), `tests/unit/sillybunny-sheet-gestures.test.js`.
  - **A confirm that is one question has no heading:** `promoteQuestion()` in `popup-frame.js` makes text that is only one question (ends with `?`, 90 characters or fewer, no `. ` or `? ` inside) the `h2.sb-dialog__heading` and sets no `aria-describedby`. Markup beside the question, a second sentence or a longer text keeps the old body. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-popup-frame.test.js`.
  - **Warning text and secrets:** upstream's `--warning` is a literal red; the theme root sets `--warning: var(--sb-warning-fg)`. `.sb-dialog table.responsiveTable` has no 2px grid borders (the hidden API keys table). `secrets.css` `.secretKeyManagerItem.active` uses `--sb-flat-selected-bg`.
  - **Close controls (9.3a leftovers):** the summary, group members and Quick Reply popout Close are `role="button" tabindex="0"` with a translated "Close" name, and the delegated keydown in `script.js` takes `.dragClose`, `#groupMemberListPopoutClose` and `.qr--close`. `.draggable .panelControlBar` closes are `--sb-density-floor` square; the Quick Reply sheet's own `height: 15px` is now `var(--sb-density-floor, 15px)`. Tests: `tests/picture-overlays.e2e.js` (gallery size, summary popout name and keys); the group members and Quick Reply popouts have no e2e of their own, only the audit surfaces.
  - **Pitfall (jest):** importing `i18n.js` into an extension file (`ButtonUi.js`) pulled `@iconfu/svg-inject` into a node-environment test, so `tests/quick-reply-button-ui.test.js` mocks `i18n.js`.
  - **Pitfall (byte budget):** the blocking sheets had no room, so the change was paid for by deleting rules whose classes appear in no markup, script or test (`.sb-character-shell-mode-button`, `.sb-agent-state-*`, `.sb-agent-action-*`, `.sb-agent-card-title`, `#agent_apply_lore_review`, `.sb-message-style-button`). Prove a class dead with `git grep -F` over `public`, `tests` and `docs` first; a class that `dialog.js` builds as `sb-dialog--${tier}` looks dead to a plain scan and is not.
- **Destructive confirm fill and sibling labels:** `sillybunny-delete-confirm.js` marked the OK response only with `sb-popup-destructive`, which `sillybunny-message-actions.css` drew as an 18% tint of Destructive Red, so the agent Delete alert read as disabled next to the solid alerts. The OK response now also carries `is-destructive` and takes the shared solid fill (`sillybunny-theme.css`); the sheet keeps one rule for a custom `sb-popup-destructive` response (Delete Swipe). A SillyBunny confirm shows a verb, never `Yes` (Data Maid Delete, the extension sync Continue, Clear cache, Purge Vectors), and the start response of an explicit-action Dialog or Window is Cancel, not Discard (DESIGN.md, Dialogs: Headers). It is Cancel only when it abandons the operation: where closing goes ahead with the unedited input (the prompt inspector returns the original prompt and generation continues), the response names that outcome ("Discard changes"). `data-maid-dialog.js` had an `onOpening` handler that looked for `.sb-dialog__header-end`, which the Popup frame never builds; it was deleted, and Scan stays in the body banner. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-dialog-responses.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-destructive-confirm.test.js`, `tests/in-chat-agents-editor.e2e.js`.
- **Upstream's `.menu_button { width: min-content }` wraps a label one word per line** wherever a button sits in a flex bar that gives it no width of its own (`public/style.css`): the dialog's bottom bar ("Delete older" in two lines), the Bunny Debugger report's two actions ("Copy to / clipboard") and the mass-delete age chips (a one-digit chip as wide as its glyph). Give the button `width: auto` in that bar (`sillybunny-theme.css`, `sillybunny-tabs.css`, the debugger's `style.css`). Tests: `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js` (mass delete), `tests/debugger-report-actions.e2e.js`.
- **A sheet title cannot simply wrap to two lines:** a `-webkit-line-clamp: 2` on `.sb-dialog__title` in sheets (to avoid "Creating a Connection …") made `tests/tag-manager.e2e.js` fail (the title clipped by 2px) and `tests/sillybunny-chats-archive-layout.e2e.js` too (`.sb-dialog__pages` 406px in a 390px sheet). `bindHeaderLayout` (`sillybunny-editor/dialog.js`) reads the title's `scrollWidth` to decide whether to move it off-centre, and a wrapping title's `scrollWidth` is the room it was given, not the room it needs. A wrap needs that function to keep the single-line width of the title while it wraps. `tests/dialog-header-metrics.e2e.js` pins what is agreed today: Dialog, Window and Popup headers share height, padding, gap and alignment at 1440px and 390px.
- **An account form with one question and one field is an Alert:** `accountFormOptions` (`adapters/misc-dialogs.js`) returns `tier: 'alert'` when the form has exactly one text or password input and no checkbox, so Reset Settings is no longer a 640px Dialog. The form's own heading is the Alert's heading, and the caption stays above the field as body text because a floating label as long as the caption is cut in a 372px Alert. Two or more fields, or a switch, stay a Dialog. Tests: `tests/unit/misc-dialogs.test.js`, `tests/window-content-dialogs.e2e.js`, `tests/misc-form-dialogs.e2e.js`; the surface registry's `account-info` state `reset-settings` expects the Alert tier.
- **`.is-plain` takes a Popup OK response out of the suggested fill** and keeps the `popup-button-ok` class that extensions query (the icon picker's "No Icon", which clears a choice). Its ink and glow are reset in one rule, and the five enabled-state suggested rules exclude it. The disabled-state and outer-ring rules do not, because `tests/libadwaita-states-css.test.js` and `tests/focus-contrast-css.test.js` pin their selectors. The icon picker's title was blank because the adapter read `input.placeholder` after blanking it for the floating label.
- **A toast is a pill, and the status colour is the icon glyph and the title only:** `body #toast-container[class] > div` (the `[class]` beats toastr's `#toast-container.toast-top-center > div` width and margin) sets `border-radius: var(--sb-radius-pill)`, `fit-content` width up to 450px and the inline padding that keeps a wrapped message clear of the end caps. Toastr's white PNG icon is replaced by a Font Awesome glyph in `::before`, drawn in the status colour lifted to the dark tone's lightness with a relative colour (the toast is dark in both tones). The difference-blend rule that made the PNG grey is gone. Tests: `tests/toast-surface.e2e.js`, `tests/toast-sheet-css.test.js`, `tests/request-errors.e2e.js`.
- **Toastr skips hiding a toast that has focus inside but never starts its timer again:** a toast with a link or button that was focused stayed for good after the focus left. `public/script.js` calls toastr's own hover handlers on `focusout` and on the end of a touch press, and holds a touch press, so a toast stays while it is hovered, pressed or focused and goes five seconds after release (`timeOut` and `extendedTimeOut` are 5000, the fade 300ms). `tests/toast-surface.e2e.js` fails without the `focusout` handler. The queue is a separate entry (below).
- **A default Popup caption is translated by the template key it came from:** `popup.js` set `okButton.dataset.i18n` to "OK" and then replaced the text with the data holder's "Yes", "Save" or "Crop", so a later locale pass turned the button back into the locale's "OK" ("确定" instead of "是" in zh-cn). Each branch now sets the key (`popup-button-yes`, `-no`, `-save`, `-crop`, and `popup-button-cancel` for the default Cancel); a framed confirm's negative response is still renamed to Cancel by `replaceDefaultNegativeLabel`, which sets its own key. Test: `tests/unit/popup-default-captions.test.js`.
- **Toasts that time out wait in a queue, and the queue lives in `sillybunny-toast-motion.js`:** `DESIGN.md` Toasts, Timing: a second toast waits behind the first, a high-priority toast shows at once and pushes the first back, replacing takes 500ms (libadwaita's `REPLACE_DURATION`, `adw-toast-overlay.c`). `toastr.info/success/warning/error` are wrapped, so `toastr.*`, its options and its return value keep working: a toast that waits is made at once, so the caller gets its real element, but toastr is told `showMethod: sbToastHold` and `timeOut: 0`, which leaves it at `display: none` with no hide timer, and the queue shows it later with the options it kept. toastr keeps no handle to its timer, so the queue starts it by running the `mouseleave` handler with `extendedTimeOut` set to the toast's own `timeOut`, and stops a pushed back toast's timer with the `mouseenter` handler. Both handlers need toastr's options object, which only the `toastr.subscribe` listener receives: the module wraps `toastr.subscribe` when it loads (before `script.js` subscribes, after the awaited page load) and passes every event on to the app's listener. If the options never arrive, the first toast switches the queue off and toasts stack as before. Rules: a toast with `timeOut` 0 (progress and persistent toasts) is not queued, because it would hold every later toast for as long as it stays; a toast is high priority when it has `priority: 'high'` or its unescaped message holds a button (no timed toast in the code has one yet: the Draft Recovery and Cancel Agent toasts, the only toasts with buttons, never time out); the shown toast goes to the head of the queue, and its `onShown` does not run again; a toast that replaces one that is still leaving shows over 500ms, and the leaving one is taken out of the flow (`position: absolute` at its own place, inline) so that the new one does not move it; an error goes ahead of the waiting toasts that are not errors, more than six waiting drop the oldest info or success toast (never an error or a warning), an identical waiting toast is shown once, and an identical toast to the one on screen only restarts its timer (not with `preventDuplicates`, which toastr answers itself); `toastr.clear()` promotes the next toast after the current task, so a toast that waits is not shown for a moment and cleared in the same loop. `onShown` now runs with the toast element as `this`, as jQuery's `complete` did. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-queue.test.js` (real jQuery and toastr in jsdom), `tests/toast-queue.e2e.js`, `tests/sillybunny-toast-motion.test.js`. A spec that needs a toast to show must not leave another timed toast in front of it: the boot helper `quietChatForSmoke` (`tests/chat-scroll-regression-helpers.js`) clears the "No agent generation is currently running." toast that its own `cancelAgentGeneration()` raises, and `tests/sillybunny-chats-archive.e2e.js` ("when the host cannot load the chat") waits 12 seconds, because the page's own error toast is in front of the archive's.
- **A sheet keeps clear of a notch:** the sheet host pads its sides with `env(safe-area-inset-left, 0px)` and `env(safe-area-inset-right, 0px)`, the action sheet's side padding is `max(8px, env(...))`, and the two JavaScript clamps that kept a floating layer 8px from the edge read `--sb-safe-area-left` and `--sb-safe-area-right` (declared in `:root`), because `env()` cannot be read from script. Playwright cannot fake an inset, so `tests/toast-sheet-css.test.js` pins the rules.
- **The colour picker sheet follows its content:** it had `height: 100%` on the sheet and on the menu, which filled a phone however short the content was. Without the sheet's height the menu's `height: 100%` has no definite parent, so the inner scroller grew instead of scrolling when the window got short; the menu now stretches as a flex item. Tests: `tests/sillybunny-color-picker.e2e.js` ("keeps the focused field visible when the mobile viewport contracts").

### Pixel art and illustrations

- **Pixel art is drawn at a whole number of device pixels per art pixel:** a picture scaled by a fraction draws some art pixels one device pixel wider than their neighbours, which shows as stripes. `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/pixel-art.js` rounds the screen scale to a step of at least 1; a panel keeps that step, and a banner steps down by whole steps. Where no whole step fits, the art is shrunk by a fraction and must be drawn smooth: `data-sb-art-fit='smooth'` switches it to `image-rendering: auto`, because under `pixelated` the shrunk art kept uneven blocks. Tests: `tests/art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js`, `tests/art-panels.e2e.js` ("the pair is shrunk to the body and drawn with smooth sampling").
- **Playwright's `deviceScaleFactor` is not a screen scale:** it lays the page out in CSS pixels, so a check of even art pixels sees seams that a real screen does not have. `tests/art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js` launches Chromium with `--force-device-scale-factor` and a window of the size it wants, with `viewport: null`, at scales 1, 1.1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2 and 3; 1.1 and 1.75 are what browser zoom gives on 1x and 2x screens, and 1.1 is reported as the nearest float.
- **Read a window's real padding:** Compact density changes a window body's padding and row gap, so `art-layout.js` measures them and passes them to the CSS (`--sb-art-inset`, `--sb-art-inset-block`, `--sb-art-row-gap`) instead of assuming the default 32px and 16px. Test: `tests/art-panels.e2e.js` ("a body with the padding of Compact density").
- **The row metrics of an art dialog are load-bearing:** `.sb-dialog:has(.sbArtFigure)` in `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css` keeps the 44px rows, the 52px subtitle rows, the 16px inset and the 12px gap. Without it Welcome at a screen scale of 3 is 1437.438 device pixels tall instead of 1437, the centred figure sits at 886.281 instead of 886.5, and `art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js` (the layers of the figures of Welcome are even blocks while they move) fails with 41781 uneven device pixels. Snapping the figure onto a whole device pixel (`alignToDevicePixels` for panels outside a transformed ancestor) fails ten more evenness tests, because `LAYER_EDGE_BIAS_CSS_PX` is tuned for a figure that the browser places itself. A way to drop the exception has to change the layer bias with it, and be checked at every scale in Chromium and WebKit.
- **Widening a window can lag a frame:** Help's sidebar follows a new window width in a `requestAnimationFrame` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/list-shell.js`), so the first read after a widening measures too little gain. `planWidening` (`art-layout.js`) probes, corrects by the gain it measured, takes the widening back when the gain is under 5%, and stops after two rounds; the window never grows past `innerWidth`. Tests: `planWidening` in `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-layout.test.js`; Help at 1024x600 in `tests/art-panels.e2e.js`.
- **Size every splash picture by the tallest one:** sizing each picture by its own height made Tewi larger in her own splash than in the pair. `chooseFit()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-splash.js` takes the step from `TALLEST_ROWS`, the height of the tallest picture, and `public/css/loader.css` draws a fractional fit smooth (`.sb-boot-mascot[data-fit='smooth']`). Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash.test.js` ("is drawn at the step of the tallest picture: Tewi is not enlarged to fill the share").
- **A resolution query fires once:** the splash makes a new `(resolution: …dppx)` query after each change of screen scale, and a query made for a splash that has gone would keep the removed markup alive, so it stops once the preloader is removed. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash.test.js` ("does not ask for the next change once the preloader has gone").
- **A spec rule and the test that reads the spec change together:** `f4c3fa2c8` made pixel slots of `assets-src/branding/export-specs/shell.json` refuse `css` (`parsePixelAsset`, `scripts/lib/export-art.mjs`), while `test/export-shell.test.mjs` still required `css` on every slot and stayed red through three commits. The test now checks the pixel-slot rules: native size of the framed master against the app's art table (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-sources.js`), `approved/pixel/<id>.png` masters, and only the two portrait rows smooth. When a spec key changes meaning, run the package's tests that read that spec in the same commit. Tests outside the package read it too: pointing the splash slots at their loop masters (`approved/pixel/splash3-<variant>--<frame>.png`) passed `test/export-shell.test.mjs`, but `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash.test.js` still expected `approved/pixel/splash-<variant>.png`, because the splash code and the spec were changed in separate trees. It now expects the master of the frame each loop opens on, as the export test does. `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-sources.test.js` also reads `shell.json`, so run both unit files with the package tests.
- **The 16 and 32 px icons are hand work on top of a draft, and the fix is tied to that draft:** `assets-src/branding/scripts/icons.mjs` draws the 16 and 32 px icons as an area-coverage draft of `approved/logo-plate-nolines.svg` and applies `assets-src/branding/fixes/logo-16.json` and `logo-32.json` on top. The edits are absolute colours, so they only fit the draft they were made against. `applyHandFix` (`scripts/lib/icon-set.mjs`) refuses a fix whose listed pixels are not all changed, or that leaves a pixel inside the outer eighth that is not solid and a colour of the SVG; before it existed, a shifted drawing still built and would have shipped a mix of the new draft and the old fix. After the logo changes, redo the fix against the new draft. Tests: `assets-src/branding/test/icons.test.mjs` ("a pixel-fix that does not fit its draft stops the build", "a stale pixel-fix stops a write run").
- **Check which file a `srcset` picture shows, not only that it loaded:** the Home and About logos take the hand-fixed PNG at 1x and a larger file at higher screen scales (`public/scripts/templates/sillybunnyHome.html`, `createLogo()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-about.js`). Any entry loads a picture, so `naturalWidth > 0` passes with the wrong one. `expectRealLogo` (`tests/helpers/logo.js`) compares `currentSrc` with the file meant for `devicePixelRatio`, and the scale tests in `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` and `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` run it at scales 1, 2 and 3. With About's 2x entry pointed at the PNG, the scale 2 and 3 tests failed and the scale 1 test passed.
- **`@import`ed stylesheets are outside the budget gate:** `scripts/check-frontend-budgets.js` counts the `<link>` stylesheets of `public/index.html`, not the 18 sheets that `public/style.css` imports (about 157 KiB on 2026-11-04), which also block the first render. Moving rules into an imported sheet passes the gate without making the first render cheaper.
- **A class that code only queries is dead, and a literal grep misses a built one:** the dead-rule pass of the blocking sheets treats a class, id or `data-` attribute as dead when no runtime file creates or sets it. A name that only a selector string names does not count: `document.getElementById('sb-chatbar-layer')` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js` and `.matches('.sb-theme-slider-caption')` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-settings-presentation.js` query elements that nothing builds, and the shell's header and tab rail are no longer built (`sillybunny-tabs.js`, "the in-shell header and horizontal tab rail are no longer built"). A name can also be built from parts: `streaming-display-btn` is `` `${CSS_PREFIX}-btn` `` in `public/scripts/streaming-display.js`, and `sb-shell-root-left` is `` `sb-shell-root-${shellKey}` `` in `sillybunny-tabs.js`, so a rule is kept when a prefix or suffix of its name is built. Names that a library creates (`.select2-*`, `.ui-*`, `.toast-*`, `.tooltip-inner`, `data-popper-placement`) never show in the repository's scripts and stay. A rule that no coverage run reaches is not dead on that evidence alone: coverage cannot reach `:hover`, `:focus-visible`, forced colours or an error state.
- **Removing a dead id from a `:not()` changes the cascade (measured, task 10.4b):** `:not(#bg_selection_mode_button)` in the icon-only `.menu_button` rules of `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css` and `public/css/mobile-styles.css` names an id that nothing creates since the Backgrounds gallery rebuild, and it is still load-bearing: the id raises the specificity of every selector of the `.menu_button:not(...)` pair by one id, so those rules beat every one-id rule that sets `width`, `min-width` or `border-radius` on an icon-only button. Served without it (`:not(#bg_selection_mode_button)` removed from all six selectors), 41 elements at 1440x900 and 21 at 768x1024 and 390x844 computed differently on the page at boot (for example `#ui_preset_import_button` went from 38px to 26.9px wide, and `#model_openai_picker_toggle` from a 9px to a 50% radius). A dead id inside a `:not()` therefore stays until the rules it competes with are known; a dead alternative inside an `:is()` is different when the other alternatives keep the rule above every competitor (the `#bg_selection_mode_button:not(:has(...))` arm of the Backgrounds icon-button rule in `sillybunny-tabs.css` went, with 0 differences in 18 captured states, because the rule keeps three ids and no competing rule has more than two). Compare the computed style of every `.menu_button` and Backgrounds element with the sheet served both ways (`page.route` on the stylesheet) before an id leaves a selector.
- **Injected boot scripts are outside the budget gate too:** the splash loop player (`public/scripts/sillybunny-splash-loop.js`) is injected by `sillybunny-splash.js`, so `scripts/check-frontend-budgets.js`, which counts the static tags of `public/index.html`, never sees it, but it and its frames still download during boot. Injected as soon as the still had loaded, fixture loops delayed `sb-app-ready` by a median of 139 ms on desktop; injected when the browser is idle (`requestIdleCallback`, at most `LOOP_IDLE_LIMIT_MS`), the delay was within noise. Measure `sb-app-ready` with `scripts/measure-frontend-performance.js` before listing a loop. `/scripts/` is network-first in `public/sw.js`, so an edited loop script reaches users anyway; the key chain is still: edit the loop, bump `LOOP_SCRIPT_VERSION` in `sillybunny-splash.js`, then bump that script's `?v=` in `index.html`.
- **A splash loop starts on its still, and Home's motion counts leave the splash out:** each splash still is the first frame of its loop (`public/img/sillybunny/splash-*--anim.json`), so the loop takes over without a visible change. `tests/splash-loop-frames.e2e.js` compares frame 0 with the still as decoded pixels and as painted at scales 1, 1.5, 2 and 3; a different still fails both checks. The splash frames share `/img/sillybunny/` with Home's motion frames, so `tests/art-motion.e2e.js` and `tests/art-motion-frames.e2e.js`, which count the motion requests to that folder, pass `splash-` files through; before that, the splash's own requests failed both specs.
- **A playing splash loop hides the still:** `play()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-splash-loop.js` sets the still's `visibility` to `hidden` while the loop's layers show the picture, so a wait for `.sb-boot-mascot` to be visible times out when the loop starts before the wait's first check. `splashPicture()` in `tests/splash.e2e.js` waits for the loop's box (`.sb-boot-loop`) once it is in the page, and for the still before that. Tests that need the still itself turn the loop off with reduced motion, as the forced-colours describe in that spec and `tests/splash-loop-frames.e2e.js` do.
- **The hero panel's padding counts against the band:** the Home hero sits in an `.sbArtPanel`, so its whole step is chosen from the band width less the panel's padding, which `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-layout.js` reads from the panel's own style. With the day art 384 px wide, a fixed step at scale 1 needs a 466 px container (384 + 2×12 panel + 2×16 band + 2×12 layout + 2 border), which no phone reaches, so phones draw the hero at a smaller whole step (256 CSS px at 3x, 192 at 2x) and 1x screens below that width draw it smooth. Narrower panel and band padding on phones was tried and removed: no padding a panel can keep reaches 466. A test that reads a fitted picture's size must poll (`settledRead`, `tests/helpers/settled-read.js`), because the fit lands a frame or two after the render.
- **Layered motion keeps whole art pixels:** the art player (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-player.js`) lays its frames over the still at the art-pixel size the layout sets (`--sb-art-unit`), and places them again after they decode, because a resize during the decode changes the step. Under a smooth fit (`data-sb-art-fit='smooth'`) art pixels are not whole device pixels, so the player keeps the still and loads nothing, and the layout calls `refresh()` after every fit. When real frames are listed in `PIXEL_MOTION`, bump `ART_VERSION` (`art-sources.js`), because `public/sw.js` serves `/img/`, the scene JSON included, stale-while-revalidate by full URL. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-player.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-motion.test.js`, `tests/art-motion.e2e.js`. Each layer edge also gets a small bias, `LAYER_EDGE_BIAS_CSS_PX` (5/128 CSS px, `pixel-art.js`): a centred figure often stands on a half device pixel, and the engines cut lengths to 1/64 CSS px units, so without it some layer edges snap one device pixel off their neighbours. The working window is narrow (1/32 to 3/64 pass in Chromium and WebKit; 1/64 and 1/16 fail in WebKit), so re-run the layered `tests/art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js` cases in both engines when it changes. Snapping the figure's origin to a whole device pixel fixed Chromium but not every WebKit scale.
- **Larger text widens the art panels' content floor:** the content column keeps `MIN_CONTENT_WIDTH` (360px) times `--fontScale` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-layout.js`), so at 200% text on a 1024×600 window a one-figure window widens and keeps its panel, while About, with two figures, flips to a banner (`tests/art-panels.e2e.js`).
- **A layout that must follow the text size needs an `em` container condition:** inside an `@container` condition, `em` is the query container's font size (`welcome-home` is 15px times `--fontScale`), so a threshold in `em` moves with the text size and one in `px` does not. The Home tiles stack into one column below `calc(15em + 40px)`, a width no screen reaches at normal text, because at 200% text on a 390px phone three tiles in one row cut their titles and broke words mid-way. Test: the `home-page` surface in `tests/helpers/dialog-accessibility-surfaces.js` (no clipped labels at 200% text).
- **A slot that tests only measure as a box can ship as a placeholder:** the Reisen and Tewi Home cards showed grey boxes that spelled `row-reisen` and `row-tewi`, because `describeAssistant` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/assistant-cards.js` gave them `art.avatar: null`, so `createCardArt` never added a picture, and `createArtPlaceholder` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art.js`) wrote its asset id into the box as text. The tests for the portraits read the size and corners of `[data-sb-art-placeholder]` and one of them read `image-rendering` of the box itself, which is `auto` for any `div`, so a box with the right size and no picture passed. Each card now shows the thumbnail of its own library card (`getThumbnailUrl('avatar', avatar)`, as the third slot and the chat avatar do), a card with no file shows the empty tinted box, and the placeholder never writes its name (the name stays in `data-sb-art-placeholder` for styles and specs). Tests: `tests/home-assistants.e2e.js` ("show the thumbnail of their own card on every card", "show no art name as text", "shows a tinted box … with no picture and no text"); the `image-rendering` check in `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` reads the picture; `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-placeholder.test.js`.
- **Tewi's card picture is zoomed on Home so her head matches Reisen's:** her card draws her head about 0.8 of Reisen's, so the shared `object-fit: cover` crop showed her whole head and much of her body. `cardFraming` (`zoom: 1.22`, `origin: '50% 22%'`) on her entry in `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/assistant-registry.js` reaches the `img` as an inline `transform` and `transform-origin` through `describeAssistant` (`assistant-cards.js`) and `createCardArt` (`assistants-view.js`); the art box already clips (`overflow: hidden`). Reisen and the pointer card have no framing. The field is not called `portrait`, because `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistant-registry.test.js` reserves that name for a card to create. A zoomed picture's `getBoundingClientRect()` is larger than its box, so a test of the layout size reads `offsetWidth` (`tests/home-assistants.e2e.js`, "show the thumbnail of their own card…" failed on the rectangle until it did). Tests: `tests/home-assistants.e2e.js` ("zoom the picture of Tewi in on her face…"), `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistant-cards.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistants-view.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistant-registry.test.js`.

### Provider errors and connection feedback

- **Breaks:** a failed request shows only the HTTP reason phrase ("Bad Request", "Unauthorized"), so a bad key, a missing model and a provider outage look alike.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-request-errors.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-provider-message.js`, the failed-response sites in `public/script.js`, `public/scripts/openai.js`, `textgen-settings.js`, `kai-settings.js`, `nai-settings.js`, `horde.js` and `custom-request.js`, and on the server `src/util.js` and `src/endpoints/backends/chat-completions.js`.
- **Guards:** `showRequestError` shows the provider's own message, with a hint for 401/403, 404, 429 and 5xx. The server turns a provider 401 into 400 so the browser keeps its Basic auth, and sends the original status in `X-Upstream-Status`, which `getFailureStatus` reads.
- **Guards:** `extractProviderMessage` gives up only on a top-level body that looks like JSON and does not parse. A nested message that starts with `[` or `{`, such as `[400 Bad Request] API key not valid`, is the provider's own sentence; dropping it left the toast with the bare reason phrase.
- **Guards:** Send with no connection (`online_status` `no_connection`, or not known yet) shows the "Not connected to API!" notice, whose Show Me opens Connections, and leaves the draft in the box (`sendTextareaMessage` and the Send button handler in `public/script.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-connection-notices.js`). Before, Enter posted the user's message with no reply and no word why. Slash commands still run offline.
- **Keyboard presses are untrusted clicks:** upstream `public/scripts/keyboard.js` turns Enter on a `<div class="menu_button">` into `target.click()`, which has `isTrusted: false`. Telling a user's Connect from an automatic one by `isTrusted` left keyboard users without the missing-key feedback; `onConnectButtonClick` in `public/scripts/openai.js` checks for `e.originalEvent`, which jQuery's `.trigger('click')` does not set.
- **A toast's own `onHidden` replaces the global one:** toastr's per-toast `onHidden` overrides the global hook that calls `fixToastrForDialogs()` (`public/script.js`), so the connection notices call it themselves.
- **Invalid fields lost their focus colour:** surface focus rules that include an id, such as `#sb-settings-page … input:focus`, beat `[aria-invalid]:focus`, so a focused invalid field looked like any focused field. `:is(.text_pole, select)[aria-invalid='true']` sets `--sb-composer-focus-border` and `--sb-composer-focus-ring`, which those rules read (`public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`); no `!important`.
- **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-request-errors.test.js` (the message matrix), `tests/sillybunny-request-error-sites.test.js` (the Kobold, NovelAI, Horde and custom-request sites), `tests/request-errors.e2e.js`, `tests/sillybunny-connection-notices.test.js`, `tests/connection-notices.e2e.js` (Enter on a focused Connect button included).
- **Fixes:** #870 (request errors; Send and Connect feedback).
- **Offline at boot is not offline:** `online_status` is `no_connection` from the start of every boot until the saved connection's status check ends. `changeMainAPI()` sets it and starts the check, nothing awaits the check, and Home renders at `APP_READY` (`public/script.js` `getSettings()`, `public/scripts/welcome-screen.js`), so Home can render while the check is still in flight. A failed check leaves the status at `no_connection`, so `ONLINE_STATUS_CHANGED` never fires for it. A surface that shows something for "offline" therefore reads `data-sb-connection` on `<html>` (`connecting`, `offline` or `online`; absent until the first hook runs) and shows it with CSS only, so no panel adds a listener per mount. `public/scripts/sillybunny-connection-state.js` writes it from `setOnlineStatus` (on every call), `startStatusLoading`, `stopStatusLoading`, and `changeMainAPI`, which clears the state when the API changes and sets it for the Horde check (`public/script.js`). The Connect a Model button on the assistant cards (`.sbHomeCardConnect`) is the first user. Cancel (`.api_loading`) ends the state through `resultCheckStatus()`.
  - **A pending state can stick** where a check starts and nothing ends it, which hides the button and keeps the spinner on. A Chat Completion check that a newer check or a change of `#main_api` supersedes returns from `getStatusOpen` without `resultCheckStatus()` (`public/scripts/openai.js`), so it never ends its own state. The newer check ends it; a change of `#main_api` has no newer check, so `changeMainAPI()` clears the state itself right after it sets `no_connection`, and a check that is still in flight is already cancelled by then (a held request answered after the change leaves the state at `offline`; `tests/home-offline-link.e2e.js`). The Chat Completion status checks that run without `startStatusLoading()` (the OpenRouter, Chutes and Electron Hub model sort changes and the bypass switch in `openai.js`) set no pending state and, apart from the bypass and no-validate cases that set an online status at once, no status before the response, so the state stays as it was and only a failed check moves it, to `offline`.
  - **Known limit:** a Text Completion, Kobold or Novel check that a newer check supersedes catches its abort, sets `no_connection` and calls `resultCheckStatus()` (`getStatusTextgen` in `public/scripts/textgen-settings.js`, `getStatusKobold`, `getStatusNovel`), which clears the pending state while the newer check is still in flight. The state then reads `offline`, so the button can appear and vanish. It takes a change of type or URL, or a second Connect, during an open check; it never happens at boot. It is not fixed.
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-connection-state.test.js` (the rule, the attribute writes, no listener), `tests/home-offline-link.e2e.js` (a held status request: no frame shows the button while the check is open, it shows after a failed check, never when the check connects, and a user with no key sees it from the first frame that has it; the Horde start; Cancel; a check superseded by a change of `#main_api`; the 44 px touch target).

### Upstream compatibility and sync

- **Breaks:** the version map reports the wrong SillyTavern base, so extensions that need a newer SillyTavern fail.
- **Breaks:** a merged SillyTavern condition on a reasoning effort changes meaning, because the two projects map efforts differently. On GPT-6 Sol and Luna, SillyTavern sends Min as `'none'`. SillyBunny sends Min as `'minimal'` and None as `'none'`.
- **Files:** `docs/upstream-sync.md`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-version-map.js`, `public/scripts/openai.js`, `public/scripts/tool-calling.js`, `src/endpoints/backends/`.
- **Guards:** the version map clamps a SillyBunny version with no mapping to the highest synced SillyTavern version for its major.
- **Guards:** `isToolCallingSupported` opens Chat Completions tools on GPT-6 Sol and Luna for None, where SillyTavern/SillyTavern#6070 checks Min. Translate a SillyTavern effort condition when a merge brings one in.
- **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-version-map.test.js`, `tests/upstream-provider-parity.test.js`, `tests/reasoning-effort-none.test.js`, `tests/locale-validators.test.js`.
- **Third-party settings drawers lost their page when settings became a page list:** extensions that predate the Extensions panel append their `.inline-drawer` to `#user-settings-block-content`, and the settings split leaves that column unpainted (`buildShell`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`), so such a drawer measured 0×0 and showed on no settings page; the old four-tab `data-active-tab` filter it had relied on no longer existed, and `tests/topbar-extension-adoption.e2e.js:265` failed on the base `f6c1b217b` too. `public/scripts/sillybunny-settings-stray-drawers.js` moves (never clones) each direct-child drawer of that column into `#extensions_settings2`, now and through a `MutationObserver`, so the Extensions page lists it as a user-installed row; authored drawers deeper in `#UI-Customization` stay. A moved drawer keeps its open state like any drawer there, and global settings search finds its settings under Extensions. Tests: `tests/topbar-extension-adoption.e2e.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-settings-stray-drawers.test.js`.
- **Native controls and upstream keyboard handling:** upstream `public/scripts/keyboard.js` turns Enter on any `.interactable` into a synthetic `click()`, because upstream's `.menu_button` controls are `<div>` elements. A native `<button>` with that class clicks on Enter by itself, so one press ran its handler twice: Change Password opened two popups, and the Clear cookies alert opened and cancelled at once. A marked early return now skips native buttons, links, `summary` and button-like inputs. When you turn an upstream `<div>` control into a native element, check upstream handlers that synthesise its activation. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-keyboard-enter.test.js`, `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js`.
- **Fixes:** #410 (SillyBunny 1.6.4 still mapped to SillyTavern 1.16.4). The same bug came back on the next minor version; #497 fixed it with the clamp. #843 and #845 (provider parity with upstream).
- **Partial adoption:** `7b6db61d1` took SillyTavern's `src/electron/` package files (`Start.bat`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`) but not `index.js`, which `package.json` names as `main` for `electron .`. `Start.bat` also installs with `--ignore-scripts`, and the electron package downloads its binary only in its `postinstall` script, so the launcher could not start. npm audit still reported 30 advisories against its lockfile (electron 41.1.1 and extract-zip 2.0.1). SillyBunny removed `src/electron/`. SillyTavern still ships it, so a sync leaves `src/electron/**` out (`docs/upstream-touch-ledger.md`).
- **Open:** issue #713, where the Quick Image Gen manual sync fails in Docker (no `bash`, no real `node`). No fix PR found.

### Dependencies and lockfiles

- **Breaks:** `package-lock.json` and `bun.lock` drift apart, or Bun cannot read `bun.lock` and resolves again from the registry without the release-age gate.
- **Files:** `package-lock.json`, `bun.lock`, `tests/package-lock.json`, `.npmrc`, `Dockerfile`, `start.sh`, `Start.bat`, `Start-Node.bat`.
- **Guards:**
  - Both lockfiles ship. The Bun launchers install from `bun.lock` with `--frozen-lockfile` (`start.sh:250`, `Start.bat:141-145`), and so does the Docker image (`Dockerfile:24`). `Start-Node.bat` and CI's `npm ci` use `package-lock.json`. The launchers restore the tracked lockfiles after an install (`start.sh:306-313`, `Start.bat:168-174`).
  - When every change in the checkout is to `bun.lock`, `package-lock.json` or `package.json`, `getRepositoryStatus()` in `src/endpoints/server-admin.js` restores them with `git restore --staged --worktree`. `POST /api/server-admin/status` and `POST /api/server-admin/update` call it, so either request discards uncommitted dependency edits in the checkout that the server runs from. Commit those edits before you start a server there.
  - When a frozen Bun install fails, the launchers retry without `--frozen-lockfile` (`start.sh:275-288`, `Start.bat:162-165`). The retry resolves from the registry and does not use the committed `bun.lock`.
  - `npm audit` reads only `package-lock.json`. Check `bun.lock` with `bun audit`.
  - `min-release-age=7` in `.npmrc` applies to npm only. Bun 1.4.4 ignores it and chose an `electron-to-chromium` release from the day before. Bun reads `--minimum-release-age=<seconds>` or `minimumReleaseAge` in `bunfig.toml`. To keep the gate, update `package-lock.json` with npm, move `bun.lock` aside and run `bun pm migrate`. Without a `bun.lock`, `bun install` also migrates from `package-lock.json`.
  - Bun 1.4 writes `"lockfileVersion": 2` for a new or migrated lockfile (oven-sh/bun#31539). Bun 1.3.14, which `engines.bun` still allows, stops with `Unknown lockfile version`. Version 2 adds only parse-time checks, and Bun 1.4 keeps an existing version 1 lockfile at version 1 (oven-sh/bun#31602). After a migration, set the version back to 1 and check that frozen installs on Bun 1.4.4 and 1.3.14 leave `bun.lock` byte-identical.
  - After a change to `overrides`, Bun 1.4.4 fails a frozen install with `overrides in package.json changed since bun.lock was saved`, and the launcher retry then resolves without the gate. Regenerate both lockfiles after an `overrides` edit.
  - Keep `overrides` top-level. With a nested override such as `"vectra": { "uuid": "^11.1.1" }`, Bun 1.4.4 writes `"lockfileVersion": 3` instead of 2 (checked with `bun install --lockfile-only`), and the reset to version 1 above does not cover it. Upstream SillyTavern nests its `openai` and `whatwg-url` overrides; SillyBunny lists them at the top level.
  - `overrides` moves four packages past ranges whose releases npm audit flags and that have no fixed release: `basic-ftp` for get-uri 6 (through proxy-agent 6), `lodash-es` for chevrotain 11, `protobufjs` for onnx-proto 4 (through onnxruntime-web 1.14 in sillytavern-transformers) and `uuid` for vectra 0.2. Nothing else in the tree depends on them. Remove an override when its dependent accepts a fixed release; `npm ls <name>` shows the dependent.
  - The server never loads the installed `protobufjs`. The Node.js entry of onnxruntime-web, `dist/ort-web.node.js`, requires only Node.js built-ins and `onnxruntime-common`. Only `onnxruntime-web/lib/onnxjs/` requires `onnx-proto`, and nothing imports it.
  - The browser bundle never uses the installed `lodash-es`. `webpack.config.js` aliases `chevrotain` to its prebuilt `lib/chevrotain.mjs`, which carries its own copy of the lodash functions it uses. That copy has neither `_.template` (GHSA-r5fr-rjxr-66jc) nor `_.unset` and `_.omit` (GHSA-f23m-r3pf-42rh). Outside `tests/`, only `public/lib.js` imports chevrotain.
  - `npm audit fix` also moves packages within their ranges when that clears no advisory; on 2026-11-01 it moved zod and three ESLint packages. `npm update <names> --package-lock-only` changes only the named packages.
  - `tests/` has its own `package.json`, and npm reads only the `.npmrc` next to the nearest `package.json`. Without `tests/.npmrc`, `npm config get` in `tests/` reported `min-release-age=null`, `ignore-scripts=false` and `allow-git=all`. `tests/.npmrc` repeats the root `.npmrc`, and `tests/npm-settings.test.js` fails when the two files differ.
- **Fixes:** the v1.2.8 release commit `221de633c` replaced `"version": "1.2.7"` with `"1.2.8"` throughout `package-lock.json`, which also changed `@types/range-parser` and `is-callable`. Their `resolved` and `integrity` still pointed at 1.2.7 and no 1.2.8 exists, so `npm ci` installed 1.2.7, but `bun pm migrate` built 1.2.8 tarball URLs that returned 404. `scripts/bump-version.js` now edits only `version` and `packages[""].version`.
- **Limit:** a name@version comparison of the two lockfiles always lists the npm aliases `string-width-cjs`, `strip-ansi-cjs` and `wrap-ansi-cjs`, and `@jimp/plugin-blur`. npm satisfies the `@jimp/plugin-blur` peer of `@jimp/plugin-shadow@0.22.12` with a top-level 1.6.x copy, and Bun reuses 0.22.12.
- **Open:** npm audit flags file-type 16.5.4, which `@jimp/core` 0.22.12 uses through jimp 0.22 in sillytavern-transformers, for GHSA-5v7r-6r5c-r473. The first fixed release is file-type 21.3.1, which is ESM-only.

### Macro whitespace

- **Breaks:** a trim in the macro engine removes whitespace that a template asks for with `{{newline}}`, `{{space}}` or `{{noop}}` (SillyTavern/SillyTavern#5673 and SillyTavern/SillyTavern#5674).
- **Cause:** the engine trims text after it resolves the macros in it. `{{if}}` trims its resolved branch, scoped macros trim their content, and variable shorthand values (`{{.x = ...}}`) are trimmed. The legacy `{{trim}}` post-processor runs after `{{noop}}` is already an empty string, so `{{noop}}` does not stop it as it did in the legacy engine (`public/scripts/macros.js`).
- **Files:** `public/scripts/macros/engine/MacroCstWalker.js`, `public/scripts/macros/engine/MacroEngine.js`, `public/scripts/macros/definitions/core-macros.js`.
- **Guards:** `public/scripts/macros/sillybunny-whitespace-markers.js`. The walker wraps `{{newline}}` and `{{space}}` output in U+FDD0 and `{{noop}}` output in U+FDD1, and removes them after each trim. `MacroEngine.evaluate()` removes them before it returns, except for the `{{if}}` branch, which `resolve(text, { keepWhitespaceMarkers: true })` returns with markers for the trim in the `{{if}}` handler. Only the `core:` post-processors see the markers.
- **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-macro-whitespace.test.js` (runs the real engine in Jest), `tests/frontend/sillybunny-macro-whitespace.e2e.js`.
- **Limit:** `{{trim}}` still removes `{{newline}}` output next to it, as the legacy engine did. Put `{{noop}}` between them to keep the newline.
- **Limit:** prompt text that already contains U+FDD0 or U+FDD1 loses those characters.

### Client addresses behind proxies

- **Breaks:** the whitelist, a rate limit or `requireHttps` decides on a forwarded value that no trusted proxy set.
- **Cause:** a reverse proxy adds the address it received the request from at the right end of `X-Forwarded-For`, and it can pass the other forwarded headers on from the client unchanged. The server cannot tell which headers its proxy set.
- **Files:** `src/express-common.js`, `src/middleware/whitelist.js`, `src/middleware/requireHttps.js`, `src/middleware/basicAuth.js`, `src/middleware/hostWhitelist.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - The trusted hops are the valid entries of the `whitelist` setting (`getTrustedProxies()` in `src/express-common.js`). `whitelist.txt` and the Docker host addresses do not count. `getForwardedIps()` returns the address in each enabled forwarded header and reads `X-Forwarded-For` from the right: it skips trusted hops, and the first other entry is the client.
  - With `enableForwardedWhitelist` on, the whitelist blocks a request unless every address that `getForwardedIps()` returns is listed, and it names the first unlisted one.
  - `getIpAddress()` adds a forwarded address only when the socket address is a trusted hop and every enabled header names the same valid address. Otherwise it returns the socket address. The basic auth, account login, account recovery, password reset and conversation message limiters and the access log read their address through it.
  - `isIpInPatterns()` parses addresses strictly and treats a value that is not an IP address as unlisted, so a malformed forwarded value or a socket without an address gets 403, not 500. An IPv4-mapped value matches its IPv4 entry.
  - `requireHttps` lets plain HTTP through only from a loopback socket address without forwarding headers, whatever the `Host` header says. It reads the first `X-Forwarded-Proto` value, and only from a trusted hop.
  - Basic auth matches no request when the configured username or password is empty or is not a string, and it compares the configured user with `crypto.timingSafeEqual()` over SHA-256 digests.
  - `hostWhitelist.enabled` goes through the `'boolean'` converter, so `SILLYTAVERN_HOSTWHITELIST_ENABLED=false` turns the host check off.
- **Tests:** `tests/forwarded-client-address.test.js`, `tests/require-https-client-address.test.js`, `tests/basic-auth-configured-credentials.test.js`, `tests/host-whitelist-enabled-switch.test.js`, `tests/middleware-whitelist.test.js`, `tests/middleware-host-whitelist.test.js`, `tests/middleware-basic-auth.test.js`, `tests/middleware-require-https.test.js`.
- **Fixes:** `1b127a865` (whitelist), `06dc7a6e0` (rate-limit keys), `e059e5d23` (`requireHttps`), `0a836ed40` (basic auth credentials), `30ecce06d` (host whitelist switch).

### Slash commands and STscript

- **Breaks:** the STscript parser reads a script differently from how it is written. A closing mark, a pipe or an escaped character is skipped or taken twice, so a command ends up in another command's value, a value gains text such as `undefined` or `[Closure]`, or the parser throws a `TypeError` instead of a parser error.
- **Cause:** the parser moves one index through the text. Branches that return from a nested parse (a nested block comment, the quoted rest of a split-count command, an unclosed quote in lenient parsing) took one more character. Positions were counted back from an index that had already moved past whitespace or a closing quote. The split branches lost an escaped first character, because the parser took it before it started to split.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/slash-commands/SlashCommandParser.js`, `public/scripts/slash-commands/SlashCommandParserError.js`.
- **Guards:** every `SlashCommandParserError` carries the text and the index. The error handler of `executeSlashCommandsWithOptions()` reads its line, column and hint, and without them it throws a `TypeError` instead of showing the parser error.
- **Tests:** `tests/frontend/StscriptParserFixes.e2e.js` (one test or more for each fix, through `tests/frontend/stscript-parser-harness.js`).
- **Limit:** macro positions are still counted back from the end of a value, so a macro is indexed one character late for each escape that follows it in the value. With `REPLACE_GETVAR`, the macro index describes the replaced text, not the source.
- **Open:** `SlashCommandExecutionError.hint` slices its source as the parser error did, so an execution error on a line that another line follows shows the line without its last character (`/let x 1 | /let x 2` followed by a new line shows `/let x `).
- **Fixes:** `15b28ab1b` (nested block comments), `cebf47278` (`/let` with an empty `key=`), `627eb8cc8` (split argument error without text), `aa6f61ce1` (escaped first split value), `b420e832d` (`/parser-flag` without a name), `996ce24cf` (quote flags for closures), `f3e74ca1d` (quoted split-count rest), `87bd300e0` (closure in a joined rest), `cfc4fcf73` (macro positions in quoted values), `5e17bdc22` (unclosed quote in lenient parsing), `039e44a8e` (unknown command position), `643ad824a` (error hint line).

### World Info scanning

- **Breaks:** a lorebook entry activates when its keys and settings say it should not, stays off when they say it should activate, or activates and never reaches the prompt.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/world-info.js` (upstream), `public/scripts/world-info-scan-core.js`, `public/scripts/world-info-scan-chat.js`, `public/scripts/world-info-character-book.js`.
- **Tests:** `tests/world-info-scan-core.test.js`, `tests/world-info-scan-chat.test.js`, `tests/world-info-scan-target.test.js`, `tests/world-info-character-book.test.js`, `tests/world-info-activation.test.js` (the activation rules in `tests/util/world-info-cases.js`), `tests/world-info-harness.test.js` (the harness stops with the name of a declaration that `world-info.js` renames or removes, and its constants match the file), `tests/frontend/WorldInfoActivation.e2e.js`.
- **Limit:** the Jest suites run `checkWorldInfo()` and its helpers cut out of `world-info.js` by `tests/util/world-info-harness.js`, with one token per word and settings that each test sets. `tests/frontend/WorldInfoActivation.e2e.js` runs seven rows of the table through the real module, tokenizer and macro engine.
- **Fixes:** #393 (legacy string positions activated but never reached the prompt), `c11467f9b` and `612ea61d6` (scanning and serialization of lorebook entries).

### Row menus and reordering

- **Breaks:** lists built their own Move items: some showed them disabled at the ends, the group member menu kept them under a tag filter, where a move changes an order that is partly hidden, and only the prompt editor announced the new position.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/reorder-menu.js` and its adopters: `sillybunny-prompt-editor.js`, `extensions/in-chat-agents/ui/agent-list.js` (with `agentMovePosition()` in `agent-list-model.js`), `sillybunny-world-info-editor.js`, `group-chats.js` (`onGroupMemberMenuClick`), `sillybunny-tag-manager.js`, `sillybunny-folder-manager.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - `reorderMenuItems()` builds the move section; an item that cannot act at the row's position is left out, and the whole section while a search, a filter or a non-manual sort is active (the adopter passes `filtered`). The adopter keeps its own data change and save in `move(to)`; a `move` that returns `false` is not announced.
  - `announceReorder()` writes to a polite status region inside the topmost modal dialog, because a modal dialog makes the body inert and its announcements are skipped; keyboard and drag moves use it too.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-reorder-menu.test.js`; the adopters' unit tests and `tests/prompt-manager-characterization.e2e.js`, `tests/in-chat-agents-list.e2e.js`, `tests/group-editor-member-lists.e2e.js`, `tests/tag-manager.e2e.js`.
- **Upstream sortables:** `attachSortableKeys()` and `adoptUpstreamSortables()` in `reorder-menu.js` give the jQuery UI sortables of upstream code (regex, Quick Reply, logit bias, the sampler orders) Alt+Arrow, Alt+Home and Alt+End moves. `adoptUpstreamSortables()` wraps the sortable widget's `_create`, so it must run before the first list is created (`firstLoadInit` in `public/script.js`, after `batchSortableHandleClasses()`), and a list that destroys and creates its sortable on every display (logit bias) is attached again each time. The helper reads `items`, `handle`, `cancel`, `disabled`, `update` and `stop` at the key press and runs the list's own `stop` with `ui.item`, so the list saves as a drag would; the logit bias lists invert their saved order in that handler, so no case of their own is needed. It shares the module with the row menu helper because both load at startup and each module is a startup request (`tests/frontend-performance.e2e.js`).
- **One tab stop per sortable:** `attachSortableKeys()` gives the first focus target of a list `tabindex="0"` and every other target `-1`; the focused target becomes the stop (`focusin`), and Arrow Up and Down (no modifier) and Home and End move the focus inside the list, so a regex list with hundreds of scripts is one Tab stop. A tabindex that a row already had is never changed, and a plain arrow key from inside a row control is left alone because only a decorated target answers it. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-sortable-keys.test.js` ("one tab stop per list"), `tests/sortable-keys.e2e.js`.
- **Open:** the labels follow the Capitalisation Rule (`Move Up`, `Move Down`), and the locale files hold only `Move up` and `Move down`, so group, tag and World Info menus show the English labels in other languages (finding F-269).

### Search entries

- **Breaks:** a bare input inside a wrapper that draws the field takes the focus border and ring that every text input gets from upstream and from the shell roots in `sillybunny-theme.css` (`:is(.sb-shell-root, …) … :focus:not([disabled]):not([readonly])`, specificity 0,5,1), so a second ring appears inside the wrapper's.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/search-entry.js`; the `.sb-search-entry` rules in `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`.
- **Guards:**
  - The wrapper draws the field and its focus (`:focus-within`); the inner input's rule (`.sb-search-entry > input.sb-search-entry__input[type='search']`) outranks the shell focus rules and sets no border or shadow.
  - The entry has a height, not a minimum, because `createListSearch` animates the height when it reveals a list's search bar; `list-search.js` measures the field when the height is a token.
  - The clear button shows through `:has(> input:placeholder-shown)`, so the component gives an input without a placeholder a single space as one; a value cleared by a script hides the button too.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-search-entry.test.js`; `tests/foundation/shared-ui.e2e.js` (search entries).

### Extension settings: adapters for upstream rows and radios

- **Regex script rows are shared entity rows:** `adoptRegexScriptRow()` and `adoptRegexScriptList()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/adapters/utility-dialogs.js` run in `loadRegexScripts()` after the template's handlers are bound. They move the template's nodes (grip, name, selection checkbox) into a `.ds-entity-row`, add one switch (`input.sb-switch.regex-script-switch`, on when the script is enabled) and one menu button, and keep every upstream control, hidden, in the row: the switch writes `.disable_regex` and sends its `input` event, and the menu items click the template's own `.edit_existing_regex`, `.move_to_*`, `.export_regex` and `.delete_regex` buttons. A click that these buttons send bubbles to the row, so the row ignores clicks that start inside `[hidden]` (without that guard a test's `dispatchEvent('click')` on `.edit_existing_regex` recursed until the page froze). The row opens the editor on a click, and toggles the selection checkbox instead while bulk edit shows it. The settings normaliser skips the rows through `SB_EXT_AREAS` (`regex_container`, `.regex-script-label`), because it had marked each row a row wrapper and zeroed its metrics. Tests: `tests/unit/extension-row-adapters.test.js`, `tests/audit/surface-audit.e2e.js` (`SURFACE_ID=list.regex-scripts`).
- **Injection positions are select rows:** `adoptPositionRadios(root, name)` (same module) replaces the radios of Summarize (`memory_position`) and Vector Storage (`vectors_position`, `vectors_file_position_db`) with a select that the extension normaliser shows as a select row. The radios stay in the page, hidden, as the state the extension reads and writes. The select reads them once and follows their `input` and `change` events (jQuery's `.trigger` sends no native event, so the adapter listens through jQuery), so Vector Storage calls it after it has set the radios from its settings. The depth and role fields move out of the in-chat radio's label into labelled fields, which the normaliser shows as spin and select rows. These adapters live in a module that already loads at startup, because the startup resource limit in `tests/frontend-performance.e2e.js` (863) counts every request until the page settles, dynamic imports included.
- **A trailing `small` in a switch label is its subtitle:** in the extension pass (`subtitles: 'titled'`), `createRowSubtitle()` takes a trailing `small` inside the label's title span as the row's subtitle, so Quick Reply's "Inject user input automatically (if disabled, use {{input}} ...)" is a title and a subtitle. `#qr--set-settings` is no longer in the Quick Reply `left` area, so its four apply-at-once check boxes are switch rows.
- **Flex columns of rows stack:** an extension's `.flex-container` whose `.flex1` children each hold a row (Vector Storage's query and chunk rows) is a column in the settings page (`sillybunny-tabs.css`), because three rows side by side shrank each to a third of the width.
- **Text areas, sliders and field hints in the extension pass:** `normalizeExtensionSettings()` gives a labelled text area a text-area field (`createTextAreaField`, with the old title's buttons ahead of Expand and the extension's own `.editor_maximize` for that id hidden) and a range a slider row (`adoptExtensionSlider()`). The section pass can pull a bare range into a group and leave its `label for` behind, so a range finds its title by `for` even when it is no longer adjacent; a `title_restorable` class is dropped from the moved title (its bold tracked heading style), a trailing `small` ("0 = default") becomes the subtitle and a restore or auto-adjust button goes to the row's end. A `small` hint between a title and its field, or before the wrapper that holds the field and only its buttons (Regex Presets), becomes the row's caption. `readOnly` text areas and a select without any label are left. Provider forms of Text-to-speech are adopted like any other page now (the `entriesOnly` area is gone), so a provider's selects, switches and sliders are rows. Tests: `tests/unit/extension-rest-adapters.test.js` ("the extension settings pass"), `tests/unit/sillybunny-extension-settings.test.js`.
- **Summarize's loose controls:** `adoptSummarizeSettings()` (same adapter module) builds the summary box as a text-area field with Restore Previous in its label row, the three prompt builders as a toggle row over the same radios (the caption under the title is the chosen builder's tooltip, kept current through jQuery `input` and `change`), and the injection position. `adoptPositionRadios()` keeps an option's explanation (an info icon's title, or its label's tooltip) as `data-sb-description`, which the select row shows under its title while that option is chosen and under the option in the picker (`mapItem`). The Prompt builder radios are a toggle row because there are three of them (DESIGN.md Selection Controls).
- **Option text in brackets is shown without them:** `optionLabel()` in `select-row.js` drops the brackets of an option whose whole text is in them (`[ None ]`, `[ No fallback ]`, `[Currently selected]`) in the row and the picker, and nowhere else. A harness that picks an option by its native text has to apply the same rule (`pickFromPicker()` in `tests/helpers/settings-inventory-steps.js`); without it Expressions' selects "did not restore" in the settings inventory.
- **Quick Reply rows and the regex editor:** `adoptQuickReplyRow()` makes each `.qr--set-item` an entity row with a grip, the label and message fields in the text column and one menu button; the icon, options, move, copy, cut, export and remove buttons stay hidden in the row and the menu items click them (a menu click has no Shift, so removing always asks). `QuickReplySet.renderSettings()` sets `role="list"` on the list, because axe requires the parent of a `listitem`. `adaptRegexEditor()` (`extensions/regex/sillybunny-editor.js`) moves the editor's fields into boxed groups; it must not destroy its rows when the editor closes, because `onRegexEditorOpenClick()` reads the fields after the popup resolves (destroying them returned `undefined` for every value, and `tests/window-dialogs.e2e.js` caught it). It loads by dynamic import when an editor opens, so it is not a startup module.

### Settings sidebar search

- **Breaks:** the old filter matched a page by substring or initials and kept a page only when a heading matched, so `Frequency` found nothing inside a card and `cl` found Console Logs; and a page that was kept showed every row.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-settings-search.js` (matcher, row filter, marks, `createSidebarSearch`); the adapter in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js` (`createSettingsSearchAdapter`, `getSettingsSearchRoots`, `filterSettingsSidebarTabs`); `.sb-settings-tab-badge`, `.sb-settings-row--filtered` and `::highlight(sb-settings-search-text)` in `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`.
- **Guards:**
  - The module loads on demand through `createDeferredModule`, and opening the search field preloads it, so it adds nothing to startup (`tests/frontend-performance.e2e.js`).
  - A typed word has to begin a word of the text and may stop anywhere, so `freq` finds Frequency Penalty and `quency`, `equency` and `temp` (in "attempt") do not; a word that starts with a symbol needs no boundary at its start. A typed `frequencypenalty` matches only a word that starts with it, never the Penalty in it. Text and query are folded alike by `foldSearchText` (lowercase, accents off, one code unit for one code unit so the highlight ranges stay valid; a decomposed accent typed alone is dropped by `normalizeSearchText`). Initials (`cl` for Console Logs) match nothing. Whole-word matching was tried first and dropped on the user's answer to the wave 3 sheet.
  - A row is one child of a top-level `.ds-pref-group` or `.sb-settings-category-body`. It matches when every token is a word of its own text or of its group's title, description or `data-sb-search-aliases`, which is how a group keeps the name it had before a regrouping. Put the alias on the group, never on the page tab.
  - Closed expander and sampler help stay in the row's text, and a match that is only in closed content opens it (`revealClosedMatches`). A row that matches but is not shown (another backend, another device) is not counted and not touched.
  - Rows are hidden by `.sb-settings-row--filtered` and `aria-hidden`, by three repeated classes under `#sb-settings-page` so that no `!important` is needed. `restoreFilteredRows()` runs before each analysis and on clear, so a clear is one synchronous pass. Only the open page is filtered; all pages are counted. Two `MutationObserver`s and a `change` listener on the backend pickers re-run the pass when rows come or go.
  - The Characters pages are only marked and counted; rows are hidden only inside `.sb-wi-settings` and `.sb-persona-settings`, and a lazily mounted panel is indexed once it exists.
  - The reveal waits until the page is shown, and a phone shows the page only after its tab is pressed, so the pulse and scroll run on activation.
  - The count is a bare number in `.sb-settings-tab-badge` (`aria-hidden`) beside a hidden `.sb-settings-tab-count-description` ("1 matching setting", "N matching settings", one `t` string for each), which the tab points at with `aria-describedby`. The tab's name stays its label, so voice control and name-based tests keep working. Both are removed with the count.
  - Neither search may show a blank or wrong state while the on-demand pages load for the first query (`prepareShellSearch`, about 400 ms). The universal search keeps its idle state or its last answer, sets `aria-busy` on the list and replaces the list in one step. The sidebar keeps its tab list and sets `aria-busy` on it, and shows a name hit at once but never the empty message until the pages have answered. Test: "never shows a blank list" and "never shows the empty message" in `tests/sillybunny-settings-search.e2e.js` (they sample every frame; the old code showed 5 to 6 blank frames in the universal list and 5 to 8 empty-message frames in the sidebar).
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-settings-search.test.js`; `tests/sillybunny-settings-search.e2e.js` (clear timing, highlight, reduced motion, aliases, both shells, 1440×900 and 390×844); the alias cases in `tests/unit/sillybunny-settings-content.test.js`; `tests/sillybunny-backend-settings.e2e.js` (page aliases).

### Select rows and the shared picker

- **Breaks:** a select row that replaces select2 loses what select2 showed or did: the details of a model, the placeholder of an empty select, the removal of a saved option that is no longer offered.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/select-row.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/picker.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/option-details.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-model-details.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-connections-form.js`.
- **Model details:** the subtitle of a model row (context size, price, capabilities, subscription badge, the Horde model page link) comes from a describer that the provider file registers per select id (`registerOptionDetails()`), applied through `createDetailsMapper()` and the `mapItem` option of `createSelectRow()`. Describers return text only, because the upstream select2 templates in `public/scripts/openai.js`, `public/scripts/textgen-models.js` and `public/scripts/horde.js` build HTML from catalogue fields (they now escape them; see "Security and credentials"). Details pass an allow-list in `option-details.js`: text, `span`, `small`, `i`, and `a` only for an absolute `http` or `https` address without credentials.
- **Placeholders:** select2 is not initialised at 768px and below, so an empty adopted select takes its words from `SELECT2_PLACEHOLDERS` in `sillybunny-connections-form.js` (`pickerNoneLabel()`). A new select2 placeholder needs an entry there; `tests/unit/sillybunny-connections-placeholders.test.js` reads the provider files and fails when an entry is missing or differs.
- **Disabled and selected:** in a picker with `multiple`, a disabled item that is selected can be removed, and a disabled item that is not selected cannot be added (`isItemDisabled()` in `picker.js`). Before that, a saved NanoGPT provider that the model no longer offers could not be deselected.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-option-details.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-model-details.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-picker.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-select-row.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-connections-placeholders.test.js`; `tests/nanogpt-controls.e2e.js`, `tests/horde-model-links.e2e.js`.
- **Row click and extension actions:** a click on the row background opens the picker, so the handler must skip every control the row holds. It once skipped only `button, a, input, select, textarea`. When the settings pass adopted the TTS provider forms (`9c5c96573`), SpeechT5's upload and delete actions (`div.menu_button`) became the suffix of the Speaker row; a click on delete opened its destructive alert and then the picker, which took the focus from Cancel. `OWN_CONTROL_SELECTOR` in `select-row.js` now also skips `[role="button"]`, `.menu_button` and `.right_menu_button`, and a handled (`defaultPrevented`) click. Any new row-background handler needs the same list, as `focusOnPress()` in `rows.js` has. Tests: the two cases in `tests/unit/extension-rest-adapters.test.js` (a select row, and the settings pass over SpeechT5's markup); `tests/native-dialog-replacements.e2e.js` ("SpeechT5 speaker deletion…").
- **Fixes:** `55244b488`.
- **Open:** the row dispatches only `change` when a value is picked (`writeValue()` in `select-row.js`), while a native select fires `input` first. `#openrouter_sort_models` listens for `input` (`public/scripts/openai.js`), so a choice made through its row is not saved. `tests/audit/settings-inventory.e2e.js` found it: the fixture records that control as saving nothing.

### Menu and popover surfaces

- **Breaks:** an exclusion written as `:not(.sb-action-menu h3)` on the theme's broad `h3` rule raised that rule's specificity, so it outranked the Connections page's own heading rules (9.4a). A popover that kept its own border, or drew its own ring, showed a doubled edge once `--sb-shadow-popover` gained a ring: 9.3b's type-ahead lists drew a `0 0 0 1px` ring in front of the shadow that already holds one.
- **Files:** `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css` (`--sb-popover-ring`, `--sb-shadow-popover`, `--sb-typeahead-shadow`, the `h3` rule); `public/scripts/sillybunny-action-menu.js`; `public/scripts/sillybunny-autocomplete-clamp.js`; `public/scripts/sillybunny-options-menu.js`; `public/scripts/swipe-picker.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - Exclusions inside a broad element rule go in `:where()`, which adds no specificity.
  - `--sb-shadow-popover` draws the popover edge as a `1px` ring (`--sb-popover-ring`, the border colour under `prefers-contrast: more`). A popover sets `border: 0` and uses that shadow; `--sb-typeahead-shadow` is only an alias of it.
- **The ring has no edge in forced colours (fixed):** forced colours remove box shadows, so a surface whose only edge is `--sb-shadow-popover` or `--sb-typeahead-shadow` had none once menus, sheets and popovers dropped their border (`dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js`, the assistant menu sheet at 390×844: `border none 0px`). One rule in the theme sheet's `@media (forced-colors: active)` block draws a `1px` `CanvasText` outline at `outline-offset: -1px` on every such surface, which adds no size and changes nothing in normal mode; the `:focus-visible` forced-colours rule is important and wins on a focused surface. A new surface that uses the ring needs its selector in that list. A surface that draws a real border (the Chats Archive mention menu) or is a drag copy of a row is left out. Dialog frames and drawers keep a border, so they have no gap. `tests/focus-contrast-css.test.js` fails for any flat rule that uses the ring and is not in the list.
- **Popovers that kept a border (fixed):** the persona and World Info property popovers and the Conversation popover set `border: 0`. The type-ahead rule names `.ui-autocomplete.ui-widget-content`, because jQuery UI's `.ui-widget.ui-widget-content` border (0,2,0) outranked a bare `.ui-autocomplete`. Still bordered under the ring, not named by any task: `.sb-search-results` and the model-id and inline-select picker menus in `sillybunny-tabs.css`, and `.sb-color-picker-menu`.
- **A picker is a sheet in the whole mobile layout:** `openDialog()` and `createDialogFrame()` take `sheetQuery` (default `OVERLAY_SHEET_QUERY`); the colour picker passes `MENU_SHEET_QUERY`. The sheet rules key on the host's `data-presentation`, so only the query moves. Tests: `tests/sillybunny-color-picker.e2e.js` ("opens as a bottom sheet at 768x800"), `tests/sillybunny-mobile-overlays.e2e.js`.
- **Suggestion lists stay inside the window:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-autocomplete-clamp.js` listens to `autocompleteopen`, shifts the list sideways and caps its height, and never flips it above the field. It loads when the first list opens (a one-time listener in `sillybunny-tabs.js`), because a startup module counts against the 863-resource budget of `tests/frontend-performance.e2e.js`. Test: `tests/autocomplete-clamp.e2e.js`.
